# Jevellan build and acceptance report

Last updated: 2026-10-06. On 2026-09-26 the user resumed the build on a second machine (dev-madrid): finish the outstanding brief work and overhaul the interface to match the project website. This report does not claim Gonçalo's acceptance.

## Current state

- Projects phase 8: finish (2026-10-05 to 2026-10-06, goncalos-macbook-pro): the Projects build is complete with blockers. Documentation: the new [Projects reference](../projects.md) (concepts, the coordinator, threads, placement, worktree and main isolation, mail and reservations, publication and pull requests, terminal takeover, limits, restart, devices, where state lives, the interface, the recorded deviations and the evidence), turns and safety profiles in [runtimes](../runtimes.md), question set p-v1 in [decisions](../decisions.md), the Projects hub namespaces and relay in [mesh](../mesh.md), the new package in [architecture](../architecture.md), and AGENTS.md's eleven-line architecture and vocabulary (decisions 546 and 548). `roadmap.json` keeps the seven Projects phases at `complete-with-blockers` and records every non-goal of the specification as a planned item (seven new entries, two extended; decision 547). The live journey PJ-live (`node scripts/spikes/live-journeys.mjs --journey PJ-live`: the coordinator starts a thread on a real Codex account and real Jev that adds a small file with a test in a disposable GitHub repository, the pull request opens, its checks are read and it is merged from the interface; Claude joins when its token is present) is written and **blocked**: its credential preflight found none of `JEVELLAN_TEST_JEV_KEY`, `JEVELLAN_TEST_CODEX_KEY`, `JEVELLAN_TEST_GITHUB_TOKEN` and `JEVELLAN_TEST_GITHUB_REPO` (ENV-JEV, ENV-CODEX and ENV-GITHUB; ENV-CLAUDE is optional), wrote the blocked receipt [PJ-live.json](PJ-live.json) and started nothing, so it is blocked, never passed, and has not run live (see [PJ-live](PJ-live.md); decisions 549 to 551). Before the review the phase fixed the Projects gaps that phase 7 had recorded: an attached thread now refuses Stop, Restart and Discard (decision 552), a main checkout claim that could not be settled is settled again at every sweep and the thread page says so (decision 553), and an attached thread holds its account for turns on its device (decision 554). It also fixed pre-existing interface and fixture defects: the owner's own stop reads `You stopped "{title}"` in the project chat (decision 555), the attached composer looks disabled (decision 556), no conversation text shows through the live bar (decision 557), the device pill fits the sidebar (decision 558), the browser fixture server runs in its own process group so Playwright's shutdown no longer kills its children (decision 559), and the heaviest skeleton journeys wait on measured budgets instead of fixed ones (decision 560). Each change came with a test that failed first, except the measured budgets (decision 560), which rest on measured durations and the earlier failures under load. Then came the crucial-issues review and its fixes (below), and the final verification: build, typecheck, lint, the secret scan and `git diff --check` pass; the complete backend run passed **1602 tests in 136 files with 4 skips (the live Jev tests) and 0 failures**; and the complete browser matrix passed **208 of 208 with four workers on its first run**, with PJ3, PJ4b, PJ5 and PJ7 at 44 of 44 in four layouts (see Latest verification). Evidence label: **simulated**, as in phases 1 to 7; the Projects definition of done audit lists what is live, simulated and not run. Not run: the automated vision checks on this machine (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 356 vision records say not run), live Jev, live Claude and Codex turns, live GitHub and a second physical device. The dev-madrid vision run and the deployment each have their own line in the Projects definition of done audit. Open items are in Next work. Decisions 546 to 578. After the dev-madrid vision review polish (decisions 580 to 591) the suites passed again on 2026-10-06: **1603 tests in 136 files with 4 skips and 0 failures**, and **208 of 208** in the browser matrix on its first run (see Latest verification). Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.
  - Crucial-issues review (2026-10-05, goncalos-macbook-pro): the one review AGENTS.md allows at the end read the whole Projects change in five read-only parts run side by side (turns and runtime workers; the thread lifecycle; the coordinator and owner questions; cross-device relay and hub state; API, interface and leak surfaces), merged into one deduplicated list and found **3 serious, 5 moderate and 9 minor** problems. Serious, all fixed: **TH-1**, a main thread's Stop, publication and turns wrote to whatever branch the owner had switched the claimed checkout to (reproduced: Stop committed the owner's untracked file on the owner's branch under the machine identity; fixed by decision 561, test `tests/project-main-checkout-guards.test.ts`); **S-1**, schema checks ran before redaction and redaction can lengthen a text past its maximum, so a synthesized report holding a token-like word made the thread page answer 400 on every read, never reached a local coordinator and stopped a member from reading the hub's relay page at all (reproduced; fixed by decision 563, tests `tests/project-redaction-bounds.test.ts`, `tests/project-report-redaction.test.ts`, `tests/project-envelopes.test.ts` and `tests/project-coordinator-loop.test.ts`); **R-T1**, Jevellan's Claude account homes kept the CLI's 30-day transcript retention, so a thread or coordinator session left alone for 30 days would lose its only transcript and every later turn would fail to resume it (traced from the bundled CLI's strings, not run live: no Claude test credential; fixed by decisions 564 and 565, tests `tests/projects-turn-runtimes.test.ts`, `tests/project-missing-session.test.ts`, `tests/project-coordinator-loop.test.ts` and the attach argument cases). Moderate, all fixed: TH-2, Stop during a main publication still pushed (decision 562); C-1, a restart after two failed coordinator turns stranded the owner's retry (decision 566); C-2, a lost coordinator status write left the hub saying `running` (decision 567); RL-1, an event that bounced back after two moves was dropped as already seen (decision 568); S-2, one unreadable relay record held a member's whole inbox (decision 569). Minor: TH-3 (decision 570), TH-4 (decision 571), TH-5 on the owner's side (decision 572), C-3 and C-4 (decision 573) and RL-2 (decision 574) fixed; TH-6, C-5 and C-6 recorded, not fixed (decision 575). Every fix has a regression test that failed on the pre-fix build (28 red, with the existing tests in those files green) and passes after it, except two pieces of defense in depth without a test of their own (the bounded Projects API replies and the chat notice for a dropped relay record, decisions 563 and 569); the bounded coordinator queue was also proven by a mutation. Because the first review found serious problems, AGENTS.md allowed a second review of the fixes. It confirmed TH-1 and S-1 fixed, and R-T1 fixed as far as can be shown without a Claude test credential (blocked live), and found **no serious problem**: N-2 (moderate), a stop refused while the owner had the checkout off main, retried once the owner came back to main, committed the owner's own work into the discard ref and reset main under them (reproduced; kept in the ref, nothing pushed; fixed by decision 576, three tests red first and two guards green before and after); N-1 (moderate), the index summary clip could cut an unredacted summary at a token-like word (reproduced at helper level only, the integration effect did not reproduce; fixed as defense in depth by decision 577, its unit test red first and its integration guard green before and after); and N-3 (minor), a session-replacement rerun of a messages turn that then rests keeps those messages nowhere (not fixed; decision 565). No third review is due.

- Projects phase 7: terminal takeover (2026-10-05, goncalos-macbook-pro): `jevellan thread attach {threadId}` hands a thread at rest to the owner's terminal on the thread's own device. The command reads the installation control file, as `jevellan doctor` does, and asks the local daemon at `POST /api/local/threads/:tid/attach`, which answers only on loopback with the control token. The daemon refuses, and the command prints its sentence and exits 1, a thread on another device (`This thread runs on {device}. Run the command there.`), a working thread, also one whose next turn is about to start (`The thread is working. Wait for the turn to end or stop it.`), an ended or already attached thread, a thread without a native session and runtimes other than Claude and Codex. Otherwise the thread becomes `attached` with its start time, and the answer carries the working directory, runtime, native session, model, effort and an environment of only the account home variables and the account's authentication variables. The command starts `claude --resume {session} --model {model}`, with `--effort {effort}` when the installed `claude --help` lists it, or `codex resume {session} -m {model} -c model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"` in the thread's directory, with inherited stdio, the user's PATH, terminal and locale and no other variable; secrets never travel in arguments. SIGTERM and SIGHUP are forwarded, and Ctrl-C reaches the native CLI once, through the terminal. Whatever the exit, the command then calls detach: the daemon adopts the newest session in the account home whose directory is the thread's and that changed after the attach began (never one Jevellan itself runs), sets the thread `idle`, tells the coordinator `[owner worked on thread "{title}" in a terminal]` and starts the next turn from the messages that waited. The command exits with the native CLI's code (128 plus the signal number after a signal); when the detach cannot reach Jevellan it prints `The thread stays attached. Run jevellan thread detach {threadId} when Jevellan is running.` and exits 1, and `jevellan thread detach {threadId}` hands the thread back later. While a thread is attached the owner's messages wait, the coordinator's are refused, and a pull request merge or close is applied only after detach. The thread page shows `Take over in a terminal on {device}: jevellan thread attach {threadId}` under the composer of every thread that has not concluded, with a Copy command button that falls back to a selection copy where the Clipboard API is unavailable (as on the plain-HTTP Tailnet address) and, when the browser refuses both ways of copying, shows the whole command selected with the reason under it; an attached thread's row reads `Attached`. Evidence label: **simulated**. PJ7 passes as one integration test that runs the real installed command (`bin/jevellan.mjs`) as a process against a daemon with its control file, with simulated `claude` and `codex` CLIs first on a PATH of only the fixture folder and `/usr/bin:/bin`, so the owner's own CLIs are never found (brief section 13 asks for simulated provider CLIs on PATH). The daemon, its local routes, Git and the worktree, the thread runner, the native session files and the command's process, environment and signal handling (Ctrl-C included, in a real pseudo-terminal) are real; the provider CLIs and the thread turns (FakeRuntime) are simulated. No real Claude or Codex session was resumed. The owner's installed `claude` and `codex` ran only to print their help, with scratch home and configuration folders and never the owner's homes, to detect the flags: `claude` 2.1.289 at `~/.local/bin/claude`, first on this machine's PATH, lists `--effort <level>` (low, medium, high, xhigh, max), so attach passes `--effort`; an older `claude` 2.1.19 at `/opt/homebrew/bin/claude` lists no effort flag, so a PATH that finds it first gets none (the command probes at every attach); `codex-cli` 0.154.0 has `codex resume` with `-m` and `-c` (decision 540). The browser journey `PJ7 an attached thread disables its composer` runs in four layouts (desktop and phone, light and dark) against the `--projects` fixture server, whose control routes attach and detach a thread through the daemon's own attach and detach, without a terminal, so it proves the page's attached state and the copy paths, not the command. Not run: the automated vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 104 Projects vision records, 8 of them PJ7, say not run, ENV-CLAUDE), live Claude and Codex terminal sessions, live Jev (ENV-JEV) and PJ-live (phase 8). In their place, and not as a substitute, the builder ran a fifth visual review round over all 100 Projects screenshots of a fresh run (68 PJ3, 16 PJ4b, 12 PJ5, 4 PJ7) and, against a fixture server driven by hand, the states the spec does not capture (focus, hover and both copy outcomes on Copy command, an attached thread's row, the chat after a detach, the line under every other thread state, widths of 360, 375, 1000 and 1185 px), in eight layouts. It recorded 7 findings, none blocking: 2 major (a copy refused both ways reported in a success-toned toast that on phones covered the very command it said was selected, and a 28 px copy target) and 2 minor (two muted text sizes in one composer, and an attached row that looked idle) were fixed (decision 544); 1 minor was partly addressed (the command still ends in an ellipsis on phones, where Copy and a long press take all of it and a refused copy shows it whole); 2 minor were logged unchanged (the sticky phone composer is 44 px taller with the line, and `Command copied.` covers it for 8 s on phones, the app-wide toast position). It also caught its own regression before finishing (a wider copy target made short thread pages scroll by 1 px), which the PJ7 journey now asserts against. Every Projects screenshot in the repository (104, 8 of them new PJ7 ones) was written by the second phase 7 browser matrix run, and the tracked vision records it rewrote are identical to the committed ones. Neither four-worker browser matrix run of the phase was fully green: skeleton conversation journeys failed under machine load and passed alone (see Latest verification). See [PJ7 evidence](PJ7.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: Stop and Restart still act on an attached thread (brief 7.1 refuses only messages), so stopping an attached main thread settles its checkout under the owner's terminal; the session of a conversation working in the same checkout with the same account during the attach of a main thread could be adopted (the checkout claim makes that unlikely); attach does not hold the account's one-run-at-a-time guard, so a headless turn on the same Codex account can run beside the terminal; a launch failure (no executable, no folder, a start error) still sends the coordinator the owner-worked line; a signal during a hung attach request waits for its 45-second timeout; the attached session gets no COLORTERM, LC_* or SSH_AUTH_SOCK, like headless turns, so truecolor detection and agent-based SSH pushes are unavailable there; first-run prompts in account homes that never ran interactively, Codex rigging that names the bridge without a token during an interactive resume, and a live resume of a session the SDK created are unverified; standard input redirected from a file inside a terminal would get Ctrl-C twice; the pseudo-terminal Ctrl-C test runs on macOS only; the copy fallbacks are exercised in Chromium only (iOS Safari is not run); the line also shows while a turn runs, where the command answers that the thread is working; the attached row, focus and hover states and the widths other than 1440 and 390 px were checked by the builder's probes only. Decisions 534 to 545. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed. Update for Projects phase 8 (2026-10-06): an attached thread now refuses Stop, Restart and Discard (decision 552) and holds its account for turns on its device (decision 554), the Claude command also carries `--settings` (decision 564), and every Projects screenshot in the repository was rewritten by the single, fully green phase 8 browser matrix run.

- Projects phase 6: main isolation with mail and reservations (2026-10-05, goncalos-macbook-pro): a thread can now work directly on main in a device's project checkout when the project's Git policy is main. Project settings, New thread and the Override restart offer Main on such projects (a Leave git project keeps its text), and Jev's Call A asks the isolation whenever both are allowed. Placement puts a main thread only on a device whose checkout is on main by its latest heartbeat and is free: a device with a main thread that has not ended, a held checkout claim (a conversation or a main thread) or a main start in flight is excluded with `main checkout busy: {title}`, from claims and thread indexes the hub derives for every device, so a second main thread is never queued behind a busy checkout; a fixed busy device is refused with that reason. Preparation claims the checkout through the conversations' checkout ownership, so a conversation or another main thread on it is refused with `{project} on {device} is in use by "{title}".` until the thread ends, and brings the checkout to clean, current main (a checkout with foreign changes fails the thread with a sentence telling the owner to commit, stash or publish them); the thread prompt carries the brief's main parts. On a done report Jevellan commits leftovers, runs the test command (the same three attempts), and under the checkout's publication lease fetches, rebases onto `origin/main`, verifies again when the rebase brought upstream commits and pushes to main; a conflict aborts the rebase and gives the thread the brief's exact main-conflict prompt, and a busy lease is tried again three times 20 seconds apart before the thread rests with `Another publication to main is in progress. Send a message to try again.`. A publication ends `done` with the published commit, gives back the checkout and the reservations and tells the coordinator `main-published`. Stopping a main thread saves its unpublished commits at `refs/jevellan/discard/{threadId}/1`, returns the checkout to main and says so in the reason, whose ref the thread page shows whole on its own line. Main threads get the mail and reservation tools: `jevellan_reserve` and `jevellan_release` for advisory reservations (equal paths or directory prefixes, no globs, up to 120 minutes; a conflict names the holder's title, its overlapping paths and the expiry), `jevellan_mail_send` to another thread, to `all` or to the coordinator (whose mail arrives as a coordinator event), and `jevellan_mail_inbox` for unread mail, marked read once received; the coordinator can mail threads too. The hub keeps mail and reservations in the brief's `project-mail` and `project-reservations` namespaces with idempotent, owner-checked operations, and prunes both on write (the second half of decision 491). Evidence label: **simulated**. Both devices are daemons on this one machine (the hub and a simulated member over local HTTP), and both checkouts are clones of one bare origin behind a GitHub-shaped remote; no second physical device was used (`JEVELLAN_TEST_SECOND_DEVICE` is not set, DEVICE). PJ6 passes as one integration test: the daemons, their routes, the hub's SQLite store and relay, checkout ownership, publication leases, Git and the bare origin are real, while the member device, Jev's placement answers (a scripted transport) and the agents' turns (FakeRuntime turns the test drives step by step, running the bridge calls and Git commands an agent would) are simulated; main publication calls no GitHub API. Phase 6 adds no browser journey and no screenshot: brief section 13 defines PJ6 as an integration test, and the PJ3 and PJ4b journeys, simulated against the `--projects` fixture server, now assert Main enabled in Project settings, New thread and the Override restart of a main-policy project, Main still disabled with the Leave git text on the external project, and the Why panel's isolation bars (Worktree 0.80, Main 0.20). No spec screenshot shows a main thread. Not run: the automated vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 96 Projects vision records say not run, ENV-CLAUDE), live Jev (ENV-JEV), live Claude and Codex turns, a second physical device (DEVICE) and PJ-live (phase 8). In their place, and not as a substitute, the builder ran a fourth visual review round over all 96 Projects screenshots (68 PJ3, 16 PJ4b, 12 PJ5) and, against a fixture server driven by hand, the main-thread states the spec does not capture (a main thread that publishes and one that holds the checkout: their rows, chat cards, thread pages, Stop and Override restart; Main focused and chosen in Project settings and New thread; a refused second main start; every reason a main thread can carry, through a stubbed view), in four layouts. It recorded 8 findings, none blocking: 3 major (a restarted main thread with saved commits lost the link to its new thread; on phones the two-line reason clamp cut the saved ref, and cut the dirty-checkout instruction and long Git messages) and 1 minor (a thread's mail to the coordinator showed only its subject in the chat) were fixed (decision 532); 3 minor ones were logged unchanged (a published main thread's page shows `Done` without the commit, a refused second main start reads with the brief's `No device can run any enabled model:` prefix, and the Settings > Devices Native conversations card touches the card above it, a pre-existing style outside the Projects area), and one was an artifact of the stubbed view. Every Projects screenshot in the repository was written by the phase 6 browser matrix run (four workers, 204 of 204); the vision records it wrote are identical to the committed ones. See [PJ6 evidence](PJ6.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: a checkout claim that cannot be settled (the hub unreachable at release, a stop on a branch the agent switched to, an unconfirmed process exit) stays held and nothing retries it, so that checkout stays refused until it is released by hand (decision 528); when the coordinator runs on another device, a restart of a main thread onto main on the same device is refused (decision 529); a main thread reads `done` a moment before its claim and reservations are released, so a start or a conversation in that moment is still refused with its title; while the hub is unreachable placement sees only this device's main threads and starts in flight, and preparation's claim then refuses a busy checkout by failing that thread; the hub matches a member's claim by its stored path or by the project's thread and conversation indexes, never by resolving member paths; the agent's own `git fetch` and `git rebase` after the conflict prompt run with its account environment, proven only against the test's local origin; reservation paths are compared as given (no normalization of `./` or case); a thread created within seconds after mail to all can miss it when its device's clock runs ahead of the hub's; main-thread rows and pages are covered by unit tests and the builder's probes, not by spec screenshots, and the mail card by a unit test only. Decisions 517 to 533. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Projects phase 5: threads across devices (2026-10-05, goncalos-macbook-pro): a thread placed on another device now starts and runs there. Jev's Call B asks for the device whenever more than one qualifies; the start travels to the chosen device through a new hub relay (`project-envelopes`: a durable outbox under `<home>/projects/<id>/outbox/` on the sending device, retried after 10, 20, 40 and then every 60 seconds, delivered in order, and polled, deduplicated and acknowledged by the target), and that device creates the worktree and runs the turns with its own account. Thread events reach the coordinator's device the same way, in order and once each, also after a hub outage, and the coordinator's commands (messages, stops, dispatches of queued threads, answered questions) reach the thread's device. A thread's page and actions, and every coordinator request, are proxied from the device the owner uses to the device that holds the work; transcripts are read and pull requests polled on the thread's device. The brief's two background peer routes (`POST /api/mesh/projects/:id/threads` and `POST /api/mesh/projects/:id/coordinator/events`) do not exist, because the relay carries that traffic: a deliberate deviation (decision 488). Move coordinator here (in the project menu, the offline notice and the New thread dialog) moves the coordinator when its device is offline or its coordinator is idle: the new device starts a fresh session from the hub's notebook and indexes, the hub retargets events still waiting for the former device, and the former device stops any turn, forwards what it holds and drops its coordinator state at its next start, at its next turn's assignment check or when an event still reaches it, so it runs no further turn (except one launch already past its last check, listed under the known gaps). A thread whose waiting messages cannot start a turn while the hub is unreachable rests with `Waiting for the hub. This thread continues when it's back.` and continues once the hub answers (decision 505). Evidence label: **simulated**. Both devices are daemons on this one machine (the hub and a simulated member over local HTTP); no second physical device was used (`JEVELLAN_TEST_SECOND_DEVICE` is not set, DEVICE). PJ5 passes as one integration test on the project fixture with a simulated member, where a scripted Jev transport answers Call B with the member's device, and as two browser journeys in four layouts (desktop and phone, light and dark) against the `--projects` fixture server and its `Browser member`; the daemons, their HTTP routes, the hub's SQLite store, the relay, the owner proxy, Git and the browser are real, while Jev's answers, thread and coordinator turns (FakeRuntime), GitHub (the local fake server) and the hub outage (every hub request of the member fails while the hub keeps running) are simulated. In the browser the owner fixes the device, so Jev choosing the member and the former coordinator device running no turn are proven by the integration test only, and the thread page opened while its device is offline uses a stubbed 409. Not run: the automated vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 12 PJ5 vision records say not run, ENV-CLAUDE), live Jev (ENV-JEV), live turns, live GitHub, a second physical device (DEVICE) and PJ-live (phase 8). In their place, and not as a substitute, the builder ran a third visual review round over all 96 Projects screenshots (68 PJ3, 16 PJ4b, 12 PJ5) and states the spec does not capture (the project menu with Move coordinator here, a refused move on the page and in the New thread dialog, `Moving…`, keyboard focus on the notice's button, a member thread opened while its device is offline, a thread resting on the hub, widths of 1000 and 1185 px), in four layouts. It recorded 9 findings, none blocking: 2 major (a thread opened while its device is offline showed only a back link, an empty title and a red error; on two-column pages the offline notice sat flush on the columns) and 3 minor (the busy label reflowed the phone notice, and two banner margins) were fixed (decisions 508 and 509); 3 minor ones were logged unchanged (the moved notice is amber like every info notice, the phone placement line wraps inside the device name, and actions stay enabled while a shown thread's device goes offline) and one was fixture-only (the seeded concluded member thread has only an index row). The phase gate also found and fixed a pre-existing defect: a request whose client left before its body was read held the daemon's lifecycle gate forever, so a member could never enter maintenance and a fixture server's close failed; the fixture close failures of phases 3 and 4 likely had the same cause, though that is not proven (decision 510). Every Projects screenshot and vision record in the repository was written by the second phase 5 browser matrix run (four workers, 204 of 204). See [PJ5 evidence](PJ5.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: brief section 4 decision 7 is not built for an offline coordinator device, so a thread's needs-decision report waits until a coordinator runs instead of becoming an owner question directly (Move coordinator here is the way out); a hub outage before a task turn, a verification retry or a publication still ends that step as a failure that nothing resumes; a member's browser cannot act while the hub is unreachable (the existing sign-in rule); the New thread and Override device selects offer every available device, and one without the project path or an eligible account is refused after Apply, in the dialog; a move accepted between the coordinator's last assignment check and its launch lets that one turn run on the former device; Move does not check that this device can run the coordinator (it then reads `Unavailable` here and can be moved again); thread starts and commands for a device revoked later stay on the hub; the start window of a new remote thread's page assumes device clocks agree within two minutes; the tablet widths were checked by hand only; the fixture member polls the hub every 500 ms on the `--projects` servers. Decisions 488 to 511. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.
  - Phase 5 gaps closed (2026-10-05, goncalos-macbook-pro; evidence label: **simulated**). Four of the known gaps above no longer apply. Brief section 4 decision 7 is built for an offline coordinator device: while the coordinator's device is away, the device that owns a thread turns its needs-decision report into the owner's question itself, under the id the coordinator's own fallback uses, and the coordinator later reads that report as already asked (decision 512). New thread and Override list every device of the mesh and disable, with placement's reason, those no thread of the project can run on (for example `Offline fixture: offline`), instead of offering every available device and refusing after Apply (decision 513). Move coordinator here first checks that this device can run the coordinator, and the offline notice, the New thread dialog and the project menu show the move disabled with the reason (decision 514). The coordinator announces each turn on the hub before it launches it, and the hub applies the move rule in the move's own transaction, so a move accepted after the last assignment check no longer lets one turn run on the former device (decision 515). Verification: build, typecheck, lint, the working-tree secret scan and `git diff --check` pass; every Projects test file passes, 341 of 341 in 33 files (9 new cases, among them the new `tests/projects-offline-decisions.test.ts`), and `tests/member-application.test.ts -t project` 10 passed (its other cases filtered out); the Projects browser journeys passed 40 of 40 in four layouts in two runs, with the PJ3 New thread step now asserting the Device options; the refusal was only checked against a stubbed work view (decision 516). The full backend and the full browser matrix were not run again. New known gaps: the fallback needs the hub, so a coordinator on the hub itself while the hub is down has none; a question answered more than 14 days before the coordinator reads its report loses the asked-directly note; a coordinator device that returns at the very moment the owner device reads it as away can receive the report before the question exists and raise it again; a crash between a turn's announcement and its launch leaves the coordinator `running` on the hub until that device restarts or goes offline. The other known gaps above stand. PJ5.md and the roadmap entry still describe the fallback as missing (updated in the phase 6 bookkeeping, decision 533). Decisions 512 to 516. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Projects phase 4: Jev placement and overrides (2026-10-04, goncalos-macbook-pro): a thread start asks Jev the placement questions of question set p-v1 (BRIEF-projects.md section 10). Call A asks for the isolation, the model (the menu descriptions as criteria) and the effort, each left out when it is fixed or has one option, and Call B for the device; both carry a redacted `placement-state-v1` packet with the task, the coordinator's note, the project's active threads and the newest override sentences. The most probable answer wins, the effort is mapped to the model, the account is the best-ranked eligible one on the chosen device, and the record keeps the probabilities and the Jev call; every Jev failure except a cancelled start (no key, 401, timeout, an unusable answer, a packet too large) places with the deterministic fallback and records why. The thread page gains Why (the placement field by field: source, Jev's probabilities as bars, the mapped effort, excluded models and devices with their reasons, the account and the Jev call), the brief's `Placed without Jev: {reason}` chip and Override: From the next turn changes the model (same runtime) and the effort of the next launch, and Restart with these choices places a new thread with the chosen fields, stops the old one as `Restarted as {newId}.` and removes its worktree. Each override is recorded on the hub, summarized to the coordinator and given to later placements as a sentence. Evidence label: **simulated**. PJ4 and PJ4b pass as integration tests on a booted daemon whose Jev transport is a scripted fetch, and PJ4b also as a browser journey in four layouts (desktop and phone, light and dark) against the `--projects` fixture server, whose own fake Jev answers placements (decision 484); the daemon, its routes, the hub, Git and the browser are real, while the Jev answers, thread turns (FakeRuntime) and GitHub (the local fake server) are simulated. While placement stays on this device and main isolation is gated (decision 294), the daemon never sends Call B or the isolation question; pure tests cover both. Not run: the automated vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 16 PJ4b and 68 PJ3 vision records say not run), live Jev placement (`JEVELLAN_TEST_JEV_KEY` is not set, ENV-JEV), live turns, live GitHub and PJ-live (phase 8). In their place, and not as a substitute, the builder ran a second visual review round over all 84 Projects screenshots (68 PJ3, 16 PJ4b) and states the spec does not capture (Override in restart mode, a refused restart, the fallback thread's Why, long reasons and titles, keyboard focus, danger buttons in dark, widths of 1000 and 1185 px), in four layouts. It recorded 13 findings, none blocking: 4 major (a long thread title pushed `Why this placement` out of the panel heading, the sidebar called idle threads running, danger buttons put white text on the dark theme's salmon at about 2.6:1, and one capture in the evidence cut the page header) and 3 minor were fixed (decision 485); 6 minor ones were logged unchanged (among them the raw model and thread ids in the coordinator's override summary and the `Restarted as {newId}.` link text, which is the brief's). The Projects screenshots and every vision record in the repository were written by the second phase 4 browser matrix run (two workers, 196 of 196), after the review's last code change; that run also rewrote the PJ3 evidence, whose placements now record `jev`. See [PJ4 evidence](PJ4.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: Call B, the isolation question and the Leave git rule for it are reachable only once remote threads (phase 5) and main isolation (phase 6) exist; brief section 13 lists the restarted thread's worktree removal and the next packet's override sentences under the browser journey, but only the integration test proves them; an override that arrives between a turn's start and its launch applies from the turn after; a restart refused by a race after its checks (a pull request opened meanwhile) is not re-checked after the stop; the Why panel cannot tell a next-turn change of a fixed field (it still reads `fixed`); the tablet widths were checked by hand only; the only fully green browser matrix ran on two workers (decision 486); a fixture server again failed its close once and left a small temporary root (cause not identified). Decisions 473 to 487. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Projects phase 3: the interface (2026-10-04, goncalos-macbook-pro): the sidebar has a Projects section with one row per project (the coordinator's dot, the running count and the waiting badge). The project page (`/projects/:id`) holds the coordinator chat streamed over SSE (owner messages, Markdown replies, one line per tool call, event cards), a composer with Stop, the coordinator chip and session, the brief's unavailable and offline notices, Waiting for you with decision cards and the Answered list, Running and Concluded thread rows, Pull requests with the merge dialog, New thread, the Notebook panel with its revision conflict and Project settings; it shows two columns from 840 px of page width and the Chat, Waiting, Threads and Pull requests tabs below that, as on phones. The thread page (`/projects/:id/threads/:threadId`) shows the native transcript with report cards, messages waiting for the next turn, a composer with Interrupt current turn, Stop, Discard and Allow 10 more turns, the pull request chip and the placement line. Settings, Git has the GitHub token card. The phase gate also found two server problems, now fixed: the sidebar's list view read far more than its rows show, and read-only project hub requests waited on the lifecycle gate's synchronous writes (decisions 470 and 471). Evidence label: **simulated**. PJ3 ran as browser journeys in four layouts (desktop and phone, light and dark) against the `--projects` fixture server: the daemon, hub and member, their routes and SSE, Git and the browser are real, while coordinator and thread turns are FakeRuntime scripted turns and GitHub is the local fake server. Not run: the automated vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set, so all 68 PJ3 vision records say not run), live Claude and Codex turns, live GitHub and PJ-live (phase 8). In their place, and not as a substitute, the builder ran one visual review round: all 68 PJ3 screenshots of its own spec runs plus states the spec does not capture (every thread state, stopped and turn-limit thread pages, the offline project, keyboard focus, long names, widths from 1000 to 1280 px, the existing pages with the new section), in four layouts. It found 15 problems: 1 blocking (a long thread title made the phone page scroll sideways), 4 major (a raw report tool row above every report card, the live bar over chat text, the current project hidden in the capped sidebar list, hover leftovers in the evidence) and 3 minor were fixed (decisions 468 and 469), 6 minor ones were logged unchanged (among them dark inline code on panels and 32 px phone controls, both app-wide styles) and one was a capture artifact. The PJ3 screenshots in the repository come from the second browser matrix run, after two server-only fixes that do not change what the pages show. PJ3 passed 28 of 28 (7 journeys in four layouts) in both matrix runs; see [PJ3 evidence](PJ3.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: Why, Override and the `Placed without Jev` chip arrive with Jev placement (phase 4); the seeded remote thread row, remote thread pages and Move coordinator here with phase 5; the take-over line with phase 7; the tablet widths (761 to 1180 px) were checked by hand, not by the spec; no single four-worker browser matrix run was green on this loaded machine (see Latest verification); a fixture server sometimes fails its close on SIGTERM and leaves a small temporary root (cause not identified); the device switcher still overflows the sidebar at 761 to 1024 px (pre-existing), and a dialog that closes can still swallow the existing `Project saved.` toast (decision 453). Decisions 428 to 472. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Projects phase 2: the coordinator (2026-10-04, goncalos-macbook-pro): an owner message to a project (`POST /api/projects/:id/coordinator/messages`, idempotent by client id) assigns the coordinator to the receiving device and queues it. The coordinator runs one read-only turn at a time in the project checkout, delivers events that arrive during a turn together in the next one, resumes its native session and starts a fresh one at 40 turns, on a model or effort change, on an account move and on Fresh coordinator session, with the brief's fresh-session context (notebook, active threads, open questions, recap) and event block. Its bridge tools start, list, read, message and stop threads, ask and withdraw owner questions, read and write the revisioned notebook, check pull requests and search and read project memory, each leaving one chat line. Without a model or account, or after two failed turns, needs-decision reports become owner questions directly (brief decision 7). New routes serve the chat as a resumable SSE stream (`event: project`), Stop, Fresh, work settings, the notebook and decision answers; a restart drops a running coordinator turn and the next start delivers its events. Chat event records are written when an event is received and marked delivered through the turn's event ids, the deliberate deviation of decision 238. Evidence label: **simulated**. Coordinator and thread turns come from FakeRuntime scripted turns that call the daemon's real bridge, and GitHub is the local fake server; the daemon, its routes and SSE, Git, the hub's SQLite store and process groups are real. Not run: vision checks (phase 2 adds no interface; `JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set), live Claude and Codex coordinator turns, live GitHub, PJ-live (phase 8) and a coordinator on another device (refused with 409 until phase 5). PJ2 and PJ2b to PJ2e pass; see [PJ2 evidence](PJ2.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: the chat, decision and notebook interface is phase 3; coordinator routes for another device and Move coordinator here are phase 5; after two failed turns the wait for an owner message is kept in memory, so after a restart in that state the queue also waits for a message (decision 405). Decisions 393 to 427. Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Projects phase 1: turns, threads, worktrees and pull requests (2026-10-04, goncalos-macbook-pro): an owner can start a thread on a project with `POST /api/projects/:id/threads`. Jevellan places it on this device with the deterministic fallback (Jev placement is phase 4), prepares its own git worktree and `jv/` branch outside the checkout from a private base fetch, and runs turns through the new runtime `startTurn` (Claude, Codex and FakeRuntime workers with `thread` and `coordinator` safety profiles) that report through the project bridge tools. After a done report it commits leftovers, runs the project's test command (three attempts with exact failure prompts), pushes with a lease, opens a pull request with the brief's body, follows checks, conflicts, merges and closes, and removes the worktree after the merge; restart recovery leaves interrupted threads idle and resumes no model. Thread indexes, questions, work settings and the GitHub token secret live on the hub, and members reach them through one route per collection. Evidence label: **simulated**. Turns come from FakeRuntime and simulated provider CLIs, and GitHub is a local fake server behind a GitHub-shaped remote redirected with `insteadOf`; Git, the daemon's HTTP routes and bridge, the hub's SQLite store and process groups are real. Not run: vision checks (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set; phase 1 adds no interface), live Claude and Codex turns, live GitHub, a second device and PJ-live (phase 8). PJ1 to PJ1f pass; see [PJ1 evidence](PJ1.md), the Projects phase progress table and Latest verification. Known gaps: coordinator turns, the notebook, decision answers and their routes arrive in phase 2; accounting failures during a turn are not retried across a hub outage; the heaviest browser journeys still time out about twice per 160 cases with four workers on this loaded machine (each passes alone); one owner question is open (decision 388). Pre-existing problems found while verifying were fixed separately with regression coverage (decisions 380, 382 to 391). Not deployed. Gonçalo's acceptance is not claimed.

- Test alignment and a guard fix (2026-10-03, goncalos-macbook-pro): ten tests that predated the plan-acceptance default and the answering-step rule were aligned (test-only, `6e7df58`); three timing budgets were widened with evidence. A reply to a no-progress stop now restarts that count (decision 235), with service, automatic and unit regression tests (simulated runtime). This machine needs Git 2.55 from Homebrew for the merge-tree preflight; Apple Git 2.39 fails it.

- Plan acceptance and readable proposals (2026-10-03): plan approval defaults on. Pending plans restrict orchestration to discussion and planning, and cannot advance to implementation or Done before approval. Explicit Request changes binds feedback to the current plan and schedules another planning step; stale approvals are rejected and revised plans need fresh acceptance. Plans use a full-width reading card with ordered steps, structured sections, Markdown, loading recovery and inline Approve plan / Request changes controls. Existing JSON and JSON-string plans are formatted without rewriting their stored content. Three focused backend/replay cases passed, plus four desktop/phone light/dark browser workflows; typecheck, lint and build passed. Live deployment and served-build match verified; plan approval is enabled and other effective settings are preserved. No live plan was approved or existing work undone. See [design and evidence](plan-acceptance-2026-10-03.md).

- Automatic move to main (2026-10-02): foreground conversations and project Context setup now switch a clean, published branch to main and prominently announce the move in setup, the conversation heading and its durable timeline. If main is absent locally and on origin, create it from the published source branch with a create-only remote lease; preserve the original branch and remote default. Background maintenance still requires main. Dirty files, unpublished work, detached HEAD and unfinished Git operations remain explicit blockers. Simulated verification: 14 focused regressions passed across Git policy, Context setup and the UI setup helper, including single-branch clones and concurrent remote creation. Typecheck, lint, build and whitespace checks passed. Live: a new isolated release is serving the existing tailnet URL after an idle-gated update; the reported project moved to main, completed Context setup and stored its completed warning receipt. Full unrelated suites, live provider execution and authenticated visual inspection were not run. See [behavior and evidence](automatic-main-2026-10-02.md).

- Conversation continuity and readable replies (2026-10-02): live inspection confirmed that a short summary request caused routing-only completion, with no answering stretch and a repeated earlier closing summary. Added a durable unanswered-message completion guard, latest-message priority, bounded actual exchanges across closed work, saved-answer context and request-sensitive brevity. Chronological follow-ups retain question/blocker evidence for Jev to interpret; no keyword routing. The transcript now shows one primary answer, grouped tool activity and expanded thinking, with handoffs/findings/technical metadata and historical blockers under Step details. Changes, Why, corrections, timestamps, edit/retry and steer/queue remain. Simulated verification: 112 unique focused backend/transcript cases passed across the targeted run and final focused reruns; four dedicated-key live Jev cases were skipped because JEVELLAN_TEST_JEV_KEY is absent. Four desktop/phone light/dark browser cases passed, including full result loading, historical fallback, details, tool output, blocker visibility and duplicate notices. Typecheck, lint, build and whitespace checks passed. The initial long reproduction exceeded its fixture timeout and passed with a bounded 60-second limit; installed Chrome resolved the missing bundled Chromium. Live: installed a new frozen release outside the checkout and switched only after the lifecycle gate confirmed idle; health and tailnet HTML match, and account/project/authentication, encrypted-secret, configuration, vault-key and eight immutable-ledger file hashes are preserved. Reloaded the reported conversation and verified the lighter rendered view with no captured browser errors. The original summary request remains in history; it was not resent. Dedicated provider contracts, automated vision judgments, physical-phone checks and full unrelated suites were not run. See [analysis, design and evidence limits](conversation-continuity-2026-10-02.md). User acceptance is not claimed.

- Shell-session visibility (2026-10-01): added an accessible Shell sessions switch below the sidebar filters. Hiding excludes all native Cursor, Claude Code and Codex entries from the visible list, search and filter counts, and hides their unavailable-source notice. Ordinary conversations, saved names/order and any open transcript remain intact. Design choice: default on to preserve the current view; remember visibility per browser in a versioned validated preference, synchronized across tabs. Build, typecheck, lint and whitespace checks passed. Automated tests and browser interaction checks were not run under the user’s iteration preference. Live web-only deployment completed with a backup and matching served HTML, without restarting the daemon.

- Adjustable Changes panel (2026-10-01): shared details panels support pointer dragging, arrow-key resizing and double-click/Home reset, with a versioned validated browser width preference and viewport bounds. Fixed the clipped heading by separating its height from the removed conversation header. Changes now groups the recorded diff into collapsible file sections, shows addition/deletion totals and old/new line numbers, uses theme-aware colors, and provides a Wrap lines toggle. Recorded-file links remain available in a compact disclosure; evidence and recovery controls are preserved. Design choice: unified per-file diffs with horizontal scrolling by default, a minimum conversation width on desktop, and a full-width overlay on phones. Live desktop inspection verified the heading, drag resizing, keyboard resizing and wrapping against an existing recorded step; no agent message or project action was sent. Build, typecheck, lint and whitespace checks passed. Automated tests and physical-phone checks were not run under the user’s iteration preference. Web assets deployed with backups, no daemon restart, and served HTML matching the new build.

- User message readability and actions (2026-10-01): fixed dark-on-dark Markdown in ordinary conversation bubbles and preserved normal bold formatting. Added Retry and inline Edit/Send edited message/Cancel. Design choice: editing resends revised text while preserving earlier messages and work; retrying the latest paused request uses the existing generation-checked resume path without duplicating the message, while other retries resend the selected text. Existing running-work, checkpoint and guard restrictions remain enforced; resend IDs are retained across ambiguous failures. Four focused desktop/phone light/dark browser cases passed against an isolated local daemon with simulated provider availability, covering contrast, paused retry, edit cancellation, edited resend, historical retry, persistence and horizontal overflow. Initial fixture-directory and missing bundled-browser launch issues were resolved using an explicit working directory and installed Chrome in temporary profiles. Build, typecheck, lint and whitespace checks passed. Full suites and live provider execution were not run. Live: web-only deployment without daemon restart succeeded and served tailnet HTML matched the new build. The reported user request was not retried or edited.

- Account availability display (2026-10-01): account badges now distinguish successful authentication from cooldown, exhausted quota, configured usage ceiling, model-specific cooldown, disabled runtime and failed checks. Per-device rows use the same presentation. Usage observations show their check time. Login completion no longer promises Ready; a disabled account is explicitly identified with an Enable account button using the existing revision-checked update. Authentication does not silently reverse an intentional disable. Nine focused simulated-status regressions passed, including consistency with account ranking; build/typecheck/lint and whitespace checks passed. Live: enabled the reported signed-in account through Settings, refreshed the other account, and verified the corrected Ready/Cooling down badges and check times after a web-only deployment without daemon restart. The refreshed provider usage values were unchanged; comparison with the provider website and a new OAuth login were not run.

- Conversation timing (2026-10-01): ordinary stretches show compact start/end timestamps to the second and duration, with a live elapsed timer only while running. User messages and notes show their recorded ledger time. Browser-local formatting includes full date/year/timezone on hover and machine-readable time elements. Missing historical end times are explicit rather than represented by an indefinitely increasing duration. Uses existing versioned timestamps; no data migration. Latest main was pulled (already current). Build, typecheck, lint and whitespace checks passed; automated tests and visual browser checks were not run under the user's standing preference. Live deployment updated only the verified pilot's web files, backed up the prior files, preserved old hashed assets and did not restart the daemon; served tailnet HTML matched the new build.

- Operational action routing (2026-09-29): clarified the distinction between executing a command and explaining it, with shell/write work using implement. Reply hands unfinished execution back as partial instead of inventing a user blocker or suggesting integrate. Main-policy update requests use the existing owned fast-forward before writing. Focused local regressions pass; dedicated-key live classification is blocked. See [evidence and limits](action-routing-2026-09-29.md).

- Ordinary conversation parity (2026-09-29): confirmed latest published main and that the clean Dev Madrid checkout is already an ancestor. Native and ordinary transcripts now share a renderer; ordinary conversations keep chronological text/tools, visible completed responses, formatted input/output and expanded thinking captured from readable future SDK events. The compact composer gains live activity and Jump to latest. Existing rename, drag/long-press, pinned user message and steer/queue behavior already applied to both. Four synthetic cases added but not run under the user's preference. See [scope and evidence](conversation-parity-2026-09-29.md).

- Session drag-and-drop (2026-09-29): replaced Move up/down buttons with whole-row desktop dragging and mobile long-press activation, a floating preview, insertion marker and edge scrolling. Normal swipes scroll; cancellation leaves ordering unchanged. Existing saved names/order and Rename menu are retained. No automated or physical-touch tests run. See [follow-up](session-list-controls-2026-09-29.md#drag-and-drop-follow-up).

- Session list controls (2026-09-29): per-row Rename, Move up and Move down work across native sessions and ordinary conversations; saved ordering survives activity updates, with Sort by activity to reset it. Native display names and list order live in the serving installation's isolated versioned document, shared by its browsers. Ordinary rename keeps its existing owner-routed operation. Build/typecheck/lint pass; automated and browser interaction tests were not run. See [evidence and design choice](session-list-controls-2026-09-29.md).

- Native conversation listing (2026-09-29): Claude Code and Codex now share Cursor's compact formatted transcript view and five-day sidebar discovery, with activity indicators, native subagent exclusion and paired tool results. This phase is read-only for the two new runtimes. Deployed on the mini with readers over existing CSG and Dev Madrid SSH access; live discovery returned 11 Claude and 4 Codex sessions without unavailable sources. Build/typecheck/lint pass; eight synthetic tests were added but not run at the user's request. Design choice: extend the existing versioned session transport additively, retain metadata-only heartbeats and avoid new services or native hooks. See [evidence and limits](native-conversations-2026-09-29.md).

- Cursor transcript density (2026-09-28): removed repeated agent-name labels and reduced inter-block spacing to 8 pixels, preserving distinct user messages. Deployed web assets; build/typecheck/lint pass. See [evidence](conversation-space-2026-09-28.md#transcript-density-follow-up).

- Cursor disclosure follow-up (2026-09-28): fixed doubled collapse/expand arrows and made thinking blocks expanded by default, with manual toggling retained. Deployed web assets and verified in the live browser; build/typecheck/lint pass. See [evidence](conversation-space-2026-09-28.md#disclosure-follow-up).

- Conversation reading-space update (2026-09-28): removed the redundant header/footer, moved the device switcher and session settings into the sidebar, widened transcripts, pinned the latest human message to two lines and added a growing one-line composer with separate send/steer and queue icons. Mini and CSG reader deployed; live desktop and phone-width checks, build, typecheck and lint passed. No agent message was sent. See [evidence](conversation-space-2026-09-28.md).

- Cursor subagent follow-up (2026-09-28): explicit child metadata now excludes subagents from ordinary conversations, journal/hook rediscovery and reconnect-cache fallback. Build, typecheck, lint and 21 focused regressions pass. The mini and CSG reader are updated. After the user restored the existing tunnel, live discovery returned 50 main conversations, excluded 125 subagents and retained the reported parent without unavailable sources. See [evidence](cursor-windows-2026-09-28.md).

- CSG Windows discovery repair (2026-09-28): the missing conversation was in the Windows Cursor database while Jevellan read only WSL. A Windows reader now uses the same existing dev tunnel; checkpoint recency, pending subagent activity and saved tool details are supported. The isolated Windows runtime and seven Jevellan hooks preserve the four existing hooks and system Node. All 18 focused regressions, build, typecheck and lint pass. Live browser inspection confirmed the restored working conversation and tool details; the final database transcript transfer completed in 1.84 seconds. Live delivery and phone acceptance remain untested. See [Windows discovery evidence](cursor-windows-2026-09-28.md).

- Installable web app (2026-09-28): standalone manifest, navy J home-screen icons, Apple metadata, install controls/guidance, safe-area spacing, public-only versioned caches, reconnect screen and explicit Reload/Later update flow are deployed on the Mac mini at the existing tailnet address. Live health and served-file readiness observations passed. The simple browser-native design adds no runtime dependency. Build, typecheck and lint pass; automated tests and physical installation are not run under the user’s iteration preference. See [PWA evidence](pwa-2026-09-28.md).

- Cursor feedback repair (2026-09-28): the user reported missing live feedback, raw timestamp titles and invisible tool text. The repair is deployed on the Mac mini; all 13 focused Cursor regression cases pass. Live browser inspection confirmed clean titles, readable tool details, changing CSG tool/thinking activity and Jump to latest. Seven Jevellan hooks are installed on each machine with all unrelated hooks preserved. Old journal-only calls no longer claim to be running. Broad tests and message-delivery acceptance remain deferred. See [repair evidence](cursor-feedback-2026-09-28.md).

- Cursor conversation iteration (2026-09-28): five-day desktop discovery, ordinary sidebar integration, formatted transcripts, activity indicators, existing-dev-tunnel SSH helpers and desktop-hook message delivery are implemented. The composer offers steering versus next-step delivery for ordinary conversations too. Build, typecheck and lint passed; no tests were run, as explicitly requested. With the user's hook approval and restarted tunnel, five Jevellan hooks are installed on CSG (all 12 existing hooks preserved) and the local Mac (all 17 preserved). An optional gateway uses the existing tunnel and identity on Dev Madrid. Live read-only observation returned two recent CSG conversations, both idle; messaging still needs a native Cursor turn to connect its hooks. Standalone packaging was repaired after installation exposed an unrelated bundled command entry point. At the user's request, main through `8075c8e` now runs in an independent copy on the Mac mini with the existing pilot data and tailnet address on port 9444. Installation readiness confirmed the exact served UI, health and two CSG sessions without unavailable sources. Dev Madrid's checkout and services are unchanged. See [the iteration report](cursor-conversations-2026-09-28.md). No live delivery, phone, Enterprise-ledger or user acceptance is claimed.

- Interface overhaul (2026-09-26, dev-madrid): the web application now uses the website's nautical-chart theme (the same colour tokens, Bricolage Grotesque, Geist and JetBrains Mono, light and dark) and the layout from the site's app mock-up. Why, Changes, evidence and memory notes open in a docked inspector column (a non-modal side panel) instead of covering the page; forms stay modal. Conversations use compact step cards with a mono chip row, grouped tool calls, pill links and a sticky composer with the three choice pills. Settings use an in-page section rail on desktop and a scrolling strip on phones; Projects and Devices use a dense card grid. New conversation now has the brief's Device field with the disabled "Choose automatically" option (the J8 test asserts it; the earlier evidence recorded it without a visible control). The sidebar gained search and waiting counts, and the tab title shows how many conversations wait for you. The Improver page is rebuilt on the unified card contract with schedule, jobs, last runs, trial log, morning memory-care cards and Change it. Browser matrix after the overhaul: **132 passed** in four layouts (vision checks not run in that pass). With the J10/J12 improver journeys added on their own fixture servers: **140 passed** in four layouts (2026-09-27, vision checks not run in that pass). Test changes were limited to portability (a hard-coded `Mac.lan` device name, a macOS-only `/private/tmp` path, load-sensitive timeouts) and the new structure (collapsed transcripts, the inspector, the choice pills).
- Vision checks (section 16): every browser screenshot is judged by Claude through the Agent SDK with the brief's rubric (`tests/e2e/vision.ts`, wired as a fixture in `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`), in a disposable configuration home with only the dedicated test token. Each screenshot is judged against its own name, with animations finished and the caret hidden; full-page captures grow the viewport instead of stretching the page, so sticky bars stay where users see them. The first vision-enabled matrix (2026-09-27) flagged 81 of 240 screenshots. The real defects it found are fixed: conversation rows collapsed when the sidebar list overflowed, success toasts never left and covered controls, the hub-wait notice covered dialog footers, dialog messages hid behind the modal backdrop, the source viewer's code was near-invisible in light mode, memory search results were broken pills, long account buttons overflowed on phones, a stale "Pick the next step:" notice showed without its picker, nested review actions pinned over diffs, the memory viewer lacked the public-repository notice, and the managed Rigging editor was titled "Edit local item". Final vision run: 247 of 248 screenshots without blocking findings; the one flagged step screenshot passed in all four layouts after the per-step wording was clarified.
- Environment (dev-madrid): the machine's global APM is 0.24.0, which emits files the pinned 0.10.0 delivery rejects ("APM produced an unsupported runtime file"). Tests and fixtures here use a private pinned APM 0.10.0 under `~/.jevellan-build/tools`. The dedicated Jev key and Claude token are available for live checks; there is still no dedicated Codex test login on this machine.
- Machine handoff (2026-09-26): the user requested all remaining project work be committed and pushed to continue on another machine. The checkpoint includes unfinished phase-6 source, tests and evidence; it is not a completed phase or a redeployment. See [handoff notes](machine-handoff-2026-09-26.md).

- Git authentication repair: Settings now exposes per-device Git connection preferences and a project connection check. The pilot uses the existing GitHub SSH identity for Jevellan's Git commands; the live `sequoias` read-access check passed. Repository remotes and native Git configuration were preserved. The isolated release passed typecheck, lint, build, targeted backend checks and all four phone/desktop browser layouts, then deployed with account/authentication/project/ledger preservation checks. See [Git settings evidence](git-settings-2026-09-26.md).

- Public website: the supplied English/Portuguese design is implemented in `site/`, with mobile header, command wrapping, touch controls and keyboard menu improvements. GitHub Pages is live at <https://gongiskhan.github.io/jevellan/> and publishes through Actions from main. The first deployment succeeded; live phone checks and exact source comparisons for both HTML pages and all 11 assets passed. The mobile showcase follow-up adds an interactive phone with progress, Why, and steering; local responsive and interaction checks pass. The update is deployed, with the public phone composer verified at 390 pixels. See [website evidence](website-2026-09-25.md).

- User-reported workflow failures and mobile UI repair: [workflow-repair-2026-09-25.md](workflow-repair-2026-09-25.md). Reproduced blocked Claude tool discovery and the activity observer timeout; fixes and responsive checks pass locally. The user explicitly authorized redeployment after removing the daemon restriction. The repaired build is live; activation preserved accounts, authentication, projects and existing ledgers. The real Todo app implementation handed off successfully. A subsequent review exposed native read-only MCP approval denial; that scoped permission fix passed 54 checks and was deployed at 20:10 UTC. The final read-only handoff succeeded, Jev chose Done, and Jevellan independently passed 17/17 tests with stable HEAD and files. The launch brief and repair path now provide exact stretch numbers to remove the guesswork exposed during this run.

- Published: the completed phase-5 checkpoint and deployed repairs are committed on main as `3d1c3fb` and pushed to the public [gongiskhan/jevellan repository](https://github.com/gongiskhan/jevellan). Unfinished phase-6 changes are included in the subsequent machine-handoff checkpoint, while remaining excluded from the running pilot. The pre-push secret scan passed. The before/after visibility comparison preserved all 207 existing repositories; the only addition was public Jevellan. GitHub browser authentication and SSH were used because the gh API login is expired; native logins were not changed. Historical approval/publication blockers below are resolved.
- Built: the workspace compiles with TypeScript and Vite. Phase 1 adds versioned schemas, isolated homes, SQLite configuration revisions, AES-256-GCM secrets, account eligibility/ranking, runtime workers and factories, login drivers, provider probes, APM Rigging delivery, environment filtering and redaction. Settings exposes passphrase login, runtimes and accounts, login panels, local/package Rigging, editable decision guidance, configuration history/import/export and secret summaries. The responsive shell has light/dark themes and a web-app manifest. Phase 2 adds the durable conversation components, owned stretch execution and bridge, git ownership/verification/publication, isolated memory operations and capture queue, context-file handling and restart recovery. The manual owner loop and startup recovery are connected to authenticated HTTP/SSE routes and the conversation UI. Projects supports add/edit, context creation/keep/merge with read-only draft review and verified publication, and read-only memory browsing; timelines show full plans, streamed tools/text, manual choices, Why and Changes with independent verification receipts. Close this work now publishes, keeps or discards checkpoints with durable settlement receipts, saved recovery refs and cancelled-work recovery. The correction endpoint and Change this step UI record overrides in the ledger and hub index, stop active execution for undo, bind resets/reverts to checkpoint receipts and launch the selected redo. Prepared undo destinations now recover missing Git results without repeating the checkout mutation; explicit retries preserve the correction and prevent duplicate redo launches. Recorded checkpoint ranges support repeated published undo while retaining unrelated upstream commits. Project memory is now an on-by-default built-in delivered by APM, with editable runtime toggles, bounded capture commands, launch-time delivery verification and durable scoped metadata capture. Blocked file changes now have an explicit review/refresh/Continue path, exact prepared checkpoints, restart recovery, deferred memory capture and later undo. Native Git rewrite receipts now bind undo to integrated checkpoints, including conflict resolutions, dropped commits and captured memory. A scoped integration tool preserves existing Git hooks, and retry recovery requires its recorded mapping. Undo of the last closed work now includes newer open work, preserves both requests and notes, transfers checkout ownership without releasing it, and recovers an applied Git result after restart. Editable titles and the conversation menu now include durable Finished outside Jevellan outcomes, stopped-process cleanup, optional reasons, kept-change settlement and restart recovery. File links from Markdown, tool inputs, findings and Changes now open recorded checkpoint contents or an explicitly selected working copy; the viewer renders source lines, Markdown and images. Projects now checks repository visibility for the public-memory notice, and memory search/detail shows local note modification times. Rigging discovers account-local files and config entries, supports stale-safe autosave, preserves whole bundles when parked/restored, and exposes durable retry or keep-current-files recovery.
- Installed: a frozen phase-5 pilot copy is running outside this checkout; no persistent OS service is installed. See [pilot handoff](PILOT.md). A disposable private uv/Python/APM/Basic Memory installation passed its live checks; see [dependency evidence](phase5-tools.md).
- Decision layer: automatic action/model/effort selection, scored recall, retry/manual fallback and Why are connected. Composer choices now apply once or persist as conversation pins, with durable override records and correction feedback. Local provider simulations have passed. The user subsequently configured the pilot accounts; real Jev classification now succeeds. Dedicated environment-based test credentials remain separate. See [composer evidence](phase3-composer.md).
- Mesh: joined devices share accounts, configuration, ownership, conversation indexes and project bindings. Device switching, remote per-device login, live owner streaming/corrections, hub waits, thirty-second configuration/Rigging synchronization and loopback/Tailscale listeners are connected. The complete [J8](J8.md) and [J11](J11.md) simulated mesh journeys pass in four layouts with separate local daemons and checkouts. J11 exposed and fixed stale memory recall after a checkout update. Account and managed Rigging saves now recover lost replies. Project/Jev saves, local Rigging and device issuance recovery are implemented; final local phase verification passed. No real second-machine acceptance is claimed.
- Running: the [pilot application](https://goncalos-mac-mini-1.tail31efa.ts.net:9444/) and separate read-only [progress report](https://goncalos-mac-mini-1.tail31efa.ts.net:9443/) on Tailscale. The pilot uses actual adapters, a dedicated Codex account and the disposable sandbox project. Its initial read-only reply stopped before launch because the moved account lacked its existing hook ownership record; that exact matching record was restored and automatic delivery now succeeds. The reply has not been rerun. First-use passphrase setup belongs to the user. See [pilot handoff](PILOT.md).
- Tested (2026-09-27, dev-madrid): typecheck, lint and build pass; the complete backend run passed 1091 tests with one missing-key skip; the vision-enabled browser matrix passed 139 of 140 in four layouts, and the remaining vision judgment passed in all four layouts on rerun. The crucial-issues review and its scoped second review are closed with regression tests. Earlier phase results are kept in Latest verification.
- Accepted by Gonçalo: not requested or claimed.

A later preflight recheck appended the current Garrison HEAD and full status to `~/.jevellan-build/garrison-baseline.txt`, preserving the original snapshot byte-for-byte. The original comparison remains the acceptance baseline; the appended snapshot does not erase the recorded upstream-status drift.

The baseline checker now parses each appended snapshot separately, including both historical command-label formats and the latest Markdown snapshot heading. Its current result is `headMatches: true`, `statusMatches: false`, `latestHeadMatches: true`, `latestStatusMatches: true`; it still exits nonzero for the original status drift. No Garrison files or refs were changed to make that check pass.

## Decisions taken during the build

1. Use port 9771 by default. Garrison's documented ports are 8777, 5777, the 80xx range, 9512 and profile offsets of 10000/20000. The installer will choose the next free port without killing any listener.
2. Use TypeScript project references for the npm workspaces. Each package compiles to its own dist directory; the web application uses Vite and React.
3. Pin TypeScript to 5.9.3. The first dependency resolution failed because the current TypeScript 7.0.2 exceeds typescript-eslint's supported range. Do not bypass peer-dependency validation.
4. Treat Garrison instructions as reference-project context, not authorization to operate its services, native homes, shared memory or checkout. Jevellan's specification overrides those instructions.
5. Preserve the preflight baseline and record the already-changed build-start status separately. Never rewrite history or the original evidence to manufacture a match.
6. Retain the Codex SDK. Points 1–4 of the transport spike passed. The dedicated Jevellan MCP bridge uses per-launch `env_vars` and `default_tools_approval_mode = "approve"`; its own token scopes enforce permissions. The initial `auto` setting prevented unattended tool calls, so it was replaced and the concurrent test rerun successfully. Tokens are never configuration arguments.
7. Run SDK execution inside a separate owned worker process group. The SDK itself does not expose enough process ownership information for complete descendant cleanup. Shared cleanup and live Codex descendant checks passed; live Claude cleanup remains blocked by its missing credential.
8. Pin Basic Memory to the installed and probed 0.22.1. Its configuration override and project-constrained resolver were tested using an isolated temporary configuration directory.
9. Claude read-only launches deny all shell tools as well as editing tools through PreToolUse. Read tools and scoped bridge calls remain available. This avoids pretending a prompt or bypass-mode allowlist enforces read-only access. Live confirmation remains blocked.
10. The phase 0 Codex rule parser passed 19 exact forms but missed four common variations; its live model made no tool call. Phase 1 uses the current hook interface for the shared command guard, as recorded below. Arbitrary programs and unsupported native tool paths remain outside its coverage.
11. Use Node's built-in SQLite API, with WAL and immediate transactions for revision checks. This avoids a native add-on dependency. Node 22.13 (or 23.4) is the minimum unflagged version, so package engines and the executable check those versions. The test machine has Node 22.22. SQLite remains experimental in Node 22; its warning is retained. See the [Node version history](https://nodejs.org/download/release/latest-jod/docs/api/sqlite.html).
12. Keep seed models disabled until runtime discovery confirms exact model ids and supported efforts. The seed descriptions are the brief's editable defaults, not claims about a discovered model's current pricing or capability.
13. Preserve encrypted data when the key is lost: refuse to initialize a replacement key for a populated vault. Authentication tags bind each encrypted secret to its id. Browser-facing summaries contain only saved state and a masked suffix.
14. Set HOME as well as the runtime-specific home variable to the isolated account home for agent processes. This also confines secondary CLI caches and prevents ordinary home-directory discovery from reaching the user's native setup.
15. Stage APM output under Jevellan before delivering it to account homes. Check ownership hashes before replacing or parking files; preserve loose and externally edited files. A matching fingerprint is insufficient if an owned file disappeared. Keep staging packages available because generated hooks can reference them.
16. Use node-pty and a headless terminal for UI-driven login. Terminal cells preserve cursor-rendered separators and soft-wrapped tokens; stripping ANSI text does not. Tokens go directly to the encrypted vault callback. The installation prepare step repairs node-pty 1.1.0's missing macOS helper executable bit in this installation only.
17. Current Codex supports PreToolUse hooks. Its [documented automation flag](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks) enables the per-launch hook without persisting a trust record for each stretch. A small CLI shim adds that flag while retaining SDK execution and sandbox controls. Project-local configuration is marked untrusted; Jevellan's account Rigging and per-launch hook are the controlled sources. The native live hook probe and a reordered git-push denial passed. This replaces reliance on prefix rules for the ordinary command forms and permits the integration-only rebase exception in the hook.
18. Implement the UI passphrase prerequisite with the first Settings APIs. Scrypt uses a random salt; device-bound signed sessions last seven days and logout revokes the session.
