Cookies are HttpOnly and SameSite=Strict. HTTPS/Tailscale listener integration remains phase 4 work. Browser test servers always use disposable data homes.
19. Credential replacement creates a new encrypted secret reference and invalidates outstanding probes. A late authentication failure for the previous credential cannot overwrite readiness for the replacement. An unavailable usage query preserves previously confirmed authentication while marking usage unknown. Codex API keys are checked against the provider; ChatGPT probes request an explicit native token refresh, as described in the [app-server authentication contract](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server).
20. Local and package Rigging selections are revisioned hub documents. APM delivery combines these selections with the configuration's APM dependencies. Safety is a locked built-in row whose hook is supplied at launch; it cannot be disabled or edited. Project memory is now an on-by-default built-in with per-runtime toggles and scoped queue capture (decisions 75–77). Account-local discovery and recovery are now implemented (decisions 96–99); promotion into managed delivery remains unfinished.
21. Share browser-safe zod schemas from `@jevellan/core/client`; do not bundle filesystem, vault or SDK code in the web application. The service worker caches only the public app shell and assets. Private API responses are excluded, and account/session data is not stored in browser local storage. Each browser-matrix project runs its own test daemon to avoid fixtures changing another viewport's configuration.

22. Track process ancestry before interruption or termination, including descendants which start another process group. Compare process start identities before sending signals so a reused PID is refused. Interruption removes owned descendants before same-session continuation; parallel interrupt requests share one cleanup promise. A live Codex fixture exposed escaped children in the initial group-only implementation; the corrected termination and interruption probes both passed. This does not claim detection of arbitrary children detached and reparented before any snapshot.
23. Pass Codex project trust as one TOML table override. The installed SDK flattens structured dotted paths incorrectly; an exact-path native config probe proves the table form disables project-local configuration. Because the untrusted layer also prevented native AGENTS context in the live probe, explicitly prepend the project AGENTS.md on the first turn. Accept local context symlinks, reject links outside the project and files over 32 KiB. The next live probe read the instruction marker without executing the project hook.
24. Allow PLAYWRIGHT_CHANNEL to select an already-installed browser. The bundled browser was unavailable on this machine, so the Settings matrix and live UI check used installed Google Chrome; no browser was installed for those checks. The live UI harness temporarily moved the whole dedicated Codex home and restored it after draining account operations; it never copied rotating credentials.
25. Scan pending tracked and untracked publishable files with --worktree as well as committed history. This lets the prepared checkpoint be checked without staging while commit approval is blocked. Symlink contents are scanned without following them into other locations.

26. The owner daemon is the only conversation writer. Remote tools and devices will forward writes to it. Ledger appends are flushed before acknowledgement, with a short exclusive append lock; startup recovery requires exclusive ownership of the daemon home. JSON projections are rebuilt from journal events. An interrupted trailing fragment remains unchanged and the next append rolls to a new segment; malformed complete records fail rather than silently skipping history. Handoffs are accepted in the ledger before their immutable file is materialised, so recovery can restore a missing file without accepting a different handoff.
27. Work messages carry their opening work id and any guard allowance in one durable event. A separate allowance receipt is idempotently repaired after a crash. Original request text is preserved, except that credential redaction always applies before persistence. Reply, note and pin changes bump generation; replay never launches work or assumes a persisted process has died.
28. Estimate brief tokens conservatively as UTF-8 bytes divided by three. Trim old handoffs, then findings, then middle follow-ups replaced by ledger pointers. Never trim the request, constraints or plan. Report an over-budget mandatory section explicitly instead of silently losing it. Memory excerpts have their own 2,000-token budget.
29. Checkout reservations do not expire with a daemon PID or a clean tree. The same work can reattach after its old daemon dies; a different work must wait until processes and unpublished commits are settled. Publication leases use hub time, last two minutes, renew every 30 seconds, and become unusable after a renewal failure. Local bare origins exercise git changes without relying on GitHub authentication.
30. Share owned-process cleanup from core so runtime workers, git commands and verification use the same process identity and descendant checks. Verification runs with an isolated HOME and a filtered environment, records full stdout/stderr in a blob, and additionally records whether HEAD stayed stable. A command exiting zero with a dirty tree or a changed HEAD does not establish a valid done-gate receipt.

31. Publication checks a daemon-written receipt before fetching, then re-verifies if rebase changes the commit. Conflicts are aborted and saved before a dedicated integrate callback runs. A real rejected push is fetched, integrated and verified again; three rejections leave local checkpoints blocked. These tests use real git operations and a simulated integration worker; the runtime loop that will supply that worker is still pending. Known GitHub URL forms and canonical local bare paths share publication keys.

32. Acquire exclusive ownership of each daemon data home before opening its application stores. A private SQLite transaction records PID, process start identity and a random instance token. A live owner prevents a second daemon from starting; an exited owner can be recovered, and an old instance cannot release its replacement. Real child-process competition and recovery tests pass.
33. A handoff repair is a reporting turn, so it loses code and memory write permissions. Claude resumes with dontAsk and its read-only callback; Codex uses the same thread id with read-only sandbox options, following the [official OpenAI SDK resume interface](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/codex-sdk) and the installed SDK's options. The bridge is downgraded before repair. Continue at most once (90 seconds; 60 for a correction), or use a fresh read-only session with the request and ledger tail when continuation is unsupported. Preserve native handoffs and original output; never turn handoff prose into application code. Cancellation permits an in-flight handoff for up to 30 seconds after process termination.
34. Scoped bridge tokens exist only in daemon memory, are sent in the launch environment and HTTP Authorization header, and never appear in command arguments. Tools cannot select another project or conversation. Tool calls are serialized within a stretch; shutdown revokes the token and drains pending memory operations before checkout ownership may be released. Read-only proposals are durable ledger entries, not immediate checkout writes. The actual Basic Memory implementation and proposal application are tested components; the owner loop still needs to connect them.
35. Ledger subscribers are notified only after append durability and lock release. A failed viewer is detached without stopping the writer; SSE replay will use persisted event ids, not observer memory.
36. Run Basic Memory with explicit local routing and isolated HOME/configuration. Disable self-updates, background sync, telemetry, semantic model downloads and automatic frontmatter/permalink changes. Use a foreground helper from the pinned provider's own Python environment for sync; validate its normalized registry name and exact project path. Exact filenames identify notes without a permalink. Installed-provider tests prove sync leaves imported Markdown unchanged.
37. Queued capture preserves an existing note with the same title. Checkpoint before recording the application receipt, so retries after interruption can finish without duplicate notes or commits. Manual recall searches the union of literal words from the request, latest message and action, up to 64 words from each section, then uses the provider's top twelve/top five ranking. Jev selection now replaces that final selection when available; search rank remains the labelled fallback.
38. Context choices carry a fingerprint of both instruction files and their tracked state. Refuse stale applications, including changes while acquiring ownership. External projects reject tracked replacements and merge applications; newly created seed files and compatibility links stay locally excluded. Until a live Claude check proves native AGENTS support, use the compatibility link. The owner loop will checkpoint real main-policy file changes.
39. Restart recovery never schedules a successor or releases ownership. It terminates the recorded process group, keeps accepted native handoffs unchanged, and records interruption plus the waiting notice in one durable event. A projection failure cannot lose the notice. A missing identity or a potentially reused PID remains unsettled with a durable error; it cannot manufacture a cleanup success. This is component-tested with real disposable workers; startup/UI wiring remains.


40. External-policy verification fingerprints tracked and non-ignored untracked paths, file content, modes and link targets before and after the test. A dirty external checkout can receive a valid content-specific receipt without a commit; a command that changes those files cannot establish completion. Main-policy verification still requires a clean checkpoint and an exact stable HEAD.
41. Check conversation generation before launch and at publication lease/push boundaries. A correction arriving during verification invalidates the pending completion. Cancellation aborts the owned verification process, waits for cleanup and preserves unpublished ownership.
42. Manual HTTP selection acknowledges asynchronously; the owner service alone schedules the stretch. Durable operation notices let other viewers see preparation and settlement. Decision projections and the hub decision index are rebuilt from accepted journal records. Requests that only read or replay history never schedule work.
43. SSE subscribes before replay, then reads from the durable cursor in bounded batches. Respect response backpressure, detach failed viewers and recheck authentication. Browser event/read responses omit native process and session identities, which remain available only for daemon recovery.
44. A failed post-stretch Git/checkpoint check records a durable block on that work. A later manual done or writing action cannot silently adopt or publish the unsafe state. Publish/Keep/Discard controls are now connected; explicit adoption of blocked uncommitted changes remains unfinished.
45. Phase 2 exposes manual selection in the conversation UI, with the selected account still ranked in code. Context inspection and memory browsing are read-only routes; their write workflows remain subject to checkout ownership and the upcoming project-maintenance controls.
46. A guard pause rejects further manual choices until a reply resumes the same work. The picker is hidden while that reply is required. Retried messages grant no duplicate allowance.

47. Record settlement intent before acting and completion only after the work and checkout reservation settle. Browser-generated ids deduplicate retries; generation checks reject stale choices, and settlement shares the conversation's exclusive operation slot with stretches. Discard records its saved ref before reset. A restart reports unfinished settlement and never resumes it automatically.
48. Keep closes the work while retaining its checkout reservation when changes remain. That conversation must publish or discard the kept work before accepting a new request, so a newer work cannot hide or inherit the older work's unpublished checkpoints. Other conversations remain blocked by checkout ownership. External-policy close keeps files and releases the reservation without git mutations.
49. Publishing cancelled or explicitly closed work temporarily reopens that same work for verification and possible integration, then preserves its original cancelled/closed-by-you outcome. Requests, counters and work ids are not reconstructed as a new task. Failed publication and restart preserve the original closed outcome and unsettled reservation.
50. Discard only resets clean, unpublished checkpoints to the work base, after saving their complete tip under refs/jevellan/discard. Fetch first and refuse any checkpoint already present in upstream history; published work needs the forthcoming revert-based undo. Dirty changes stay owned until explicitly inspected and checkpointed. The confirmation names repository code/memory and excludes outside effects.
51. Apply memory proposals from a successful integration stretch before final verification/publication. The service test now exercises a real conflicting upstream push, simulated integration, actual Basic Memory capture, a final code-and-memory verification receipt and publication to a local bare origin. It does not establish a live-provider acceptance journey.

52. Undo application is one versioned durable work event. Preserve requests, user messages, base commits and allowances; rebuild semantic state and guard counters from surviving steps. Keep immutable handoffs and monotonic step numbers, so redo cannot overwrite old evidence. Repeated ids repair projections without applying twice.
53. Git undo takes a recorded source range, saves its complete pre-operation tip, and persists the plan through a callback before changing HEAD. Unpublished reset checks the remote again immediately before mutation. Published undo reverts the original checkpoint commits rather than resetting a published branch; a later clean publication rebase does not require their old SHAs to remain ancestors of HEAD.
54. Older revert plans without an exact prepared destination retain sequence-abort recovery, with restored HEAD, clean tree and operation-state checks. New plans use the prepared commit mechanism in decision 62. Existing recovery refs cannot be replaced with different tips, and an unexpected HEAD during retry requires reconciliation rather than guessing that the operation can run again.
55. Clean porcelain output does not establish a settled Git operation. Undo and Discard also inspect rebase, sequencer, merge, cherry-pick and revert state. A real empty cherry-pick regression proves that its pending state remains untouched.
56. Browser tests wait for the Rigging installation response and validate successful delivery, then assert dialog closure. The earlier five-second generic assertion expired while APM was still installing during concurrent test execution. The bounded response wait retains positive delivery assertions instead of treating elapsed time as success.

57. Persist a before/after receipt for each writing or queued-memory checkpoint. Undo must bind to a continuous recorded chain; missing or rewritten boundaries require reconciliation rather than deriving a range from today's remote HEAD.
58. Store corrections in the conversation ledger and hub index without message text. Take the context from facts recorded with the original decision; identify legacy decisions lacking those facts instead of reconstructing their past state.
59. Undo waits for owned execution and its final checkpoint, records the Git plan before mutation and the result before conversation replay. A durable result can repair missing history at startup without a second Git mutation or runtime launch. An unresolved operation keeps its recovery evidence and reservation.
60. Refresh the isolated Basic Memory search index after repository undo. External-policy undo changes conversation history while preserving files and Git refs, and its UI states that limit.
61. After undo, launch the selected step with redo provenance. Subsequent work follows the current scheduling mode: manual until the Jev decision loop is implemented. Do not label this partial flow as completed J13 acceptance.
62. Prepare published undo as ordinary reverse-change commit objects before moving the checkout, pin the prepared result against garbage collection, and persist its exact destination in the plan. A conflicting reverse merge leaves the checkout untouched. Applying the prepared chain is a fast-forward; publication still requires the existing verification and lease checks. Actual Git 2.50.1 tests cover this mechanism.
63. Recover a missing undo result only when the saved backup and clean, settled HEAD match the recorded destination exactly. Startup repairs journal receipts without changing Git or launching a runtime. Preserve a cancellation recorded after the correction in the same undo event. Dirty, unexpected and older ambiguous states remain blocked for reconciliation.
64. Retry uses the original correction plus a versioned, generation-checked client request. Completed Git changes are retained. A decision tied to that correction and an actual started stretch (or completed done action) prevent another redo if the final operation receipt was lost. Integration used to prepare publication is not the requested redo.
65. After undo, compare HEAD with its result and subsequent daemon-recorded checkpoints/publications before resuming. Newer outside commits remain intact and block further checkout actions until reconciled; an idempotent retry cannot silently adopt them.
66. After an earlier published undo, surviving checkpoints may occupy separate recorded ranges. A completed undo receipt and its successful publication before the redo decision can explain the gap, including a clean rebase during that publication. Reverse only those ranges, newest first; reject duplicate checkpoints and never reset across a gap. An unexplained boundary or a conflict integration without mapped checkpoints still requires reconciliation.


67. Context creation and choices create ordinary visible work titled Context · {project}. Local-only links acquire ownership without committing; tracked main-policy changes use preparation, checkpoints, independent verification and publication. Failed verification keeps the checkpoint and reservation for Retry or settlement.
68. Context merge is a fixed read-only drafting task, using the explicitly selected model or the first eligible menu model at high effort. Persist the full draft and both original files before showing Apply/Cancel. Applying or retrying is a versioned, generation-checked, deduplicated request; Retry never grants approval. Preserve ready drafts across restart without automatically launching anything.
69. Record the observed project-context revision before drafting so the daemon's own metadata refresh does not stale its result. Later project edits and file fingerprints still fence application. External projects reject merge and tracked replacements before launch. Ambiguous partially applied changes remain blocked for inspection rather than being replayed.
70. Bind an applied merge checkpoint to its drafting step with a context receipt. Changes includes that receipt and subsequent memory checkpoints; retries bind it to the successful drafting step, not an earlier failed attempt; undo uses the existing recorded-boundary mechanism, preserving the original files and published-history rules.

71. Classify the complete work change before granting memory-only verification exemption, including attachments but detecting both sides of a rename. Check the remaining change again after integration so newly added code requires verification. An already-upstream memory patch may become empty. Publication still requires ownership, a clean tree and the remote lease.
72. Preserve both authored note versions for memory-only rebase conflicts, with upstream metadata outside and the complete local document appended under its device/date heading. Mark unresolved, retain delete/modify intent, and use Git blob hash round trips to refuse lossy binary or invalid-UTF-8 rewriting. Non-note and mixed conflicts retain the ordinary integration path; a later code conflict aborts all tentative note merges. This deliberately does not port Garrison's derived-content winner policy.
73. Refresh the isolated Basic Memory index after successful publication before closing work and releasing ownership. A successful automatic merge records source blob ids and the resulting commit in a versioned ledger receipt; the next recall labels unresolved versions.
74. Persist memory-only classification for the exact pre-push commit, original work base and upstream. Reuse it only for the same work, base and HEAD on retry, so code brought in from upstream is not misclassified as local work after a failed push. A subsequent local code change loses that exemption. A regression reproduced the erroneous blocked retry before the fix and passes after reopening the ledger.

75. Adapt Garrison's current metadata-only continuity hooks, not its retired raw-transcript capture. Project memory queues one structural checkpoint per step from owner-recorded metadata; PreCompact, Stop, SessionEnd and retries coalesce. Provider content, native session identifiers and transcripts never enter the proposal. A lifecycle event is not a completion receipt. There are no recall hooks, native-memory imports, global vaults or detached capture workers.
76. Skip automatic checkpoint notes for an ordinary answer-only reply, preserving J1's no-Git-change outcome. Explicit remember replies and other actions may queue metadata, but all writes wait for the settled owned boundary. Handoff repair, a disabled runtime toggle and token expiry prevent capture. Stop after handoff may queue the same metadata; it cannot change the accepted handoff or authorize direct writes.
77. Verify stable Rigging at launch so existing accounts receive new built-ins. Deliver through APM, check the required handlers before touching the account, preserve other hooks and merge Claude Settings hooks additively. Project memory content is immutable while runtime toggles are editable; Safety remains locked. Runtime toggles show the pending choice immediately during delivery and restore the saved choice on request failure; disabled rows remain reachable in the runtime filter. The hook uses a lightweight entry and a two-second input/request deadline within the native three-second timeout, emits empty JSON and never exposes provider errors or payloads.

78. Accept blocked uncommitted files only after an explicit review bound to their content, Git boundary, owning work and generation. Include untracked files in Changes. Persist the original Git snapshot on each block; older blocks without one cannot infer authority from today's checkout. External policy, changed Git history, an unresolved undo and another checkout owner prevent acceptance.
79. Prepare and retain the reviewed checkpoint before recording its intent and moving main by compare-and-swap. Exact prepared results let startup repair a lost receipt without repeating the commit or launching a runtime. Keep the original failed step and handoff, bind the new checkpoint to that step for undo, capture deferred memory findings, and block any newer unreviewed files separately. Preserve cancelled work's closed state while accepting its kept files. A restored clean tree needs no empty commit.
80. Preserve all Garrison baseline snapshots and parse their historical command labels, including `git -C ~/dev/garrison`, and the latest dated snapshot separator. The original status comparison still reports the pre-existing upstream drift; the latest appended snapshot matches. Parsing a later snapshot does not replace the original acceptance baseline.
81. Refresh the isolated Basic Memory index after accepting edited files and before applying deferred notes. Basic Memory's background synchronization is deliberately disabled, so an already-open provider otherwise keeps the old search results. A real-provider regression failed before this refresh and passes with it, including the next read-only brief.

82. Preserve the current build as an isolated preview copy and publish only curated read-only progress assets over the user's tailnet. Keep live Codex evidence distinct from simulated acceptance/undo captures and from the unimplemented Jev classifier. The preview does not expose a writable test daemon or install the product service.
83. Accept the native Codex file-change lifecycle's intermediate in-progress state, emit one Edit start and retain the actual terminal success/failure. Keep field-level validation diagnostics and preserve returned runtime errors in the ledger before handoff repair; an already streamed identical error is not duplicated. These cases were found in the requested live demonstration and have regression evidence.

84. Record native Git post-rewrite pairs under an isolated, versioned integration plan and pin the original checkpoints. Scope hook forwarding to owned Git commands, preserving existing hooks and Git configuration. Let integration stretches resolve files through a bounded bridge command, while code owns rebase/staging, lease checks and mapping capture. Validate ordered boundaries, treat dropped commits as no-ops, reconcile lost receipts before retry or undo, and retain ambiguous state for reconciliation. See [integration undo evidence](phase2-integration-undo.md).

85. Refresh project memory again after publishing undo reverts and before starting the selected redo. Upstream notes can arrive during that publication, after the pre-publication refresh; the next read-only step must see them. A real Basic Memory regression reproduced the missing note before this refresh.

86. Undo from the last closed work explicitly includes any newer open work and all of its steps. Reopen the selected work with its immutable original request, retain the newer work as cancelled with its own request, and carry its request and notes into the reopened work by ledger message identity. Preserve both requests verbatim and rebuild semantic state only from surviving steps. The confirmation states that newer work is included. See [cross-work undo evidence](phase2-undo-following.md).
87. Transfer checkout ownership directly between the two recorded works after execution terminates. Never release the reservation between them. Reconcile an interrupted hub/local mirror update only for this exact same-conversation pair; other work remains blocked. Persist both work identities before stopping execution or changing Git.
88. Translate each checkpoint range through applicable native rewrite receipts. A merge-base boundary changes to upstream only when that range's end is replayed by the same rewrite. Keep unchanged published checkpoints separate from later rewritten checkpoints so undo cannot absorb unrelated upstream history. The missing-upstream-file regression failed before this fix.

89. Rename conversations through a versioned, deduplicated ledger event with a previous-title comparison. Preserve generation, exact work requests, counters and execution. Trim titles and limit them to 200 characters. Update the hub index and sidebar immediately; keep expanded tool details mounted during those updates.
90. Finished outside Jevellan records durable intent before stopping execution, then records completion after process cleanup. Keep repository changes and existing main-policy reservations for settlement; release clean unchanged or external-policy ownership. Trim the optional reason, allow up to 2,000 characters, and preserve it with the completion time in conversation history. A repeated old request cannot close newer work. Publication temporarily reopens kept work without clearing its outside outcome. Starting new work or explicit undo clears the current outcome, while historical reasons remain available for the future Trial log.
91. Recover unfinished outside intents after normal process/undo recovery without launching models or publishing. Keep failed cleanup blocked with an explicit retry. Repair a missing context-draft cancellation projection from the completed conversation outcome. Permit reopened work to settle again despite an earlier completed settlement; the reproduced conflict now has a regression. See [conversation controls](phase2-conversation-controls.md).

92. File evidence is scoped to the conversation project and recorded step. Main-policy history opens the last checkpoint for that step, falling back only to its before-step version for deleted files. Current files require an explicit working-copy choice; external-policy projects are labelled as current contents. Refuse symbolic links, sensitive filenames, unsupported binary files and reads above 2 MB. Redact known secrets before returning text. File reads never mutate Git, schedule work or fetch remote URLs.
93. Use the same file/evidence panel for Markdown links, tool-input paths, streamed/handoff findings and changed-file lists. Resolve relative links inside Markdown against that file; preserve source line references. Show verification output as literal text and keep ordinary HTTP links external. Adapt the existing reference viewer tests without its writable workspace, uploads or host-rewriting behavior.

94. Probe saved project visibility read-only with GitHub CLI, using the resolved checkout and a five-second timeout. Treat missing CLI/authentication, timeout and invalid responses as unknown. Share only in-flight checks; opening Projects checks afresh. Hide the old public-memory notice when editing the saved path or remote. Single-device checks precede phase 4 owner routing.
95. Basic Memory 0.22.1 does not return an update time in its note JSON. Use the exact local note file’s modification time, validate it as an ISO timestamp, and identify its source in the viewer. This is local file history, not an assertion about the original authored date across checkouts. Preserve note content and modification time when browsing. See [project memory presentation](phase2-memory-viewer.md).

96. Discover Rigging only in registered Jevellan account homes and its parked stores. Use delivery hashes for ownership, retain ownership on drift, and keep installed copies read-only. The current delivery record owns configuration files as a whole; changed managed configurations can be parked as complete copies before delivery is retried. Show account/runtime scope explicitly.
97. Local text edits use content-and-ownership fingerprints and the same per-account queue as APM delivery. A stale edit requires reload. Preserve skill bundles and file modes. Parse individual supported config entries and preserve other values when writing JSON/TOML; formatting and TOML comments may normalize. Bound text to 1 MB and bundles to 256 files / 10 MB.
98. Persist a versioned move intent before parking/restoring. Preserve the destination before removing a config entry, compare exact source/destination results on retry, and retain both copies on outside changes. Explicit cancellation keeps files as they are. Metadata discovery never returns config values; invalid-config errors do not quote their input, detail is redacted, and credential-bearing moves are refused.
99. Automatic delivery snapshots remain read-only and recoverable, but identical already-active contents are not shown as disabled. Restore locally creates an account-local copy and never replaces an occupied file/server/setting or duplicates a hook. Promotion into APM-managed cross-runtime delivery is a separate explicit action. See [Rigging discovery and recovery](phase2-rigging-discovery.md).
100. Promote loose skills, rules and commands, matching the reference's file-based promotion scope. Keep the source runtime selected for the initial claim; use managed runtime toggles afterwards. Hooks, MCP entries and settings retain local editing and park/restore. Normalize the delivery basename independently of the user-editable display name.
101. Store captured skill assets as bytes and executable flags in a versioned portable bundle, alongside editable primary instructions. Public Settings views expose only supporting-file count, name and digest. APM must preserve every captured skill file and executable flag in private staging before account files change; exact raw skill instructions need no injected frontmatter. Local edits retain the supporting bundle.
102. Persist a promotion intent before claiming source files. Pending claims block account delivery until exact retry creates or recovers the managed source. Retain claim identity across later delivery, and never reset newer managed edits/toggles on request replay. Permit cancellation only before claiming; it preserves the current files. Empty directories left by parked delivery files are removed without removing nonempty siblings. See [managed Rigging promotion](phase2-rigging-promotion.md).

103. Render recalled memory from the decision record in Why: chosen note references first, the recorded selection source, and expandable candidates with scores only when stored. Do not reconstruct historical selection from today’s index. Legacy decisions without memory omit the section. The live J11 omission has a browser regression across desktop/phone and both themes.

104. Keep versioned local Jev envelopes separate from the provider wire shape. Preserve returned model and usage, reject malformed answers without repair, and expose only fixed transport-error descriptions. Resolve single-option choices in code; never manufacture probabilities.
105. Build the decision packet from configuration rules and separately supplied conversation evidence. Keep the request intact, trim oldest handoffs first, and use manual fallback if protected content still exceeds the 12,000-token cap. Memory score ties retain search order; cancellation never becomes rank-based fallback.

106. Run Call A and Call B in the owning conversation operation, retaining generation checks before each effect. Persist a typed waiting record for unavailable decisions; explicit retry requires the same generation and cannot bypass guards, plan approval or checkpoint settlement.
107. Retain returned model, usage and latency for each actual Jev call, including memory selection. Aggregate calls only from observed responses; manual records keep their source and unavailable notice. Account login links carry the selected account identity rather than attempting to rediscover it from notice text.
108. Save redo completion before starting subsequent automatic work. A failed save remains recoverable as blocked; a later automatic failure cannot turn an already-persisted completed redo into an incomplete operation.
109. Record composer intent immediately, then bind it to the actual first applicable decision when that decision is durably recorded. Pending/superseded choices stay in history and the hub index but do not become correction feedback. Record pins at their first actual use to avoid weighting one user choice as repeated corrections.
110. Resolve once before pin independently for each field. Setting a pin replaces that field's once choice; setting once preserves the pin for afterwards; Back to Auto clears both. Pins continue across work. Changes during a stretch do not interrupt it, redo does not consume queued choices, and forced integration preserves the queued action.
111. Use null for Auto, preserving real model identifiers such as `auto`. Describe composer feedback as the user's setting rather than fabricating a Jev answer. Outcome updates never bind choices that arrived after the decision began. A no-longer-allowed action waits without consuming the choice or calling Jev.
112. Include initial composer choices in Start's idempotency identity and use stable per-field request identities to recover interrupted initial writes. Why reads its referenced correction metadata through an authenticated, versioned, eight-record-bounded endpoint; no message text enters this index.
113. Commit composer binding identities and application states inside the first durable decision event. Replay consumes once from that event, even if later override receipts fail. Startup repairs missing receipts and the hub index without relaunching work. Outcome updates retain the first decision's bindings; they cannot consume choices accepted during its stretch. A planned stretch reference alone never proves runtime launch.

114. Consume a join code in the same SQLite transaction as the device and encrypted token. During its ten-minute lifetime, an identical normalized request may recover the same token; another request cannot use that code or replace an existing device. Nested document/vault transactions use savepoints.
115. Calculate device presence from hub receipt time, retaining the reported heartbeat separately. Exact ninety-second and ten-minute boundaries follow the brief; missing, invalid and future receipt times are offline. Switch grants store only token hashes, bind the target and local route, and expire at sixty seconds. Device origins preserve ports; direct HTTP is limited to loopback and Tailscale IPv4.

116. Keep device credential-exchange routes separate from browser Settings routes. Device bearer identity supplies the acting member; UI cookies do not authorize device endpoints. Only explicitly validated join/sign-in/switch responses deliver authentication material. Normal data responses remain redacted.
117. Share the UI signing key with joined members in a private authentication file, but require the hub to confirm session revocation before accepting member requests. A local signature alone cannot turn a hub outage into successful authentication. Recheck device authorization after asynchronous passphrase derivation.
118. Save stable join metadata without its code, under the existing exclusive data-home ownership lock. Write device.json last. Retry a lost response with the same request; if the device token was already saved, recover remaining signing material through bearer-authenticated HTTP. Preserve conflicting or unexplained authentication files.
119. Put shared account operations behind an asynchronous, device-bound interface. Keep provider processes on the requesting device and account metadata, encrypted credentials and model-menu reconciliation on the hub. The member asks for authoritative data over versioned HTTP; it does not simulate synchronous database access with an offline account cache.
120. Replace a shared credential in one hub transaction, including every known device's readiness invalidation and removal of the old secret. Tie status, identity and usage reports to the credential reference actually used. A late report for a replaced reference cannot change the new credential's status; credential delivery is scoped to the current account reference.
121. Drain account requests during shutdown and check closure before deferred local preparation starts. Queue runtime usage reports without holding up the running process, retain its local outcome before surfacing a reporting failure, and await fresh account authority before recording a new stretch as running. Full member outage/ownership recovery remains a separate unfinished boundary.
122. Execute publication lease operations on the hub, using its clock and a device-scoped work identity. Members request acquire, renew, assert and release rather than calculate lease authority locally. A failed renewal remains a push barrier for that publication attempt after transport recovers.
123. Bind checkout records to the authenticated device and absolute-path hash, use revision checks for changes, and mirror the hub-returned claim locally. Reconcile a lost successful release reply only for the exact recorded owner and process; never clear a later owner's reservation. Keep the local reservation when the hub is unreachable.
124. Keep only the conversation and decision index fields prescribed by §5.6–5.7 on the hub. Migrate existing full hub records together at startup; leave owner ledgers and full projections intact. Version the narrower conversation-list response as v2 rather than accepting full records through the shared API.
125. Order index writes by the owner's durable ledger event sequence, not its wall clock. Bind writes to the conversation owner and project, commit each document with its acknowledgement cursor, make identical replay idempotent, and reject conflicting content at the same event. Page conversation/correction reads in groups of at most one hundred.
126. Use the existing durable ledger as the index delivery recovery source. Coalesce pending projections in memory, deliver conversation ownership before child records, retry after thirty seconds, and reconstruct current records on startup without relaunching work. A delayed acknowledgement removes only the exact update sent; authoritative reads still require the hub.
127. Await shared correction data before building a new decision packet, retaining the existing generation check afterwards. Index delivery itself stays outside the runtime event stream and local completion path; shutdown drains its in-flight request, and startup can reconstruct any undelivered result.
128. Use explicit, versioned shared-state operations for configuration, projects, rigging and Jev credentials. Fetch authoritative values before new work and retain revision checks on writes; a prior successful response is not a member's offline authority. Deliver launch credentials separately from browser-facing summaries.
129. Store context operations and merge drafts in the owner's isolated home. Migrate legacy shared copies only after verifying the owning conversation and saving every local copy. Exact interrupted migration can replay; differing copies remain intact for recovery.
130. Record the current configured allowance in each new work request, leaving replay independent of current configuration. Recheck generation after remote lookups; a running step keeps its delivered rigging and owner-local stream and handoff storage.
131. Hold the existing account-local promotion lock and source claim across asynchronous rigging validation and creation. Preserve the captured item's identity so a lost successful reply can be reconciled without duplicating the shared item or overwriting later local changes.
132. Construct services from the registered device role. A member opens private registration files and authenticated hub clients, never an authoritative local hub database. Refuse incomplete joins instead of silently creating a new hub. Load saved authentication values into the restart's redactor without exposing them.
133. Allow only an already authorized SSE stream to retain local signature/expiry validation during a hub network outage. New requests still require authoritative authentication, and an existing stream closes when a hub check reports revocation. Serialize asynchronous authentication, replay and keepalives so concurrent events retain cursor order and backpressure.
134. Keep rigging application receipts in device-local versioned files. Save migrated receipts before removing old shared rows, preserving divergent copies. Refetch configuration responses older than the highest materialized revision; model discovery refreshes the authority before materializing its result. This does not add an offline configuration authority or periodic synchronization.
135. Stop owned local execution during daemon shutdown even if the hub is unreachable. Retain unsettled work and index delivery for recovery, release local services and the data-home lock, and preserve real process-cleanup errors. Restart opens history without automatically launching another step.

136. Report presence immediately after recovery and every thirty seconds, coalescing overlapping pulses and draining the current pulse on shutdown. Use read-only Git status without fetch; ahead/behind is relative to the locally known upstream. Delivery failure does not interrupt an executing runtime.
137. Keep external-session sensing metadata-only, bounded and independent of runtime attachment. Canonicalize paths, choose the most specific registered project, and exclude sources under isolated Jevellan runtime homes without excluding unrelated native sessions at the same cwd. Preserve first-record identity, original cached activity ages and successful-empty invalidation; unavailable sources make writing admission fail closed.
138. Run Cursor's selected SQLite metadata query in a read-only worker with a deadline so database access cannot block the daemon event loop. Never infer cwd from lossy directory slugs or carry native identifiers, titles or transcript text into heartbeat documents. Default native paths are supported; explicit test roots remain injectable.
139. Record the exact blocked manual choice in a versioned external-activity wait and bind Retry to its work and generation. Automatic Retry asks for a fresh decision. Observe newly active sessions during a running stretch and again at its closing boundary; retain a main-policy checkpoint block across quiet periods and restart until Changes review accepts the current fingerprint. Never stop the owned runtime because an outside journal becomes active.
140. Check external activity around ordinary publication coordination before pushing. Keep the remaining context, memory, undo/discard and external-policy acknowledgement integration explicitly unfinished until exercised; the current checkpoint does not claim those boundaries or all of §11.1.

141. Await resource checks at actual Git mutation boundaries, including after ownership and recovery-ref waits. Revalidate reviewed content after asynchronous checks before moving main, so a stale diff cannot be accepted because a guard or authority lookup yielded.
142. Give context changes their own versioned review response and durable prepared checkpoint. If external activity appears after files are applied, preserve those files and refuse plain Retry or manual conversation actions until the complete current diff is explicitly accepted. Bind acceptance to operation, generation, project settings, canonical checkout path, Git state and content. Reconcile a lost successful result on restart and refresh the resulting tracked-file fingerprint. Kept work can reopen for review; a Finished outside Jevellan outcome cannot.
143. Check outside activity for memory preparation and mutations as well as queued-write admission. Latch overlap through queue completion and preserve partial notes behind the existing Changes barrier. After review, title lookup completes the existing proposal rather than duplicating a note. Never treat an expired activity window as acceptance of partial or outside changes.
144. Preserve undo/discard intent and recovery refs when activity prevents the actual mutation. Await checks before reset/revert and again after coordination waits; publish reverted work through the same fresh activity barrier as ordinary publication. Existing explicit retries remain the continuation mechanism.
145. Use a versioned acknowledgement mode in the existing Changes review for external Git policy. It clears only the reviewed activity blocks and records the user's acknowledgement without a Jevellan checkpoint, reset or push. The UI explains this mode; deferred memory writes still require current ownership and quiet activity.
146. Require a clean main-policy working tree immediately before ordinary writing launch, even after a formerly blocked journal goes quiet. Preserve integration's explicit conflict-working-tree exception. Verify unexplained history drift separately before treating all checkout-boundary work as complete.

147. Reconstruct the expected checkout HEAD from the owning work's immutable base and existing versioned checkpoint, rewrite, undo, context and settlement evidence. A clean tree, quiet journal or matching remote is not proof that an unrecorded commit belongs to the work. Refuse later writing, publication and discard while preserving the checkout and reservation.
148. Keep completed-history checks separate from in-progress integration checks. Recover integration receipts before replaying their Git boundary and check again after publication coordination, immediately before pushing. Validated published undo may retain unrelated newer commits; its recorded result becomes the new boundary.
149. Recover a lost successful discard result only when main is clean at the recorded base and the exact conversation-scoped discard ref names the expected prior HEAD. Use the existing requested/blocked settlement and read-only Git evidence; do not invent ownership from the current checkout or perform the discard twice.

150. Use one explicit conversation route table and dispatcher for local and owner-relayed requests. Resolve only an indexed owner and its registered URL; the receiving peer serves its own local history without another proxy hop. Creation remains local, and opening foreign history never creates a second conversation directory or launches work.
151. Keep ordinary UI cookies bound to their issuing device. Explicit owner requests carry the authenticated source session; the receiving member uses its own hub bearer to verify that session, both memberships and the conversation owner. New requests require authority; only an already admitted stream can use the existing signed-session outage rule until the hub returns.
152. Relay bytes with backpressure, preserve conflict status and replay cursors, refuse redirects and Set-Cookie, and release readers/listeners on caller disconnect. Keep the reference's twenty-second ordinary deadline and 125-second SSE connection deadline; release the latter when live headers arrive instead of imposing a lifetime on the stream.
153. Retain hub-outage continuations only in the daemon that accepted the work. A successful heartbeat wakes explicit safe boundaries; only the mesh client's typed hub-unavailable error permits that retry. Store versioned boundary evidence without credentials or executable continuations. Daemon restart interrupts the wait and requires user continuation.
154. Retry a post-runtime checkpoint without repeating its native step. Bind the deferred write to the completed files and Git snapshot, and recheck outside activity and existing Changes barriers before committing. Preserve changed files for review if another actor edits them during the wait.
155. Re-read authority during launch and automatic-decision recovery. A prepared automatic choice is reconsidered after a prelaunch outage; the explicit manual choice remains subject to its original generation. Post-runtime account reporting and settings retries retain the already recorded native outcome.
156. Continue queued memory at the ownership check that failed, retaining the current queue's note results. Do not replay an already completed write. Bind resumed memory mutation and checkpointing to the files and Git snapshot from before that wait; outside edits require Changes review.
157. Keep the original publication-renewal failure typed. A lost authority check ends its lease attempt; after reconnection, settle that exact token and obtain fresh authority. Continue lost successful releases separately from the push, so cleanup cannot repeat publication.
158. Recover a lost lease acquisition reply only for the same authenticated device and work while its lease is valid. Permit repeated release of the same token, including expired cleanup, while refusing to release a newer token or a different owner. This relies on the existing exclusive owner daemon and one operation per work.
159. Allow a cleanup wait to name already-closed work explicitly. Its evidence and eventual release belong to that work; neither a heartbeat nor restart reopens it or schedules a native step.
160. Wait at an interrupted integration's abort before retrying publication. Fence that abort with local files, refs, the index, original HEAD and the complete rebase metadata; do not contact the remote merely to compare this local boundary. Recheck ownership, outside activity and the fingerprint immediately before cleanup, preserving any intervening edit.
161. After the abort settles, discard the previous publication lease attempt and continue through existing rewrite reconciliation with fresh authority. A conflict fallback must check that authority again before starting its integration stretch. A restarted daemon retains the interrupted checkout and never recreates the old wait's continuation.
162. Keep an admitted settlement's hub waits bound to its work and generation, including a previously closed work with retained changes. Recheck checkout authority on reconnection; cancellation and restart do not recreate the in-memory continuation.
163. Resume an interrupted discard using only its exact recorded recovery ref and expected checkpoint. Refuse changed files, history or saved refs, and recheck the ref immediately before resetting. Wait for release separately after closing so a lost response cannot repeat the Git operation or reopen the work.
164. Once a stopped writing runtime is gone, attempt to account for its finished files with one guarded local checkpoint, preserving the existing undo contract. Fresh authority and unchanged files are still required. Do not enter a hub wait during cancellation; an unavailable hub leaves a review barrier, and a continuation already waiting remains interrupted.
165. Record cancellation's local closed-work outcome immediately after confirmed termination, before fetching project metadata or releasing authority. An offline hub can leave that cleanup pending, but it cannot leave the terminated work logically open. This does not bypass ownership checks or complete the remaining UI/request-retry work.
166. Wait separately at undo preparation and application. Retain the exact recorded plan during automatic recovery, recognize an already-applied result through the existing Git checks, and keep explicit manual reconciliation/replanning available for a blocked operation. No failed hub read should replay the whole undo-and-redo pipeline.
167. Bind undo waits to their work and generation, including a closed work that receives ownership from newer work. Reconcile that exact same-conversation transfer without releasing ownership. Once undo changes the conversation generation, use the new generation for the guard read before redo; cancellation or restart never reconstructs the continuation.
168. Keep cancellation's confirmed local outcome separate from hub-dependent metadata cleanup. Try cleanup once; a typed hub outage transfers only that cleanup into the conversation's owned operation slot. Closed history has no next-action menu and needs no shared settings/project read to acknowledge its outcome. Authentication of a new UI request remains a separate hub boundary.
169. Reconcile cancellation release against the exact closed work and conversation. Freshly retained ownership requires clean files, no pending undo and the unchanged work base; a release already accepted by the hub may finish deleting its matching local lock after later file edits. Never release a newer owner. Repeated Cancel preserves this cleanup wait, while daemon shutdown aborts and drains it without recreating it on restart.
170. Bind admitted context waits to the original work and generation. Retry draft admission only before a stretch starts, retain the existing reviewed-draft boundary, and reconcile shared context metadata after a lost reply without applying the files again.
171. Record the applied checkout digest and a separate context-content fingerprint before waiting for shared metadata. Git staging may change tracked status without changing content; later outside edits still require review. Keep the existing full fingerprint as the fallback for older records.
172. Prepare and save context checkpoint plans before moving main, and retry their application separately. Explicit Changes acceptance uses the same prepared-plan continuation. Include HEAD paths in working-tree digests so staging a reviewed deletion cannot erase it from the comparison.
173. Leave unfinished context cleanup blocked after restart even when its work was already closed as Done. Permit explicit Retry of that exact last applied work, preserving its existing files and request. Never claim cleanup complete from the closed outcome alone or reconstruct a continuation automatically.

174. Return typed hub-unavailable errors with request-specific retry permission. Opt browser callers into waiting explicitly; generic HTTP errors and loss of the local response remain unknown outcomes, not permission to replay a mutation.
175. Commit configuration request receipts atomically with revisions. Scope each ID to its device and bind the validated input hash; return the original result after later revisions or restart while preserving ordinary stale-writer rejection.
176. Keep pending UI requests only in memory, freeze their serialized bodies, and cancel waiting on navigation or form unmount. Stop waiting does not undo an already admitted server request. Keep provider-login polling outside the ordinary repeatable GET classification.

177. Validate initial conversation choices against one settings/project read, then save their local records without further hub reads before scheduling. Share the initial action guard predicate with later decisions, and create the conversation object only after validation succeeds. Repeated creation must not restore consumed choices or reconstruct an interrupted older admission.

178. Preserve an admitted native login across typed hub failures during credential capture or readiness, within its original deadline. Keep the accepted code exchange single-use; cancellation ends pending capture. Do not treat an ordinary provider error as a hub outage.
179. Commit credential-capture receipts atomically with the credential. Bind the original device/account/revision and keyed input fingerprint, retain only metadata in the receipt, and reconcile a lost reply without rotating the credential or replacing newer account changes.
180. Bind browser login starts to local request receipts so a replay after restart cannot silently launch a new process. Put hub-wait controls inside modal dialogs and let cancellation run independently of a waiting submission. Browser unmount stops pending requests, not already admitted native work.

181. Route provider-login operations through a dedicated peer endpoint and verify the source browser session plus source/target membership at the hub. Preserve the returned login's device identity for polling, submission and cancellation; keep native authentication files on the target device.
182. Drive Settings and the header from the hub's roster, refreshing presence every thirty seconds. Use the existing expiring invitation and one-use switch grants; retain the current route when switching. Device availability controls remote login and Open, while automatic device placement remains disabled.

183. Synchronize shared configuration and Rigging at startup and every thirty seconds, using existing delivery fingerprints and file-preservation rules. Coalesce timer ticks and serialize explicit/periodic application. Record redacted local status and drain delivery before closing services; never restart an agent or daemon to apply settings.
184. Bind explicit loopback and Tailscale IPv4 listeners to one application and port. Clean up partial startup without touching existing listeners. Fresh-device advertisement uses the selected address; installed service/HTTPS setup and occupied-port selection remain installer responsibilities.

185. Refresh the isolated Basic Memory index from the checkout before searches, including read-only recall and the memory viewer. Git can update notes while a daemon remains running; cached registration must not hide those notes. Preserve imported file bytes, HEAD and clean status, and keep the index inside the isolated home. See [memory refresh evidence](phase4-memory-refresh.md).
186. Bind account and managed Rigging saves to a stable browser request ID and a versioned, transactional hub receipt. Fingerprint secret-bearing requests with the vault's keyed hash; store only public results. Return current values on replay so later changes survive, and abort waiting requests when their editor closes. A Settings key replacement uses its explicit revision and credential receipt without a provider-login deadline. See [Settings save recovery](phase4-settings-saves.md).
187. Freeze a project save's original input and local context inspection in the owning device's isolated home before submitting its receipt-backed hub write. Reconcile the same context operation after a lost reply or restart; skip starting superseded context work and return the current project. Jev key receipts preserve later credentials and contain only keyed request fingerprints and public summaries.
188. Record context operations before changing shared state; their existing owned execution records the resulting context and waits at hub boundaries. Local Rigging waits at account admission and reuses its existing move/promotion receipts. Stop waiting also skips the follow-up refresh. Invitation and switch-grant issuance may repeat, while consuming a one-use grant remains separate. See [remaining Settings boundaries](phase4-settings-boundaries.md).
189. Keep merge drafting read-only for both instruction files and shared project state. Generic stretch preparation must not advance the revision the context operation will compare at Apply. The operation records the resulting context after application; newer external project edits still invalidate the draft.
190. Bundle the CLI/web workspaces and their production dependency closure into the root distribution. Resolve command and web assets from the application's root so workspace and packed layouts behave consistently. An extracted-tarball check confirms CLI startup, HTTP/UI/assets and the memory-hook command outside the checkout. Source packaging remains separate from the installed versioned copy.
191. Put native service control behind an injected adapter, with strict versioned definitions, exact path/content checks and scoped user-service commands. Tests replace all native manager calls, and only use the macOS property-list parser for real format validation. The installer will own file creation/removal and manifest persistence. See [service adapter evidence](phase5-services.md).
192. Keep TypeScript's web modules in `apps/web/lib` and the browser bundle in `apps/web/dist`. Vite cleans only its bundle output. This prevents a production build from deleting the compiled web request module before backend tests run. The private web application has no library entry-point export.
193. Record pending application copies and service-file changes before touching their destinations. Compare complete application contents before reuse/removal, preserve changed or unlisted files, and keep older versions during preparation. A SQLite transaction serializes installers; native service control remains separate from file ownership. See [installation file evidence](phase5-files.md).
194. Pin dependency setup to the versions already exercised: APM 0.10.0 and Basic Memory 0.22.1, with managed Python 3.12 and uv 0.11.23. Reuse compatible existing APM/uv read-only; always give Basic Memory an owned environment. Bootstrap missing uv through a digest-checked archive, without shell/profile changes. Keep dependency environments versioned for rollback and record the install intent before downloads. See [dependency evidence](phase5-tools.md).
195. Use shared SQLite read transactions for admitted work and an exclusive transaction for an idle service switch. Track startup, mutating requests, conversation operations and Rigging synchronization; a separate activity document is informational. This closes the gap between an idle check and new work admission without a stale process timeout. The daemon never performs its own service switch.
196. Prepare local source with a build and npm pack, then extract into a manifest-listed distribution directory before copying the application. Record pending service switches, retain old versions and permit rollback after a failed new start. Native Git help may advertise `--[no-]merge-base`; accept either documented option form when checking required capabilities.
197. Purge requires the exact canonical home path. The planned removal first records a private sibling receipt and atomically renames the old home to an installation-specific removal directory. Cleanup then targets that old directory, so a concurrent fresh installation at the original path is preserved. The receipt supports cleanup even if interruption already removed the old home's manifest. Component checks cover exact-path confirmation, busy refusal, interrupted cleanup, a fresh replacement home and external-file preservation.

198. Run doctor version probes in a disposable isolated home, and query the already-running daemon through a private, rotated loopback control token. Do not open a second daemon or read native account logins. Report recorded account readiness explicitly; observations older than five minutes or with a last error need refreshing. Check Jev's configured model only when its key is saved. See [diagnostics evidence](phase5-doctor.md).

199. Connect both install --join and the standalone join command to the existing durable mesh registration before starting the service. Persist the target hub and chosen address without the invitation code, and reuse a prepared application for retries. Preserve membership on data-retaining uninstall, refuse hub conversion and never substitute a new hub for an unfinished join. The Devices command uses the GitHub distribution specified in §4.3; the npm example in §14 cannot be a working delivery path without separately authorized npm publication.

200. Install the command in a standard user bin folder: prefer `~/.local/bin` or `~/bin` already on PATH, otherwise use `~/.local/bin` and report its full path. Do not change shell profiles or system/package-manager directories. Bind the launcher to this installation and its selected version, record its write/removal ownership, preserve conflicting files, and report PATH shadowing accurately. See [command evidence](phase5-command.md).

201. Offer optional HTTPS at first installation and accept explicit `--https`/`--no-https` choices for scripted install/join. Register the final address before member startup, own a separate unused Tailscale port, retain its pending/active/removed receipt, and preserve any route that no longer matches. Trust forwarded HTTPS metadata only on loopback for the configured public origin. Existing HTTP members keep their registered address; this does not implement member address migration. See [HTTPS evidence](phase5-https.md).

202. Generate browser request and object identifiers from Web Crypto random bytes so the printed plain-HTTP Tailscale address supports first run. Keep UUID format, stable retry identities and existing schema validation. The first packed browser attempt exposed the secure-context-only `randomUUID` dependency; direct Chrome capability evidence and an identifier regression cover the correction. See [first-run evidence](phase5-first-run.md).

203. After creating the hub passphrase, open a four-step setup page that reuses the existing Jev connection, account, project and conversation controls. Keep its current step in the URL for reloads and offer it again from About; saved configuration remains authoritative, without a second onboarding database. A ready account on an enabled local runtime and an available local project gate progress. Skipping Jev leads to the normal manual picker. Connection checks return version 2 of the response document with measured elapsed milliseconds, displayed alongside the returned model aliases.
204. A newly saved project can still have an owned instruction-file operation running. Before creating a conversation from either composer, wait for that operation's busy flag to clear, including final ownership cleanup. Read the existing versioned context panel rather than maintaining a second readiness state. An unfinished stopped operation requires attention in Projects → Context; navigation cancels the wait. Keep the request in the composer until the setup check succeeds, and retain the normal owner guard against a later concurrent operation.
205. Coalesce conversation snapshot refreshes while an HTTP read is pending. Render each completed read and then catch up with queued stream notifications, instead of discarding a successful read merely because a later one has started. Leaving the page aborts the pending read and suppresses its late result. This addresses the observed initial-loading delay when stream replay and member reads overlap; it does not cache shared authorization or configuration.
206. Preserve missing dependency license and attribution texts under `docs/licenses/`, indexed from the root NOTICE. Copy the upstream tag matching the installed version without edits and record its source and checksum. Keep original package notices as well. The packing manifest includes the supplemental files and Codex's nested native notices, license texts and source inventory. The public delivery target is GitHub source; dependencies are fetched on the installing machine and retained with their original terms in private installation copies. No npm or binary release is published. Four actual packed installations verified preservation of 227 notice files, including these supplemental texts and native source metadata.


207. Add improver settings as configuration schema version 2, with an explicit version-1 migration. Reads normalize in memory; historical documents and acknowledged request receipts keep their original bytes. All three jobs and the nightly schedule default on at 03:00. Memory defaults to Apply and tell me; routing and context remain Suggest. A missing project override means selected, while an explicit false excludes it, so new projects are selected by default.
208. Use only applied explicit corrections for routing groups; include applied composer transitions to or from Auto as their recorded null values. Group across projects by the specified fields, retaining project/conversation links as evidence. Use record time for the fourteen-day window, include its exact boundary, and reject future records. Redo weighs two for grouping but does not count as two new corrections after dismissal.
209. Saved decision cases use fixed, synthetic account availability to isolate the guidance under test from local login and quota changes. A removed fixture model blocks evaluation. Compare all acceptable action/model/requested-effort fields together: better means failing to passing, worse means passing to failing, otherwise unchanged. Preserve both choices even when two acceptable alternatives differ. Tests with simulated Jev are labelled simulated; the manual command uses only the dedicated live key.
210. A nightly job identity consists of the hub-local date, job and project; manual requests use their own stable request identifier. Do not backfill earlier missed dates. Completed and skipped nightly jobs do not rerun; failures need an explicit retry. Claims have renewable two-minute leases, and expired/replaced workers lose authority. Native execution and checkout ownership remain separate requirements when orchestration is connected.


211. Save a routing edit, suggestion outcome and idempotent action receipt in one hub transaction. Configuration writes participate in the hub's existing nested savepoints. Preserve the original action result for retries even after later decisions. Apply and Undo compare only their field. Mark stale Apply for recomputation; do not overwrite it. Keep all outcomes, display recently decided items for seven days, and require three previously unseen correction IDs after any decision, including Apply or Undo. Union decided IDs so equal timestamps cannot resurrect old evidence.
212. Bind each saved-case comparison to checksums of the complete evaluated configuration, proposed configuration and case set. Freeze those configurations before asynchronous evaluation. Accept an actionable draft only with the exact comparison for its text and every expected case. An unrelated later setting does not invalidate field-specific Apply; the receipt continues to identify the snapshot actually evaluated.
213. Keep background draft requests and ordinary ledgers under the isolated home's `improver/` directory, with private input copies under `tmp/draft_<id>/`. Use a read-only reply stretch and the usual bridge, handoff repair, account ranking and native process controls. Expose no project-memory tools in these drafts. Retain failed/interrupted identities without silently relaunching them; completed identical retries reuse the result. Native identifiers stay in private runtime history, not public draft results.
214. Improver drafts request high effort, mapped to the first eligible model's supported efforts, with a timeout capped at five minutes and no longer than the configured stretch timeout. Share the account reservation with conversation execution when an adapter lacks per-launch isolation. Use the existing thirty-second cancellation handoff grace, and retain lifecycle activity when restart recovery cannot confirm process cleanup.
215. Routing job orchestration only creates suggestions. Renew its hub claim every thirty seconds, recheck ownership across asynchronous boundaries, and record judgment/draft/case-check/suggestion stages. Explicitly distinguish live from simulated provider evidence. The callable nightly entry point does not itself install a scheduler; daemon scheduling and the UI are connected in subsequent work.

216. Mark private input-copy launches explicitly in the runtime contract, permitting that optional flag only for read-only replies without memory writes. Codex then skips only the Git-repository existence check for those copies. Its sandbox, network and approval settings remain unchanged, and ordinary project launches omit the flag. Verify initial and continued turns through the actual SDK transport, not only a stubbed options object.
217. Accept improver revisions as durable asynchronous requests. Identical retries return the saved result; an interrupted provider request is recorded as failed and requires a new explicit request to retry. Commit the checked preview or recomputed suggestion and its completion receipt together, without changing configuration.
218. A stale routing Apply queues recomputation against the current field and requires a fresh explicit Apply afterwards. Direct edits run only the saved-case check; plain-language changes use one additional private read-only stretch and must retain the same target field.
219. Start hub-local nightly polling only after the daemon's bridge is listening. Share account reservations and lifecycle activity with ordinary conversations, cancel improver work before closing its dependencies, and route member requests to the single hub authority. Public job summaries omit worker claim tokens. Test-injected runtimes or judge transports label case evidence simulated.

220. Memory-care and context jobs are claimed per project and cycle by the device that has the checkout: the hub itself, otherwise the first online device by join date. Devices poll the hub over a device-authenticated protocol that browsers cannot call. Checkout changes (Apply, Undo, recompute) are hub tasks bound to that device; results from other devices are refused, and tasks interrupted by a restart fail visibly instead of resuming.
221. Memory-care thresholds: merge a pair at p ≥ 0.7, archive a stale note at p ≤ 0.3, and suggest a working rule at p ≥ 0.7. Code archives stale notes and computes all counts from the patch; the draft may only merge, reconcile and fix links in involved notes. Pairs are reconsidered only when a note changed since the last care run.
222. A failed memory-care or context publication is saved to a discard ref and discarded, so the improver never leaves a checkout owned by unpublished work. A daemon restart restores journaled patch text and discards unpublished improver commits before releasing ownership.
223. One memory-care suggestion per project waits at a time. Dismissed or decided project suggestions are suppressed while the involved notes' contents are unchanged. Morning-card Undo has no time limit; suggestion Undo keeps the 30-second window.
224. The trial log counts `done` conversations without an outside outcome in the week of their last update, and finished-elsewhere conversations in the week of that answer (UTC Monday weeks). The conversation index now carries the outcome. Conversations Jevellan starts for context operations carry `origin: 'context-operation'` and are excluded.
225. Skip the Basic Memory provider sync while the memory folder's names and contents match the last sync. Invalidate that record on every provider write, so a note written through the bridge and later removed by undo is still dropped from the index. Each sync is a full Python run of about six seconds of CPU on the build VM. Recall synced on every search, which made four memory integration tests exceed their 60–90 s limits there.
226. The improver browser journeys run on their own fixture servers (Playwright projects `{layout}-improver`, ports base+4 to base+7). They save a Jev key and run hub-wide jobs, which made the shared server's "automatic decisions resume from missing configuration" journey start with a key already saved and run automatically. Screenshots and vision evidence keep the plain layout name through project metadata.
227. The secret scanner has an exact-text allowlist, with one entry: the J4 live script's random value in the OAuth token shape, used to test invalid-token recovery. Each entry is removed before the patterns are checked, so a real token elsewhere in the same file still blocks the push; `tests/secret-scan.test.ts` covers both cases. Approved by the user on 2026-09-27.
228. Success messages close after eight seconds; errors stay until dismissed. Desktop messages sit below the header in the centre of the workspace, phone messages at the bottom. While a dialog is open, the message and the hub-wait notice render inside the dialog as an inline notice, because the dialog is in the top layer and the message concerns it.
229. Only a dialog's closing actions stick to its bottom; action rows nested in dialog content stay in place.
230. Vision evidence judges each screenshot by its own name as one step of its check, with finished animations and a hidden caret. Full-page captures temporarily grow the viewport to the document height rather than using a stretched capture, which draws sticky elements at viewport positions.
231. A graceful daemon stop is a restart, not a cancel (brief 7.5): running steps are stopped, marked interrupted with a code handoff, and the work waits for the user with the restart notice; ownership stays held and nothing relaunches. User Cancel is unchanged.
232.
