When an interrupted writing step (not Integrate) leaves the checkout dirty on main with HEAD unchanged and no rebase in progress, restart recovery records a checkpoint block, so "Send a message to continue" goes through Changes review and Accept instead of a dead end. A moved HEAD stays with the recorded receipts; Integrate stays with integration history recovery.
233. Improver Undo reverts only a commit the improver made (its subject prefix and exact file set). A change that leaves nothing to commit, for example an edit to a gitignored CLAUDE.md behind an AGENTS.md link, is recorded as applied on this device without a commit and is never published. A blocked improver publication discards back to the tracked upstream when it had rebased onto newer work, so only the improver's own commit is dropped and the checkout is always released.
234. Display headings keep the website's Bricolage Grotesque with a small word spacing, because its narrow space made sentence titles run together at small sizes. The manual picker's sentence heading uses the body font.
235. A user reply to a no-progress stop restarts the no-progress count, recorded on the message (`noProgressReset`) and applied in replay and undo, mirroring how a reply to a stop grants extra steps. Without it the unanswered-message rule (Done needs a completed answering step) and the no-progress guard re-pausing after that step left Close work as the only way out. The cost cap and the failing-tests guard can still loop the same way when the user wants to finish; Close work remains the exit there, and they are recorded as known limits.

Decisions 236 and later were taken during the Projects build (BRIEF-projects.md, local and untracked like BRIEF.md). The id in parentheses is the build design's, which code comments and test names cite (decision 392). Deliberate deviations from the brief: decisions 238, 244, 250, 255, 299, 448, 488 and 564.

236. (D1) Projects defines every brief section 5 document and every project bridge tool schema in phase 1, as versioned zod schemas in `packages/core`; later phases add handlers, views and hub operations only.
237. (D2) Project ledgers use a generic `JsonlLedger` base extracted from `ConversationLedger`, with a parallel `project-ledger-event-v1` envelope; blob documents keep the internal `conversation-blob-v1` literal, so conversation ledger files stay byte-identical.
238. (D2a) Deliberate deviation from brief 5.13 ("an event delivered in a turn"): a `coordinator-event` record is appended when the event is received, so the chat can show owner messages and thread events at once. `coordinator-turn-start` lists the delivered event ids, and the chat marks an event delivered by joining on them.
239. (D3) The `revision` field inside every Projects hub document equals the hub row revision (written as the expected revision plus one and checked on every read), so a rewritten revision is detected.
240. (D4) Owner-local sidecars `thread-local.json` and `coordinator-local.json` hold process identity, the last pushed commit, the git identity cache, pull request notification memory and fallback event ids, so the brief's thread and coordinator schemas stay verbatim.
241. (D6) The coordinator is assigned to the receiving device on the first coordinator message or the first owner-created thread, before any event exists.
242. (D7) Members reach Projects hub state through one POST route per collection, `/hub/mesh/projects/<collection>`, with operation-discriminated bodies; the collection list already includes the later `envelopes` and `checkouts` collections.
243. (D8) Placement candidates are devices whose presence is `online` (stale devices are excluded), except that the placing device always counts as online because it is running; remote project paths are checked only from `project.paths` and `allowedDevices`.
244. (D9) Deliberate deviation from brief 8.2 and 10, which say "running" without defining it: running limits count live work only (`preparing`, `running`, `publishing` and pending dispatches), so idle, waiting, in-review, queued and concluded threads never block the queue. Every turn that starts from a non-live state, including an in-review fix turn, passes the same admission and otherwise waits with a `Queued:` or `Waiting for a free slot:` reason; remote counts come from hub indexes, and the last good read is reused while the hub is unreachable.
245. (D9a) Thread starts run on the coordinator device: a thread over a running limit is placed ignoring the limits, created as `queued` and dispatched first in, first out by the coordinator's sweep (device-limited threads skipped, stopping at the project limit). Consequence: while the coordinator device is offline no thread can start.
246. (D10) Thread indexes are ordered by a hub cursor (`project-thread-cursor-v1`) keyed to the thread ledger's last event id, and every index-visible change appends a ledger event.
247. (D11) Project scopes share the existing bridge registry, token rules, environment names and `/api/bridge`; tool inputs are bare objects, outputs are versioned `*-result-v1` documents, and validation errors become field sentences.
248. (D12) `RuntimeAdapter.startTurn` and `capabilities.turns` are required members; every adapter and test literal changed together.
249. (D13) Turn coverage uses a `checkTurnResume` helper and the PJ1e tests; the existing seven-entry runtime contract matrix is unchanged.
250. (D14) Deliberate deviation from brief 8.3 (`git fetch origin <base>` and an `origin/<base>` start point): worktree threads fetch their base into the private ref `refs/jevellan/threads/<id>/base` with `--refmap=`, `--no-tags` (decision 346) and `--no-write-fetch-head`, and conversation snapshots and rebase fingerprints ignore `refs/heads/jv/`, `refs/remotes/origin/jv/` and `refs/jevellan/threads/`, so Jevellan's own thread operations never disturb a running conversation step (brief 1). An agent's own `git fetch` inside its worktree still moves `origin/<base>` and counts as outside activity, like any manual fetch.
251. (D15) Thread turns receive `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*` from the project checkout's git configuration, read with the daemon's own HOME (the launch allowlist is extended for turns); a missing identity omits them.
252. (D16) A thread's native session stays on its account; when that account is no longer eligible, Jevellan ranks another account of the same runtime, starts a fresh native session and records a notice.
253. (D17) Codex write turns in a linked worktree get the common git directory's `objects`, `refs` and `logs` folders and the worktree's own git directory as additional writable directories, never the common directory root, so the owner checkout's `index`, `HEAD` and `config` stay outside the sandbox. Live Codex behavior is unverified; publication commits leftovers either way.
254. (D18) GitHub detection reads the raw `remote.origin.url` (before `insteadOf`); tests redirect a GitHub URL to a local bare origin with `insteadOf`.
255. (D19) Deliberate deviation from brief 7.2 ("a second call is refused"): a second `jevellan_thread_report` in one turn is refused with `This turn already reported. Put everything in one report.` unless it is an identical retry, which returns the original receipt with `repeated: true`. MCP transports retry calls whose reply was lost, and refusing that retry would turn a delivered report into an error the agent cannot act on.
256. (D20) Claude turn text gets a blank-line separator after tool results, and the synthesized summary and coordinator text use the text after the last tool call, else all text.
257. (D21) Codex reads the root `AGENTS.md` only for the first turn of a session, and the first-turn prompt has no stretch header.
258. (D22) A later turn with one queued message sends its text as is; several messages are each prefixed `From the coordinator:` or `From the owner:` on their own line and joined by a `---` line.
259. (D23) When no pull request can open (no remote, no token, a remote that is not on GitHub, or a git or GitHub error), the thread goes `idle` with the reason and the coordinator receives a synthesized `blocked` report carrying it.
260. (D24) Daemon shutdown never rewrites thread states; startup recovery is the single path, and an orphan process whose cleanup cannot be confirmed marks the thread `failed`.
261. (D25) Thread branches are `jv/<slug>-<last 6 id characters, lowercased>`; an empty slug becomes `thread`.
262. (D26) Untracked `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` links are mirrored into a worktree only when `git check-ignore` confirms they are excluded there; otherwise a notice is recorded.
263. (D27) The first push uses a create-only lease (`--force-with-lease=refs/heads/<branch>:`); later pushes lease against the last pushed commit.
264. (D28) A stop from the owner tells the coordinator with `thread-interrupted(stopped)`; a stop the coordinator asked for sends no event.
265. (D31) The turn-limit decision item is recognized by `from: 'thread'` plus its exact two option labels, and its options act (allow more turns or stop) instead of messaging the thread.
266. (D35) Owner-started `thread-user-message` texts (and, from phase 7, terminal-work ones) are stored preformatted and emitted verbatim in event blocks. Terminal-work lines are built in Projects phase 7 (decision 537).
267. (D37) Placement checks runtime capabilities with this daemon's adapters for every device.
268. (D38) GitHub responses are parsed with loose zod schemas named after REST API version 2022-11-28.
269. (D39) Thread turns, preparation and publication hold lifecycle activities of kind `conversation` with the thread or project id, so lifecycle idle checks count them as running work.
270. (D48) Project integration tests share one fixture, `tests/helpers/project-fixture.ts`.
271. (D49) Verification output tails are the last 4,000 characters of redacted stdout followed by stderr.
272. (D52) Answered questions stay in the hub's question list for 14 days, and the waiting list shows open items plus the 10 most recently answered; the 14-day concluded thread list arrives with the interface.
273. (D57) The GitHub token secret has `DELETE /hub/secrets/github` and a masked summary with the last four characters and `updatedAt`; the Jev key secret is unchanged.
274. (D58) Pull request lists include branch-only threads whose reason is one of the two branch-only texts (no token, not on GitHub).
275. (D60) Native transcripts are read in a worker thread with a 12-second timeout, never on the daemon event loop, cached by file, size and modification time with one read in flight per thread; an 8 MiB parse every 1.5 s per open thread page would otherwise stall streams, bridge calls and API requests.
276. (D61) Thread views embed the native transcript with its session `cwd` always null, so no worktree path leaves the owner device.
277. (D62) The coordinator safety texts are `Jevellan handles this. Ask a thread to do it with jevellan_thread_message.` and `The coordinator is read-only. Use the read tools and the Jevellan tools.`.
278. (D63) A Codex coordinator keeps a read-only shell under the coordinator safety profile, which keeps every stretch denial.
279. (D66) A failed worktree setup marks the thread `failed` and tells the coordinator with `thread-interrupted(failed)`.
280. (D67) A thread interrupted during `preparing` re-runs its idempotent preparation before its next turn.
281. (D68) `pr-update` events fire at most once per head commit for checks and for conflicts.
282. (D69) A pull request closed without merging marks the thread `stopped` with `The pull request was closed without merging.` and removes its worktree.
283. (D71) Only invalid fixed fields and the absence of any candidate refuse a start; running limits queue it.
284. (D72) After a `progress` or `blocked` report with queued messages, the next turn starts and the report is not sent (brief 8.2 read literally), except at the turn limit, where no next turn runs and the report is sent (decision 285).
285. (D73) The turn limit is checked at the end of every turn: a `progress`, `blocked` or `needs-decision` report at the limit moves the thread to `waiting-for-you` with `This thread reached its turn limit.`, creates the decision item at once and still sends the report to the coordinator. A `done` report at the limit still publishes; a check before every turn catches the fix turns publication would need, Allow 10 more turns then re-runs the publication, and a message blocked by the limit is answered `delivery: 'queued'`.
286. (D75) Merge is refused by the server when the pull request has conflicts or failing checks, mirroring the disabled button.
287. (D78) Owner-created thread starts are idempotent by `clientRequestId`, with receipts kept on the owner device.
288. (D81) `canMessage` means the composer is enabled: false for done, stopped and failed threads (and for attached threads from phase 7). Messages sent while a thread is queued, preparing, running or publishing wait for its next turn. Attach is built in Projects phase 7 (decision 536).
289. (D82) Until Jev placement exists (phases 1 to 3), non-fixed placements record `source: 'fallback'` with the error `{ kind: 'not-enabled', message: 'Jev placement is not enabled yet.' }`. Superseded in Projects phase 4 by decision 481: Jev places threads, and a device without a Jev key records `no-key`.
290. (D83) `{runtime}` in the pull request body, one-liners and placement lines is the runtime adapter's display name (for example `Codex`), as in the brief's own example.
291. (D85) When Jevellan itself unblocks a thread (Allow 10 more turns), it withdraws the thread's turn-limit item silently.
292. (D86) The leftover-commit subject is `<title>: <first line of the summary, cut to 72 characters>`; the brief's limit applies to the summary line.
293. (D87) Jevellan does not compare the owner checkout before and after each worktree-thread turn, because a conversation step or the owner's own work on that checkout during a turn (allowed by decision 250) would fail the thread falsely. Protection is the worktree working directory and system append, the narrowed Codex sandbox (decision 253), and the PJ1 assertion that HEAD, `main`, `origin/main`, status and the index digest are unchanged.
294. (D88) Placement is phase-gated: until main isolation exists only `worktree` is offered (a fixed `main` is refused with `Main isolation is not available yet.` and a `main` default is coerced), and until remote threads exist only this device is a candidate (a fixed other device is refused with `Threads run only on this device for now.`). On external-policy projects the brief's Leave git text wins over the phase text. The device gate opened in Projects phase 5 (decision 496). The main gate opened in Projects phase 6 (decision 525).
295. (D89) Per-project hub lists query by `projectId` in SQL with `HubDatabase.listByField` instead of parsing a whole namespace. Since phase 5 pending envelopes are selected by target device the same way (decision 492).
296. (D91) The coordinator chip shows the current session, or, before the first turn and after Fresh, the planned model and effective effort from the settings and the brief 5.1 null rule.
297. (D92) A project's paths and Git policy cannot change while it has a thread that is not done, stopped or failed; other project settings stay editable, so in-review threads do not lock settings for days.
298. (D94) Whenever a thread turn starts without resuming a session (a restart during setup, a failed first turn or an account change), the prompt starts with the task block, then `---`, then the messages.
299. (D95) Deliberate deviation from brief 8.5, which polls in-review threads: pull request polling covers every local thread whose pull request is open, whatever its state, so merges and closes during a fix turn are not missed. Since Projects phase 7 a merge or close of an attached thread is applied only after detach (decision 536).
300. (D97) An unavailable coordinator says `No enabled model can run the coordinator on {device}.` when no menu entry can run read-only turns there, and the brief's `No account can run the coordinator model on {device}.` when models qualify but no account does.
301. (D98) The phase 1 hub protocol has sixteen operations (settings get and put; coordinator get and assign; coordinator status get and put; thread list, get and publish; question list, get, create, withdraw and answer; notebook get and put), each mapped to one collection route. Single-record results are `{ revision, document }` refined so the document revision equals the row revision (decision 239), writes return the stored record, and thread and question lists are pages of 100 with `next`.
302. (D99) The `coordinator-tool-v1` ledger payload types `tool` as an id string in core rather than the closed bridge tool enum, because the bridge schemas import report, option and isolation schemas from the project schemas and the reverse import would be an ESM cycle; the project ledger writer checks the name against the tool enum.
303. (D100) Core exports the project ledger payload schemas by event type and `parseProjectLedgerData(type, data)`, so the owner's ledger writer and the browser validate payloads identically.
304. (D101) `project-work-settings-request-v1` carries an optional `clientRequestId`, so a retried settings save reaches the hub's idempotent receipt.
305. (D102) The phase 5 envelope, thread command and outbox documents and the phase 7 attach and detach documents are defined in phase 1 with the other schemas (decision 236); the hub protocol and `ProjectHub` carry only phase 1 operations, and later phases add theirs. Projects phase 7 uses the attach and detach documents (decisions 536 and 537).
306. (D103) Branch slugs fold accented letters to ASCII (Unicode NFKD with combining marks removed) before other characters become `-`, so `Café menu` gives `cafe-menu`; titles without Latin letters or digits give `thread` (decision 261).
307. (D104) Project scope tool names live in core `bridge-schemas.ts` from the start, because the Claude coordinator allow list needs them before the tool schemas exist; the four mail and reservation tools are filtered out through one set until main isolation exists. The filter was removed in Projects phase 6 (decision 524).
308. (D105) A turn worker's factory error is reported as a classified failure, while the stretch start path keeps its generic `Invalid runtime command or state.`, so stretch behavior stays byte-identical. A worker that received `start-turn` refuses a later `start` or `continue`.
309. (D106) The Claude turn separator (decision 256) is inserted only when text was already emitted before the tool call, so a turn that opens with a tool has no leading blank line; stretches never get it.
310. (D107) FakeRuntime turns match their script when the turn starts: an unmatched turn settles `failed` with `No scripted turn remains.`, and a throwing step uses the stretch text `The scripted runtime failed.`. Its native files name bridge calls the way Claude and Codex do, mark failed calls, write the assistant row on every settle and start a new journal when a resumed session's journal is missing.
311. (D108) The Codex safety hook accepts a context without an action: with neither action nor profile it behaves as a stretch (every stretch denial, rebase denied), and its fail-closed path writes the profile's reason once the context parsed.
312. (D109) `nativeTranscriptAt` finds a session by comparing names, never by joining the id into a path (Claude `<root>/projects/<dir>/<id>.jsonl` except `agent-*` files, Codex the rollout whose `session_meta` id matches). A file hint is used only when it lies inside the root's journal layout and holds that session, the newest of several journals wins, and the worker request restricts session ids to `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,199}`.
313. (D110) The transcript worker request `native-transcript-request-v1` is a union of `read` and `sessions` operations, and the session list is wrapped as `native-sessions-v1`. Only the 404 keeps its text across the worker; every other failure, including file system errors that carry account-home paths, becomes `The thread transcript could not be read on this device.`, and a malformed request becomes `Invalid thread transcript request.`.
314. (D111) A thread transcript's activity state comes from the last 512 Ki characters of the same 8 MiB read used for conversations, and its last activity time is the journal's modification time. Session listing reads only each journal's 256 KiB head, skips unreadable journals and keeps the 20,000-entry scan budget.
315. (D112) The transcript worker's stderr is drained rather than forwarded, because every worker isolate would otherwise print Node's SQLite experimental warning again.
316. (D113) The thread-ref regression test advances origin `main` before the conversation step's first snapshot, because a push to origin `main` during a step is outside activity whatever the ref filter does; it still proves `--refmap=`, since the checkout's `origin/main` stays behind origin. `threadRefLine` is exported from core `git.ts`, and the filtered ref text is byte-identical when no thread ref exists, so snapshots and checkpoint plans recorded earlier still compare equal.
317. (D114) `GitHubClient` times out after 15 seconds over headers and body with `setTimeout` and an `AbortController` (which fake timers can drive), caps success bodies at 4 MiB and error bodies at 4 KiB, and reports GitHub's `message` redacted, whitespace-collapsed and cut to 300 characters (else `GitHub answered with HTTP {status}.`). Statuses map to error kinds (401 and 403 auth, 404 not found, 405 not mergeable, 409 conflict, 422 unprocessable), redirects, timeouts, network errors and unexpected bodies have fixed sentences, and a token with characters outside printable ASCII fails with `The saved GitHub token is not valid.` before any request.
318. (D115) Check runs and commit statuses are read 100 per page, following pages up to `total_count` and at most 10 pages each; list items carry no merge fields on GitHub, so `findOpenPull` falls back to `merged_at` and leaves the merge state null. `parseGitHubRemote` accepts https, scp-like and ssh URLs on github.com only, keeps owner and repository case and refuses credentials, other hosts and extra path segments; the conversation publication key keeps its own rule.
319. (D116) The fake GitHub server goes beyond the brief where tests need it: a `/_redirect/` prefix that answers 302, custom check run and status lists, an optional repository on control helpers, whole failure bodies, list items without merge fields, 422 for a missing head or base branch, checks answered from the open pull request with that head, and merges that mark the pull request merged with a fake sha without changing the bare origin.
320. (D117) `JsonlLedger.lastId()` replays the ledger and `append` keeps its full replay; an in-memory tail cache was deferred, because a cache keyed on the last segment would let a handle append after an earlier segment was corrupted, which today stops every conversation append. Project ledgers stay small by appending only aggregated coordinator text, one-line tool summaries and lifecycle events.
321. (D118) The `JsonlLedger` constructor refuses a relative or aliased directory with `{label} directories cannot alias another location.`, while `ConversationLedger` keeps its own earlier check, so conversation errors are unchanged. Error texts take the ledger's label (for example `Invalid project blob reference.`), the base reads `ledger/<id>` and `blobs/<sha256>` pointers, and only the conversation ledger adds `handoffs/<n>`.
322. (D119) Project tool inputs fill their documented defaults in the schema (`include: 'active'`, `detail: 'summary'`, `interrupt: false`, `minutes: 60` and an empty reservation reason), so the model sees them and handlers never default again. `jevellan_ask_user` refuses one option or more than four with `Give no options or two to four options.`, `thread-start-result-v1` carries the optional queued `stateReason`, and every project tool maps to its result schema.
323. (D120) With the project tools in the closed bridge tool enum, a stretch token that calls a project tool passes request validation and is refused by the stretch list guard with 403 `This step cannot use that tool.`; stretch lists, descriptions and input schemas are unchanged, and project tool descriptions never use the conversation vocabulary.
324. (D121) `HubDatabase.listByField(namespace, field, value, schema, { limit?, after? })` is one SQL statement over `json_extract` with an exclusive id cursor. Only top-level field names matching `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*$` and positive safe-integer limits are accepted, and the namespace and cursor are id-checked like `get` and `list`.
325. (D122) The phase 1 hub namespaces are `project-work-settings`, `project-coordinators`, `project-coordinator-status`, `project-threads`, `project-thread-cursors`, `project-decisions` and `project-notebooks`. Settings, coordinator, notebook and question writes refuse an unknown project with 404 `Project not found.`; `thread-publish` does not, because the index publisher retries until it succeeds and projects are never deleted.
326. (D123) `coordinator-assign` only assigns the calling device (403 `A device can only make itself the coordinator.`), with `assignedAt` from the hub clock. `coordinator-status-put` requires both the status device and the current assignment to be the caller and writes at the current row revision.
327. (D124) `thread-publish` identifies an index by the sha256 of its stable JSON with the revision zeroed, because the hub sets the revision (decision 239), and the same digest decides whether a newer event needs a write. Cursors live in `project-thread-cursors`, an older event returns the stored cursor, and ownership refusals are 403 with fixed texts.
328. (D125) Hub lists filter before paging: thread lists keep every index of the project, and question lists keep open questions plus those answered in the last 14 days (decision 272). Pages hold 100 records with `next` set to the last returned id (null at the end), and members check every page (ascending ids past the cursor, the right project, a non-empty page that ends on `next`).
329. (D126) `decision-create` refuses a new question that is already answered or withdrawn and returns the stored record for an identical retry; withdrawing or answering an unknown id is 404 `This question was not found.`, and withdrawing an answered or withdrawn question returns it unchanged. Answers go through the settings-mutation receipts with `{ id, answer }`: `repeated` is false only for the call that wrote the answer, and reusing a request id for a different answer gets the receipt's 409.
330. (D127) The GitHub token secret mirrors the Jev key: `github-put` and `github-remove` go through settings-mutation receipts and return the current state, so a retried save after a removal elsewhere answers `saved: false`. `DELETE /hub/secrets/github` is retryable during a hub outage because removal is idempotent; a waiting removal can delete a token another device saved meanwhile, which is accepted.
331. (D128) `/hub/mesh/projects/<collection>` accepts POST only: an unknown collection is 404 `Not found.` and an operation of another collection is 400 `This operation belongs to another project collection.`, which the member client also refuses before sending. The route builds the hub project store per request with the hub clock and replies through the redacting sender.
332. (D129) The path and Git policy guard (decision 297) compares paths with stable JSON (key order ignored) and the branch policy by value, inside the existing settings mutation after the unchanged claim guard, and treats `done`, `stopped` and `failed` as closed. The hub does not refuse known secret shapes in work settings or notebook content, because the redactor's `Bearer` pattern would refuse ordinary notebook text and replies are redacted anyway.
333. (D130) Placement input adds the placing device id, the phase gates (`mainIsolation`, `remoteDevices`) as inputs, so the decisions package never changes for a gate, and runtime display names for account reasons such as `Mac mini: Codex needs login`; candidate computation enforces decision 243 itself.
334. (D131) Placement candidates expose the union of model options over the allowed isolations, with main narrowed to devices that pass the main rule; each model carries its candidate devices with an eligible account and, with reasons, the devices without one. Every menu entry and every roster device is in exactly one of eligible or excluded.
335. (D132) Models are excluded in this order: `not chosen`, a disabled entry (its unavailable reason when present, else `disabled in Settings`), `runtime disabled`, `runtime cannot run threads here`, then per device the best-ranked account's reason, joined as `{device}: {runtime} {reason}`. Devices are excluded in this order: `not chosen`, `not available until remote threads exist`, `offline`, `not set up for this project`, `at its running limit ({n})`, then the two main-checkout reasons.
336. (D133) Running limits never refuse a start (decision 283): when excluding full devices leaves no device and model but ignoring limits leaves one, candidates are computed without limits with `atLimit: true` and the caller queues the thread with the queued sentence. Refusals are built from the evaluation without limits, so they name lasting reasons (login, offline) instead of a passing limit.
337. (D134) Fixed placement fields are checked first, in this order: unknown model `Choose a model from the configuration.`, unknown device `Choose a registered device.`, the Leave git text, `Main isolation is not available yet.`, `Threads run only on this device for now.`. Without candidates the refusal is `No device can run any enabled model: ` plus deduplicated per-device reasons in roster order (phase-gated and unchosen devices left out), `No enabled model can run threads.` when no menu entry can run threads, and for a fixed model `{label} cannot run threads: {reason}.` or `No device can run {label}: {reasons}.`. The main gate opened in Projects phase 6 (decision 525).
338. (D135) Placement copy lives in `packages/decisions/src/placement.ts` as exported constants, because the decisions package sits below `packages/projects`; the Projects copy module re-exports them and never retypes them.
339. (D136) `placementFallback` picks the project's default isolation when allowed (else worktree), the first model of that isolation in menu order, the coordinator device when it has an eligible account (else the fewest running, then name, then id) and the first eligible ranked account; a fixed effort is mapped too. The record lists the chosen isolation's model options and every other roster device as excluded, cuts the error to 300 characters, and records `source: 'fixed'` without an error when every field was fixed.
340. (D137) `approximateTokens(state)` is exported from the decisions state module with the identical formula and the decision state builder uses it; the same-named estimator in the conversations package stays.
341. (D138) Thread index labels (the menu label at placement and the latest turn's account label) live in an optional `labels` field of `thread-local-v1`, because the brief's placement record has no label to carry. The index takes `updatedAt` from the ledger event it is published under, so one event id always yields byte-identical content, which the hub requires (decision 327).
342. (D139) A thread store update appends its explicit event and then a `thread-state` when the state or its reason changed; other index-field changes append a `thread-state` listing them, and owner-local-only changes publish nothing. A hub 409 for an event id makes the publisher ask for a fresh `thread-state` and publish under the new id, other 4xx answers except 401, 408 and 429 drop that update so one thread cannot hold back the others, and startup reconciliation repairs a missing state event.
343. (D140) The fresh-context recap separates items with a blank line, always keeps the newest item (cut to the 8,000-character budget when it alone is longer) and reads `(none)` when empty; active-thread and open-question lines are flattened to one line.
344. (D141) `packages/projects/src/copy.ts` holds every brief 9.8 notice as the reference text; the offline notice and the fallback chip will be rendered by the browser from its own copy module in phase 3, whose copy test should assert that both modules agree.
345. (D142) Texts the brief leaves open: the turn-limit question `"{title}" reached its turn limit of {n} turns. Allow 10 more turns or stop it?`, the merge refusals `This pull request has conflicts. Ask the thread to resolve them first.` and `Checks are failing. Ask the thread to fix them first.`, and `This thread was not found.`. Also context notes for instruction files a worktree lacks, `Worktree setup failed: the command timed out after {n} minutes.` (the whole reason cut to 400 characters) and `Git {command} did not finish in time.`.
346. (D143) The private base fetch also passes `--no-tags`: with a destination in the refspec git follows tags that point into the fetched history (reproduced with Git 2.55), and conversation snapshots digest `refs/tags`, so a followed tag would fail a running conversation step. The regression test creates the worktree through `ThreadWorktree` with a tag on origin and fails when either `--refmap=` or `--no-tags` is dropped.
347. (D144) `ThreadWorktree.create` takes the base from the private base ref (else the stored base commit), never from HEAD, and fetches only when neither exists; it reuses a registered worktree on the thread branch, adds a branch left without a worktree, recreates a vanished or unregistered folder and refuses a worktree on another branch. `remove` is idempotent (`worktree remove --force` or `worktree prune`, then `branch -D` for `jv/` branches only and the thread's private refs) and never touches the remote.
348. (D145) Thread transcripts are read from the account home of the thread's current placement without creating it, located once and then read through the file hint, and cached per thread by file, size and modification time with one read in flight; failures are never cached. Ledger helpers drop every `nativeSessionId` key from public data as defense in depth, recovery clears abandoned locks on every project ledger, and output tails put a newline between stdout and stderr when stdout lacks one (decision 271).
349. (D146) The turn launcher refuses a thread with no eligible account with `No account can run {model label} on {device} right now: {runtime} {reason}.`, and a busy account of a runtime that cannot run two turns at once makes the turn unavailable with `This account is already running a turn, and its runtime cannot run two at once.`. A session is resumed only when its pinned account is still used, the git identity is read once per checkout and cached in memory, and every guard and grant is released when anything after the account guard fails.
350. (D147) Turn execution clears its last-message buffer at every tool start and end, so the final text is the text after the last tool call, else all text (decision 256). Shutdown wins over stop over steer, but a steer counts only when the run ended interrupted; usage and error reports to the account service are chained and their failures swallowed, so accounting never fails or holds a turn (no hub-outage retry yet).
351. (D148) Project tool handlers are `isCurrent`, `call`, `memory` and an optional one-liner builder for the coordinator ledger; a one-liner that cannot be built or written is dropped, so a performed action never reads as failed. Results are validated and redacted, input errors use the first zod issue as `{path}: {message}. Check the tool input.`, and a call that outlives its turn still returns its result.
352. (D149) Admission counts per project, because both limits are project work settings: remote live work comes from hub indexes owned by other devices and this device's work from runner memory, deduplicated with pending holds. During a hub outage the last good counts and limits apply (zero and defaults when never read); admissions are serialized, and a granted one holds a pending slot until released, so two turns cannot take the last slot.
353. (D150) The Projects `Placement` takes only phase 1 inputs (settings, accounts, runtimes, roster, admission counts, device, clock and closed gates) and returns `atLimit` and the runtime display name with the placement; an absent `allowedDevices` means allowed, and the placing device is added as an online row when the roster lacks it (decision 243).
354. (D151) Publication commits leftovers with plain `git commit` (hooks run) and the cached git identity, and verification passes only with exit code 0, within the timeout and with HEAD unchanged; a moved HEAD or a timeout appends its sentence to the output tail. An origin URL with credentials is refused with `Remove credentials from the origin URL. Use a Git credential helper or SSH.`, a refused first push whose remote tip is one of the thread's own commits retries leasing on that tip, and an open pull request is found and read, never edited.
355. (D152) The pull request tracker serializes reads and merges per thread and runs merge and close transitions on the thread's own step chain (decision 299), re-checking that the pull request is still open. Check and conflict notifications are stored before the coordinator hears them (at most once per head, decision 281); a merge marks the thread `done` and a close `stopped` unless it had already ended, cleanup runs even when the event fails, and polling failures leave `The pull request status could not be read: {message}` once per error kind.
356. (D153) Thread messages are idempotent by message id: the sidecar keeps the last 200 ids with the sha256 of their redacted text, so an owner retry or a retried decision answer repeats with `repeated: true` and no second turn, and a reused id with other text is refused 409 `This message id was already used for different content.`.
357. (D154) A failed turn without an error message is summarized `The turn failed.`, and a launch that throws is treated like an unavailable launch (no process, a synthesized `blocked` report with the redacted error, a `failed` turn end); it counts as a turn, so an unlaunchable thread cannot loop.
358. (D155) When a turn's process cleanup cannot be confirmed while the daemon keeps running, the process record is kept for recovery, the thread becomes `failed` with `Jevellan could not confirm this thread's process stopped.`, and the coordinator gets `thread-interrupted(failed)`.
359. (D156) Every thread step ends at rest: an unexpected failure leaves an error notice, sets the thread `idle` with `This step failed: {message}` and tells the coordinator with `thread-interrupted(failed)`; a daemon shutdown is the exception (decision 260).
360. (D157) The waiting sweep retries exactly the threads at rest whose reason is a `Waiting for a free slot:` text (durable across restarts), oldest queued message first; a needs-decision thread with messages queued during its turn keeps waiting for a new message.
361. (D158) After the third failed verification the attempt counter resets with the `idle` state (the event still says three attempts), so a later done report gets three attempts again.
362. (D159) Allow 10 more turns acts only when the thread is at its limit, so a repeated answer adds nothing; afterwards queued messages run first through admission, and only without them does a publication whose fix turn the limit blocked run again.
363. (D160) Messages queued while a thread was publishing run right after the publication in a turn that continues the live step, without a new admission (like decision 284). The thread passes its rest state for an instant, so a queue sweep can, rarely, dispatch one thread too many in that window; this is a known limit.
364. (D161) Stopping a thread (by the owner, the coordinator or the turn-limit answer) also withdraws its open turn-limit item silently.
365. (D162) Recovery reconciles stale state events before the restart transitions and enqueues each `thread-interrupted` event before the state write, under an id derived from the reason, the thread and its last ledger event, so a crash between the two writes repeats nothing. Decision answers to the coordinator use an id derived from the decision id for the same reason.
366. (D163) Starting a thread on its owner device carries the index labels with the thread (decision 341), and `ThreadService.command` applies a thread command to a local thread, so the phase 5 inbox can reuse it.
367. (D164) The last step of a start is a serialized admission for the new thread id, so two starts cannot take the last slot; a refusal creates the thread `queued` with the admission's `Queued:` text.
368. (D165) Worktree fields (`cwd`, branch, base branch and base commit) are stored when preparation passes and also on a setup failure, so a failed thread shows its branch and Discard removes the worktree. A worktree creation error fails the thread with `Worktree setup failed: <first line>`, and an empty `cwd` or a missing worktree makes the next turn prepare again (decision 280).
369. (D166) An answer whose option label is not one of the question's options is refused 400 `Choose one of the offered options.`. On the turn-limit item, `Allow 10 more turns` with text also sends the text as an owner message, and `Stop the thread` stops with `Stopped by you at the turn limit.` and tells the coordinator (decision 264).
370. (D167) The coordinator plan (the brief 5.1 null rule and the texts of decision 300) also skips menu entries with an unavailable reason, like placement.
371. (D168) Until remote threads exist, thread starts and messages on a project whose coordinator is assigned to another device are refused 409 `Remote threads arrive in phase 5.`. The Projects service takes narrowed views of the account service and memory and leaves ownership, leases, outside activity and the decision client optional until the phases that use them. Phase 5 replaced that refusal with the relay and the proxy (decisions 497 and 498).
372. (D169) Phase 1 views: pull request entries are every index with a pull request plus `idle` branch-only threads (decision 274); the coordinator chip reads `offline` also for a revoked device or one missing from the roster; an unreadable transcript leaves `transcript: null`; overrides stay unavailable until Jev placement; Discard is offered for stopped or failed worktree threads not yet discarded.
373. (D170) Phase 1 routes: thread actions (`stop`, `discard`, `allow-turns`, `pr/refresh`) answer 202 with the thread view, messages 202 `thread-message-receipt-v1 { repeated }`, a start 201 `thread-created-view-v1` (also when it repeats) and `pr/merge` 200. Owner routes resolve the thread owner before reading the body (404 `This thread was not found.`, or 409 `Remote threads arrive in phase 5.` for another device), and a known path with another method is 405 `This operation does not support that method.`. Since phase 5 a thread on another device is proxied, with the refusals of decision 498.
374. (D171) The Projects route handler takes a `retryable()` callback, so routes whose body carries a client id (thread start, thread message) mark a hub outage retryable; the phase 1 route table holds only phase 1 rows, and every other `/api/projects/:id/...` path keeps its existing handler or 404.
375. (D172) Application wiring shares the conversations' lease instance and the `StretchBridges` registry (so `/api/bridge` is unchanged), wraps shared Rigging items in an async function, takes periodic timers from the application options and exposes `projectHub` on the application.
376. (D173) The project fixture's `app`, `server` and `base` are getters that a restart replaces, with a hook that runs while no application is running (crash simulations write thread files there). The GitHub token is saved through the Settings path, repository visibility is stubbed public so no probe reaches GitHub, and `commitStep` commits with `--allow-empty` so a turn that rewrites unchanged content still leaves a commit to verify.
377. (D174) Phase 1 acceptance is split: `tests/projects-threads.test.ts` runs PJ1 to PJ1d on a booted daemon plus only the supporting cases that the in-process runner and API tests do not already cover (the device limit queue, an in-review fix turn waiting for a slot and updating its pull request, Discard refused in review, the account move to a fresh session and the placement gates over HTTP).
378. (D175) Route traversal checks extend the existing route-table test: encoded slashes and dot segments match no Projects route.
379. (D176) The project bridge test drives stub-handler scopes registered on the daemon's shared registry through `bin/jevellan.mjs mcp-bridge` over stdio, without a runtime turn; MCP clients receive refusals as tool errors carrying the daemon's sentence, and an ended turn also rejects `tools/list`.
380. (D177) Pre-existing problem found during phase 1 verification: `eslint.config.mjs` now ignores `.claude/**` and `.codex/**`, gitignored local agent folders that ESLint 9 flat config still linted because it does not read `.gitignore`; no tracked file is excluded.
381. (D178) Phase 1 leak check: the project fixture records every response, and after every `projects-threads` test the recorded responses, fresh list, work and thread views, the hub thread indexes and both ledgers must contain no native session id, bridge token, GitHub token, browser session value or worktrees root path. Each collected value is checked to be at least 16 characters long, so the absence cannot pass vacuously.
382. (D179) Pre-existing problem found during phase 1 verification: eight conversation tests were too tight for their 15-second timeouts under the full suite's four workers; each passes alone, and their durations alone match the previous commit within 0.7 s. They got the 60-second limits (one 120-second limit) their files already use for comparable tests; assertions are unchanged.
383. (D180) Pre-existing product bug found during phase 1 verification: `npm pack --ignore-scripts --json` still runs the root `prepare` script in the foreground, so build output broke the JSON. The distribution code and the installation script now pass `--foreground-scripts=false`, covered by a real-npm regression test.
384. (D181) Pre-existing test problem: two `skeleton.spec.ts` assertions used a bare `getByRole('status')`, which on desktop also matches the sidebar's empty drag status and fails in strict mode; they now filter by their text.
385. (D182) Pre-existing browser drift from conversation commits made without browser runs was repaired in the tests with the same behavioral assertions: the next step, model, effort and work settlement live in the Conversation settings panel, the device switcher lives in the sidebar, and phones open the sidebar drawer first. Shared helpers live in `tests/e2e/navigation.ts`, which also follow the changed transcript markup and the `Steer` button.
386. (D183) On phones the conversation and session headings were named "Open navigation <title>" because the navigation button sits inside the heading; both headings now take `aria-labelledby` of their title (a pre-existing accessibility bug; layout unchanged).
387. (D184) The answer-options browser journey saved a Jev key on the shared fixture servers and broke the journey "automatic decisions can resume from missing configuration" whenever it ran first; it moved unchanged to `tests/e2e/answer-options.spec.ts`, which runs on the keyed servers.
388. (D185) A step that ended at a blocked checkpoint still does not answer the request after the owner accepts its reviewed changes, so the picker offers no Done; two skeleton journeys now close the work through the settings panel (Close this work, then Publish) with their assertions unchanged. The product rule is unchanged and recorded as an owner question about the experience. Known limit (2026-10-05): after accepting a blocked step's changes, Done needs another answering step, so the owner who wants to finish there uses Close this work (publish, keep or discard the changes), the same exit decision 235 records for the cost cap and the failing-tests guard; the guards, counters and limits are unchanged.
389. (D186) `login-recovery.spec.ts` waits up to 60 seconds for the login heading after Add account, like the skeleton's account creation, because adding an account prepares its home and Rigging.
390. (D187) `playwright.config.ts` sets the default `expect` timeout to 15 seconds, equal to the action timeout: 28 skeleton cases failed under four workers on default 5-second assertions that wait for server work, and passed with one worker. Two waits on server work got the budgets their tests already use (30 seconds for Done after publishing, 45 seconds for the picker after adopting outside changes).
391. (D188) `scripts/spikes/installation-browser.mjs` checked a finished conversation through a meta line that became the `Conversation details` chips; it now reads that label, like the skeleton tests (pre-existing drift found once the installation journey had enough disk).
392. (D189) Decisions copied from the Projects build design keep the design's id in parentheses, because code comments and test names cite those ids (for example `(D9)`); references between decisions use these report numbers. Each Projects phase copies the decisions it implements, so the decisions for coordinator turns, the interface, Jev placement, remote threads, main isolation and attach are added when those phases land. Projects phase 7 added the last of them, for attach (decisions 534 to 545).
393. (D5) The coordinator's device publishes `project-coordinator-status-v1` to the hub (state, menu label and account label), so any device can show the coordinator's state and, once remote threads exist, decide Move. Publication is serialized and best effort; the next state change corrects a lost write.
394. (D32) A needs-decision report that the fallback (brief decision 7) already turned into a decision item is still delivered to the coordinator later, with the suffix ` (Jevellan asked the owner directly.)`, so the coordinator knows the owner was asked.
395. (D33) Events delivered in a coordinator turn that the owner stopped count as delivered; events that arrived during that turn were not in its batch and stay queued (brief 8.1).
396. (D34) The coordinator runs in the project checkout of its device; without one it is `unavailable` with the checkout path error as its reason.
397. (D36) `{HH:MM}` in the event block is the coordinator device's local 24-hour time, and an answer given as text only renders after the arrow. The fresh-session recap leaves out the batch being delivered, which follows as events, and labels its items `Owner:` and `You:`.
398. (D42) `jevellan_thread_read` with `transcript` detail for a thread on another device returns its state and reports with a note that the transcript stays on that device (decision 417).
399. (D59) A notebook revision conflict returns the current notebook content to the coordinator in the tool error, so it can merge and write again in the same turn.
400. (D70) An unavailable coordinator is re-evaluated on every new event and on every queue sweep (every 5 seconds), so it runs as soon as a model, account or checkout appears.
401. (D76) Coordinator turns on a member postpone while the hub is unreachable, because the assignment check before every turn needs the hub; the postponed turn retries after the retry delay and does not count as a failure.
402. (D77) Fresh coordinator session sets the session to none. Rotation compares the pinned model, the effective effort, whether the session's model can still run here and whether its account is still eligible (decision 408).
403. (D93) The coordinator's `has conflicts with {base}` event line uses the base branch detected in its own project checkout (`origin/HEAD`, else `main`), so the brief's index, event and pull request schemas stay verbatim. The other half of this choice, the merge dialog reading the base branch from the owner's thread view, arrives with the Projects interface.
404. (D190) Coordinator turn numbers count every coordinator turn of the project across sessions, kept in `coordinator-local.json` as the last number handed out, instead of the session's turn count plus one. The turn input, the bridge scope, the turn start and end records and the tool lines share that number, so a resume the launcher drops (decision 252) cannot renumber a turn after its tools ran and numbers never repeat across sessions; a coordinator bridge token is valid only for the exact scope of the running turn, and the session's own `turns` still counts its completed and stopped turns for the 40-turn rotation.
405. (D191) A failed or timed-out coordinator turn, a launch refused after the plan passed, a preparation error and a turn whose process cleanup was not confirmed all record the redacted error and add one to `failedTurnsInARow`; the first failure retries once after the retry delay (30 seconds) with the same events in front, except an unconfirmed cleanup, which writes its error as a notice and waits for the next event, and the second sets `idle`, writes the brief's failed-twice notice and turns on the decision fallback. After two failures in a row only an owner message starts the next turn: this reads brief 8.1's notice ("Send a message to try again.") as the retry rule, where its general rule would start a turn within 1 second for every thread event and each would launch two more failing turns. Other events wait queued with the fallback active until a turn succeeds, and because the owner-retry flag is kept in memory, the queue also waits for a message after a restart in that state.
406. (D192) Coordinator Stop interrupts only the running turn: its batch counts as delivered (decision 395), the turn counts toward the session, no reply is written and the failure count is unchanged. The loop is not paused, so events that arrived during the stopped turn run in the next turn (brief 8.1), and a Stop before the launch leaves the batch queued. Stop before any assignment does nothing, and for another device's coordinator it answers 409 `Remote threads arrive in phase 5.` until remote threads exist.
407. (D193) The failed-twice notice uses the redacted first line of the error, cut to 300 characters, and strips the error's own final periods so the sentence keeps one. The unavailable notice is written when the coordinator becomes unavailable or its reason changes, never twice for the same reason, and an unchanged re-evaluation rewrites neither the coordinator state nor the hub status. New notices: `The coordinator moved to account {label}; a fresh session started.` (decision 252), a turn timeout text and a text for a process whose stop could not be confirmed.
408. (D194) Session choice checks the plan first (the brief 5.1 null rule with the texts of decision 300), then the checkout (decision 396), so unavailable reasons rank model, account, checkout. A session is kept while it has fewer than 40 turns, the same pinned model, runtime and model string, and the settings effort mapped onto its model equals its effort (decision 402); a kept session launches on its own account, every turn that does not resume records a new session with `turns: 0`, and the fresh context is computed before every launch because the launcher can drop a resume. A Fresh pressed while a turn is being prepared or running wins: that turn never writes its session.
409. (D195) The fallback (brief decision 7, decision 394) creates each needs-decision item under an id derived from the event id, with the event's time, after checking that id on the hub, so a repeated fallback never duplicates an item or conflicts with an answered one. Covered event ids are kept in `coordinator-local.json` (newest 500) until the report is delivered with the suffix. The fallback runs at enqueue while it is active, on every unavailable evaluation, after a second failure and in the queue sweep (which also retries a write the hub refused); its writes are serialized, its errors silent, and it finishes before a prompt is rendered.
410. (D196) Every coordinator turn reads the hub's coordinator assignment first: with no assignment or another device's, the events wait, and a hub outage there or during preparation postpones the turn without counting a failure (decision 401). The status (decision 393) is published when a coordinator with saved state begins, on every state change and on Fresh, with the menu and account labels.
411. (D197) A completed coordinator turn applies its outcome in one `coordinator.json` write (the batch removed by id, the session's turns and native id, the failure count reset, `lastTurnAt`, `idle`) before the reply and the turn end are recorded, so a crash in between loses chat lines but never delivers a batch twice. The turn's bridge grant is closed first, and `lastTurnAt` is set for completed and stopped turns.
412. (D198) `ProjectWork.start()` begins every coordinator that has saved state on this device after recovery, so a non-empty queue runs a new turn (brief 8.6). The periodic queue sweep and `pulse()` re-evaluate coordinators and retry fallbacks, `idle()` waits for coordinator work, and `close()` closes coordinators before threads, leaving a running turn `running` for recovery; the coordinator tool handlers are built by `ProjectWork` on first use, because the thread service and the pull request tracker are constructed after the coordinators.
413. (D199) Phase 1 tests whose fixture runtime cannot enforce read-only turns now see the coordinator evaluate its first event as `unavailable` with the no-model text of decision 300, so `project-runner.test.ts` and `project-work-api.test.ts` assert `unavailable` instead of `idle`.
414. (D200) Coordinator chat lines come from one function in `packages/projects/src/copy.ts`, for example `Started "{title}" · {placement summary}`, `Sent a message to "{title}"` (plus ` and interrupted its turn` only when the delivery interrupted), `Asked you: {first line}`, `Withdrew a question: {reason}`, `Updated the notebook` and `Checked PR #{n}`; a failed call reads `Could not {action}: {first sentence of the error}`, and lines are at most 400 characters. Titles come from this device's thread, else the project's hub index, else `(unknown thread)`. A line carries `reason` only when the call actually withdrew the question, so the interface's withdrawn-question toast must require it.
415. (D201) Coordinator starts and messages are idempotent per tool call: the start passes a client request id derived from the project, the turn and the exact input, and the message uses an id derived the same way (decision 356), because MCP transports retry calls whose reply was lost (the reason of decision 255). An identical call in the same coordinator turn returns the same thread or repeats the message, while the same call in a later turn is new work.
416. (D202) `jevellan_threads_list` with `all` adds concluded threads whose end (else last update) is within 14 days on the coordinator device's clock, through one core rule that the interface's Concluded section will share. `device` is the roster name and `runtime` the runtime id, as the result schema requires.
417. (D203) A thread that is not on this device but has a hub index in the project is read from that index (state, reason, pull request) with the last three reports this coordinator received, and `transcript` detail answers `transcript: null` with `The transcript stays on {device}.` (decision 398). Messages and stops for such threads answer `This thread was not found.` until remote threads exist. Since phase 5 they are relayed to the thread's device (decision 497).
418. (D204) `jevellan_ask_user` with a `threadId` outside the project is refused 404 `This thread was not found.`; `jevellan_thread_stop` answers the thread's state after the stop, and `jevellan_thread_message` the state at return (a `started` turn can still read `idle`). A notebook conflict re-reads the hub for the revision and content in the error, and placement and pull request reasons are cut to 400 characters. The Claude coordinator's allow list and hook already derive from the project tool names (decision 307) and the Codex coordinator keeps its `coordinator` safety profile; both are unchanged.
419. (D205) A `main` default isolation is stored as written, and every read (the settings view, the PUT answer and the project view's settings and notice) shows `worktree` with the reason: `This project is set to Leave git to me.` on a project that leaves git to the owner, else `Main isolation is not available yet.` while main isolation is gated (decision 294). One phase-gate value feeds placement and these reads, and a PUT of `main` is accepted because brief 5.1 coerces on read. Since the main gate opened in Projects phase 6 (decision 525), a `main` default reads as `main` on a project whose Git policy is main.
420. (D206) Fresh coordinator session before any assignment is a no-op and assigns nothing, like Stop (decision 406): only an owner message and an owner-created thread assign the coordinator (decision 241). Stop and Fresh for a coordinator on another device answer 409 `Remote threads arrive in phase 5.` until remote threads exist. Since phase 5 they are forwarded to the coordinator's device (decision 498).
421. (D207) Coordinator Stop and Fresh answer 202 with the project work view after the action, like thread actions answer the thread view (decision 373); Stop answers after the stopped turn has ended, so the view shows the coordinator at rest. Messages answer 202 `coordinator-message-receipt-v1 { repeated }`, decision answers 202 `decision-answered-view-v1 { repeated }`, and settings and notebook reads and writes 200 with their views.
422. (D208) The chat stream reuses one generic `streamLedger` in `apps/daemon/src/conversation-events.ts`, whose frame supplies the event name, the payload and the cursor refusals, so conversation streams keep their texts and bytes. The project frame is `event: project` with `project-event-v1`: blob payloads resolved and redacted again, with `nativeSessionId`, `cwd` and `sessionId` dropped at any depth; cursor refusals are 400 `Invalid project event cursor.` and `Project event cursor is ahead of its history.` before any header. The stream serves this device's ledger when the coordinator runs here or is not assigned yet, answers 404 for an unknown project, 409 for another device's coordinator and 503 `retryable` to a member during a hub outage, after which the interface must reopen the stream itself because `EventSource` does not reconnect after an HTTP error.
423. (D209) Route table rows take a list of methods (work settings and the notebook answer GET and PUT on one row) and name their captured ids per row; only the events row is a stream, so the phase 1 rows keep their shape. Coordinator and settings bodies are parsed before the coordinator is resolved, and a hub outage is retryable for coordinator messages and decision answers, for a settings PUT only when its body carries `clientRequestId` (decision 304), and never for notebook edits, Stop or Fresh.
424. (D210) The phase 1 API test asserted 404 for `GET /api/projects/:id/notebook`, because that route did not exist yet; it now expects the empty notebook view `{ notebook: null, revision: 0 }`.
425. (D211) The phase 2 acceptance fixture option `coordinatorAccountless` adds a second FakeRuntime that enforces read-only turns, a `Coordinator` menu entry after the default one and a single disabled account, and pins the coordinator model to that entry, so PJ2c meets an account gap rather than a capability gap; the fixture project is named `Shop`. The leak check (decision 381) moved into the fixture as `expectNoLeaks`, which also reads the hub coordinator status and every hub record of the project, and it runs after every coordinator and thread acceptance test. PJ2b and PJ2d start their thread from the coordinator, PJ2b's coordinator script answers by the events each turn delivers so batching cannot depend on timing, and PJ2 releases its first coordinator turn only once both publication events are queued.
426. (D212) After a coordinator turn dropped by a restart, the next turn resumes the session recorded when the dropped turn launched (its turn count unchanged), and its prompt is the event block with the undelivered events followed by the `thread-interrupted` restart events of threads that were mid-step.
427. (D213) Phase 2 copies the decisions it implements as 393 to 427 in the format of decision 392. The coordinator half of decision 403 is built and the merge dialog half waits for the interface; the offline chip and notice, the withdrawn-question toast and the other interface decisions are copied with the interface phase. Decision 238 is not repeated: the coordinator chat stream and the turn start's delivered event ids are where that deliberate deviation now shows, and no phase 2 decision is a new deviation from the brief. The roadmap entry `projects-coordinator` is `complete-with-blockers` for the phase 1 reason: the phase gate passed with the full backend and browser matrix green, but the vision checks that brief section 13 requires for every phase were not run (ENV-CLAUDE).
428. (D51) `tests/e2e/projects.spec.ts` runs on its own fixture servers, one per layout (`{layout}-projects`, ports base+8 to base+11), started with `scripts/test-server.mjs --projects`, so the Projects journeys never share state with the existing browser journeys.
429. (D53) The project page switches between two columns and tabs with a container query at 840 px of page width (about a 1180 px viewport without a panel), so opening an inspector panel such as the notebook switches the page to tabs.
430. (D54) Thread dots: running, preparing and publishing are animated magenta, waiting-for-you solid magenta, in-review sea, idle, queued and attached a muted outline, done ok, stopped muted and failed danger. Since Projects phase 7 an attached thread's row also reads `Attached` (decision 544).
431. (D55) The sidebar Projects section is expanded by default, its list is capped at about 30% of the viewport height (in whole rows, decision 467), and its waiting badge counts open decision items.
432. (D56) Thread transcripts label prompt turns `Prompt`, collapse prompts longer than 1,200 characters and place report cards by aligning prompt segments from the end (decision 441).
433. (D74) The New thread modal opens the new thread's page after a successful start.
434. (D79) The thread page reads its view every 1.5 s while the thread is running, preparing or publishing and every 10 s otherwise (decision 454).
435. (D80) The coordinator shows `Offline` (chip and the brief's notice) when the roster reports its device offline (no heartbeat for 10 minutes, or revoked); a stale device keeps its normal state. The view state shipped in phase 1 (decision 372).
436. (D84) The withdrawn-question toast takes its reason from the coordinator chat's `coordinator-tool` record for `jevellan_withdraw_question` (its `reason` field), received over the chat stream; only events after the page opened can toast (decisions 442 and 449).
437. (D96) The sidebar Projects section is its own `nav` landmark labeled Projects inside the existing navigation aside, whose label is unchanged.
438. (D214) Sidebar Projects section: `ProjectsSidebar` renders `<nav aria-label="Projects">` between New conversation and the improver notice. Its toggle reads `Projects section` to assistive technology, so no element besides the Settings tab is named exactly `Projects`, and when collapsed it shows the total of open questions as the magenta badge, so collapsing never hides waiting work. Rows are buttons sorted by name (case-insensitive) so they never move as counts change, each with a dot aligned with the conversation rows (pulsing while the coordinator works, solid magenta while questions are open, else the muted outline), the name, `{n} running` and the waiting count only when they are not zero, and `aria-current="page"` on the current project, also on its thread pages; without projects it reads `No projects yet.` with `Add a project`. The list polls `GET /api/project-work` like the conversation list (every 5 s, on navigation and after the page's own changes); the collapse state is a versioned local document (`projects-sidebar-v1`) synchronized across tabs, and the document title stays the conversation list's.
439. (D215) Texts the browser reads back from the server live in core `project-rules.ts`: `Concluded without changes.` and the prefixes of the owner-started and owner-worked thread messages; the server's copy module builds its lines from them with unchanged bytes. Concluded outcomes read `Merged #n` for a done thread with a merged pull request, `Published to main` for a main thread done with changes, and `No changes` for every other done thread (branch-only threads rest idle, decision 259). Since Projects phase 8 the owner's stop text `The owner stopped this thread.` lives there too (decision 555).
440. (D216) Chat items: each chat record is parsed by its type and invalid records are skipped. Owner messages and event cards are marked delivered by joining on the coordinator event id (decision 238), never on the ledger id. Event cards are one sentence with an optional detail and a link to the thread, for reports (`"{title}" reported progress`, `is done`, `needs a decision`, `is blocked`), publications, test failures, interruptions, answered questions, pull request changes, mail and the owner's own thread actions; an unknown title reads `a thread`. The working state follows the view when it says running, unavailable or offline, and otherwise an unclosed coordinator turn in the stream, which can be ahead of the view.
441. (D217) Report cards are placed by splitting the transcript into one segment per owner prompt and anchoring on the latest segment whose `jevellan_thread_report` call carries the summary of a recorded, non-synthesized report; segments are numbered from that anchor, and without one the last segment is the current turn (the running turn while one runs). Reports older than the first segment come first and newer ones last; without a transcript the cards are listed in order. The anchor keeps cards right when the transcript lags the view; stopped turns and Claude interrupt records can still shift older cards by one.
442. (D218) Withdrawal toasts consider only chat events after the work view's `lastEventId` when the page opened, so replayed history never toasts. Placement lines and row details take the runtime display name (decision 290) from the caller, and a worktree thread without a branch reads `Worktree`. Running lists the newest created first, so rows keep their place while they update; Concluded lists the most recently ended first.
443. (D219) Routes: the interface accepts exactly `/projects/<id>` and `/projects/<id>/threads/<threadId>` with valid ids (the query is ignored), and the header-hiding flag uses the parsed route, so a malformed `/projects/...` path falls through to New conversation with its header. Settings, Projects cards show **Open** first in their actions; the setup page's embedded list does not show it.
444. (D220) Every muted dot uses `--ink-3`: idle, queued and attached are an inset ring and stopped is solid, so both keep 3:1 contrast in dark mode; under reduced motion the live states keep a static ring that still tells them from solid waiting magenta. The new `Confirm` dialog orders Cancel then the action and focuses the action, or Cancel when the action is dangerous. `TranscriptTurn` gains `userLabel` and `collapseLongUser` (prompts over 1,200 characters fold behind their first line) and renders as before without them. From 840 px of page width the side column is 400 px wide and sticky under the page heading; below it the tabs show the chat or the chosen side sections (Threads holds Running and Concluded).
445. (D221) The project work view carries `gates: { mainIsolation, remoteDevices }` from the phase-gate value placement uses (decision 419), so the interface disables what placement would refuse: the New thread Isolation select and the Project settings Main radio are disabled with `This project is set to Leave git to me.` or `Main isolation is not available yet.`, and other devices in the Device select with `Threads run only on this device for now.`. A disabled option cannot show text, so the sentence is a muted line under the field tied to it with `aria-describedby`; a test asserts the interface sentences equal the server's. Since phase 5 the device gate is open and every available device can be chosen (decision 496). Since phase 6 Main is offered as well (decision 525).
446. (D222) The Pull requests section lists open pull requests and branch-only threads (decision 274); merged and closed pull requests belong to Concluded, so a merged pull request leaves the section, and the section count and empty line use the filtered list.
447. (D223) The Project settings dialog reads the settings when it opens, keeps its fields and Save disabled until that read lands and saves with that read's revision, because the page view can predate a save made a moment earlier. A stored `main` default reads as worktree with its notice (decision 419) and is sent back unchanged unless the owner picks an isolation. A revision conflict stays in the dialog with Reload, and only a 401 reaches the app's error handler. Saving shows `Project settings saved.`; Fresh coordinator session shows `The next coordinator turn starts a fresh session.`, because nothing else changes until the next turn.
448. (D224) Deliberate deviation from brief 12.2 (`Sent to the coordinator.` after every answer): answering a thread's own question (the coordinator fallback and turn-limit items, whose answers go to the thread, not the coordinator) shows `Sent to "{title}".` (or `Sent to the thread.`) instead of `Sent to the coordinator.`, which the coordinator's questions keep. The answered card leaves at once, and the line (with a check icon and `role="status"`) stays at the top of Waiting for you until a question arrives that was not open when the answer was sent. Options are stacked buttons with their detail underneath, the free answer is a growing text field (Enter sends), the source line reads `From the coordinator` or `From "{title}"`, and the collapsed Answered list shows the source, time, question and answer.
449. (D225) The project page reads one work view: on open, shortly after each chat frame, every 5 s (thread indexes, pull requests and the roster change without chat events) and after its own changes. Chat frames are deduplicated by id. `Reconnecting…` shows only while the browser reconnects a dropped stream; a refused stream (an HTTP error, decision 422) reopens from the last event id after 5 s, doubling to 60 s, without a notice, because the brief's coordinator notices explain it. Requests made in a dialog or the Notebook panel show their failures in place, because Projects answers 409 for refusals and conflicts and the app reloads every setting on a 409; only a 401 is passed on. Send, Stop and Fresh keep the app's error toast.
450. (D226) Project page layout: the header holds the name (truncated with a tooltip), New thread, Notebook (pressed while its panel is open) and the Project menu, icon-only below 560 px of page width with their names kept for assistive technology; under them the coordinator chip (Idle, `Working…` magenta with a spinner, Unavailable, Offline) and the session as muted text that phones keep. The tabs follow the tablist pattern with roving focus, and the chat follows new entries unless the reader has scrolled up, which shows `Jump to latest`. Owner messages and event cards not yet delivered in a turn say `Waiting for the coordinator` (cards dashed), tool one-liners take an icon per tool and link to their thread, and the composer shows Stop beside Send while a turn runs and disables Send while the coordinator is offline.
451. (D227) Thread rows link to the thread page, with the state word as the dot's tooltip and screen-reader text, an elapsed time and colored concluded outcomes. Pull request cards hold the GitHub link, the badges, `Open thread`, `Open on GitHub` and Merge, which is disabled on conflicts or failing checks with the reason as its tooltip and screen-reader description. The merge dialog takes the base branch from the owner's thread view (the interface half of decision 403), else the project's base branch, else `its base branch`; Merge stays disabled until the base is read, and a refusal stays in the dialog.
452. (D228) New thread: Model lists the enabled menu entries as `{runtime display name} {label}`, Effort the chosen model's efforts (an incompatible effort resets to Automatic), Device the available devices, and Placement is a closed disclosure. A start toasts the server's placement summary and opens the thread page (decision 433); while the coordinator is offline the dialog shows the brief's offline notice and Start thread stays disabled (decision 245). The Notebook panel shows who updated it and when, the Markdown (or its empty text) and Edit, which opens a monospace editor with Cancel and Save; a conflict shows the brief's sentence with Reload, and while reading, the panel follows the view's notebook revision. Since phase 5 the notice carries Move coordinator here (decision 504).
453. (D229) Success toasts from a Projects dialog that closes in the same update (`Project settings saved.`, the placement summary) are sent once no dialog is open, because the app's toast joins the dialog that is open when it mounts, and a message sent while that dialog closes never shows. `main.tsx` is unchanged, so the same race can still swallow `Project saved.` and `Project added.` in existing flows.
454. (D230) The thread page reads its view every 1.5 s while the thread is preparing, running or publishing and every 10 s otherwise (decision 434); after every owner action (send, Stop, Discard, Allow 10 more turns) it reads at once and keeps the 1.5 s pace for 6 s, so the turn an action starts shows without a 10 s wait. Stop sends no reason (the server writes `Stopped by you.`); Stop and Discard are danger confirmations with Cancel focused whose refusals stay in the dialog, and Send and Allow refusals show on the page, so none reaches the app's global 409 reload (decision 449). Discard and Allow toast `Worktree discarded.` and `The thread can take 10 more turns.`.
455. (D231) The thread composer shows `Interrupt current turn` only while the thread runs, sends it with the message and resets it after a send. An attached thread keeps the input disabled under the brief's sentence; a done, stopped or failed thread shows no input, only `This thread has ended. Start a new thread for new work.`, which a test asserts equal to the server's refusal (decision 288). The live bar reads `Working on turn {n}…`, `Preparing…` or `Publishing…`. Messages waiting for the next turn are listed after the transcript under `Waiting for the next turn` as dashed bubbles, because a message sent during a turn would otherwise disappear until the next prompt and invite a resend.
456. (D232) The thread page header holds Stop, Discard and Allow 10 more turns in the brief's order, each only when it applies (Stop until the thread concludes, Discard when the view allows it, Allow at the turn limit of a live thread).
