A busy lease (the same text members receive) is tried again three times after the first try, 20 seconds apart and abortable by a stop, then the thread rests `idle` with `Another publication to main is in progress. Send a message to try again.`, a blocked report goes to the coordinator and the claim is kept. A conflict is recorded as a `conflict` publication with the files, and the step continues with the exact main-conflict prompt (no admission, state `running`). `main-published` sets `done` and the published commit, records the publication with the commit, releases the claim as published and the reservations, then sends `thread-published` with the commit; `no-changes` on main releases the claim unchanged and the reservations.
528. (D291) A main checkout that cannot be settled stays held. When the release after a publication or a conclusion fails, or a stop cannot save and reset the checkout (the hub unreachable, a branch the agent switched to, Git errors), the thread still reaches its state and its ledger gets the notice `The project checkout stays held by this thread: {message}`; a turn whose process cleanup was not confirmed keeps the claim too. Nothing retries the settlement later (a known gap): conversations and main threads stay off that checkout with the `in use by` text until it is released by hand. Corrected in Projects phase 8: every sweep retries the settlement until it succeeds and the thread page shows the held checkout (decision 553).
529. (D292) The main rule and the thread itself (brief 10; decision 480). A placement can name a thread whose own claim and main thread do not count against the main rule. The next-turn override's model check names it, so a main thread can change its model where it is. A restart of a main thread that has not ended (the Override dialog sends every shown field, so the default restart fixes main and the same device) names it too when the coordinator runs on this device: the new thread is placed and created first (a refusal still leaves the old thread untouched, as decision 480 says) but does not prepare yet; the old thread then stops, which settles its checkout (decision 517: commits saved, claim released), and only then does the new thread prepare and claim the checkout. The old thread's reason keeps the saved-commits sentence after `Restarted as {newId}.`. Known gap: when the coordinator runs on another device the restart start is relayed without that name, so a restart onto main on the old thread's device is refused there with `main checkout busy: {old title}` (choose a worktree or another device, or stop the thread and start a new one).
530. (D293) Thread words for checkout errors, and the form rhythm (decision 518). The workspace's dirty-checkout refusal reads `{project} on {device name} has changes that don't belong to this thread. Commit, stash or publish them, then start the thread again.`, any other `conversation` or `conversations` becomes `thread` or `threads`, and other Git messages keep their text. With the main gate open the Isolation field usually has no note, which exposed a tighter gap under note-less fields on phones (the Device field too): such fields now keep the form's 12 px rhythm, with the 4 px gap only above a note.
531. (D294) PJ6 harness and assertions (brief 13 PJ6; no production change). `tests/projects-main.test.ts` uses the PJ5 harness: the hub with a read-only coordinator whose turns all say `Noted.`, a member over real HTTP and a scripted Jev transport that answers placements only, with `src/app.txt` seeded on origin before the member clones it and a menu entry with the efforts `medium` and `high`, so every placement that does not fix everything asks Jev something. Thread turns are driven step by step from the test (a turn runs the test's actions in order, bridge calls and Git included, and stays open until the test ends it or it is stopped), so one turn per thread holds the reservation, the mail, the edit and the report, and both devices' periodic work is pulsed without waiting for idle. Details that differ from the build plan: the fixture project is named `Shop`, so the refusal reads `Shop on {device} is in use by "T1 title".`; the conversation is refused through the conversation API (created, then a manual implement step that pauses with that sentence), and after T1 is done the same conversation's implement step claims the checkout and runs in it; the automatic placement of T3 asks Jev only the effort (the hub's device is excluded with `main checkout busy: T1 title` and the member is the only device left, so no device question remains) and records `source: 'jev'` with only the member eligible; the refusal naming both titles is asserted in either roster order; a worktree thread on the hub's device starts while T1 holds its checkout and has no mail or reservation tools. A main thread reads `done` a moment before its claim and reservations are released (state, ledger, release, then `thread-published`, decision 527), so the test waits for the release. The agent resolves the conflict with `git rebase origin/main` without fetching, which proves that the publication's own fetch had already moved `origin/main` to T1's commit.
532. (D295) A main thread's saved commits on the thread page, longer reasons, mail bodies (brief 12.3; decisions 439, 517 and 526). The builder's fourth visual review, standing in for the vision checks that did not run, led to these fixes. The saved-commits sentence is shared text in core (decision 439), from which the server builds the reason with unchanged bytes. The thread page splits the reason: `Restarted as {newId}.` links the new thread even when the saved-commits sentence follows (before, a restarted main thread with unpublished commits showed the raw id without a link); the ref sits whole on its own line (in `code`, one tap selects it), because on phones the old two-line clamp cut the ref the owner needs to recover the commits; and the rest of the reason clamps at four lines with its full text as the title, because the old clamp also cut the dirty-checkout instruction and long Git messages. A sentence that only looks like the saved-commits one (not at the end, or a ref outside `refs/jevellan/discard/`) stays part of the text. A thread's mail to the coordinator shows its body as the chat card's detail (three lines, like a report's summary). Not changed: the page of a main thread that published shows `Done` without the commit, because the thread index carries no published commit (the concluded row says `Published to main`).
533. (D296) Phase 6 copies the decisions it implements as 517 to 533 in the format of decision 392: the main-isolation decisions deferred until now (517 to 521), the phase 6 decisions (522 to 532) and this item; decision 523 builds the mail and reservation half of decision 491. No phase 6 decision is a new deviation from the brief. Decisions 294, 307, 337, 419, 445, 478, 481, 482 and 491 now point to their phase 6 successors. The roadmap entry `projects-main` is `complete-with-blockers`: PJ6 passes with simulated evidence, both devices being daemons on this machine over local HTTP with one bare origin (no second physical device, DEVICE), with a scripted Jev transport (ENV-JEV) and test-driven FakeRuntime turns, but the vision checks that brief section 13 requires for every phase were not run (ENV-CLAUDE). Phase 6 adds no browser journey, because brief section 13 defines PJ6 as an integration test; the PJ3 and PJ4b journeys cover the interface changes, and main-thread pages were checked by the builder only. The phase 6 matrix run rewrote every Projects screenshot. `Last updated` already read 2026-10-05, the day phase 6 ran. The texts that still described Main as unavailable (PJ3.md and PJ4.md) or the offline-coordinator fallback as missing (PJ5.md and the `projects-mesh` roadmap entry) get dated, minimal updates.
534. (D46) Attach is allowed only for a thread at rest (`idle`, `waiting-for-you` or `in-review`) that has a native session; its answer, sent only on the loopback control route, is the one API answer that carries the native session id and the account's authentication variables (decisions 536 and 538).
535. (D47) `jevellan thread attach` exits with the native CLI's exit code (128 plus the signal number when a signal ended it), and with 1 when its detach could not reach Jevellan; `jevellan thread detach {threadId}` then hands such a thread back (decisions 539 and 540).
536. (D297) Attach on the daemon. The control route's attach checks, in this order, each refusing with 409 unless noted: a thread with no local file whose hub index names another device, `This thread runs on {device}. Run the command there.` (the device's roster name, else its id), and with no index (or one naming this device) 404 `This thread was not found.`; an ended thread, `This thread has ended. Start a new thread for new work.`; an attached thread, `This thread is already attached in a terminal. Exit that terminal first, or run jevellan thread detach {threadId}.` (refused, not repeated: a second terminal's exit would hand the thread back while the first still works); a thread that is not `idle`, `waiting-for-you` or `in-review`, or has a step queued on its runner (a turn about to start from rest), `The thread is working. Wait for the turn to end or stop it.`; no native session, `This thread has no session to attach to.`; a runtime other than `claude` or `codex`, `Only Claude and Codex threads can be taken over in a terminal.`. A main thread then takes its checkout claim again for this process, so a conversation that took a stale claim after a restart refuses in its own words, and the account resolves in its launch form, so a Codex API key never appears. Only after all that is the thread checked again and set `attached` with `attach.startedAt` (the wall clock, because detach compares it with file times) and its state reason cleared, with no wait in between, so no turn can start meanwhile; attach never waits on the runner, which could hold a six-hour turn. The answer (`thread-attach-v1`) carries the thread's stored working directory, the runtime, the native session id, the placement's model and effective effort, the device name and an environment of exactly `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `CODEX_HOME`, and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` when the account has one. While a thread is attached the pull request tracker applies no merge or close to it (no `done` or `stopped`, no worktree removal under the owner's terminal); its first read after detach applies it. Owner messages queue and the coordinator's are refused (decision 288); nothing else refuses an attached thread, so Stop from the owner or the coordinator still acts (a known gap). No lifecycle activity is held while attached, so updates are not blocked for the length of a terminal session, and the thread stays attached across a restart. Corrected in Projects phase 8: an attached thread refuses Stop, Restart and Discard (decision 552) and holds its account for turns on its device (decision 554).
537. (D298) Detach on the daemon. Detach runs on the thread's runner (an attached thread has no live step, so it is quick, and it is ordered with Stop). A thread that is not attached (detached before, the command retrying, or stopped while attached) answers `{ adopted: false, state }` and changes nothing, so the command and `jevellan thread detach` can always call it. Otherwise the account home is listed through the transcript worker (decision 275; the home is never created), and the newest session whose working directory resolves to the thread's (`/var` and `/private/var` match), modified strictly after `attach.startedAt`, and that is neither another local thread's session nor the project's coordinator session (the coordinator works in the project checkout, which is a main thread's directory) becomes the thread's native session. When none qualifies the stored session stays; a failed listing keeps it too, with the thread notice `The session from the terminal could not be found, so the thread keeps its earlier session. {error}`, so a thread never stays attached because of a scan. Then one write: state `idle`, also for a thread that was in review or waiting (the brief's literal; an open pull request is still polled, decision 299, and an open question stays open), and `attach` removed. After that step the coordinator receives `[owner worked on thread "{title}" in a terminal]` as a `thread-user-message` (a failure becomes a notice), then the messages that waited start the next turn through the usual admission (decision 244), resuming the adopted session. The request's exit code is accepted and not used.
538. (D299) Local control routes. Every `/api/local/*` request goes to its own module before the browser rules. `/api/local/doctor` keeps its behavior byte for byte (POST only, else 401 `Local diagnostics require the installation control file.`; any failure, a bad body included, is its one 503 sentence). `POST /api/local/threads/:tid/attach` and `/detach` authorize first with doctor's rule (127.0.0.1 on both ends, no `Origin`, the timing-safe control token; a server without the control file refuses), else 401 `Local commands require the installation control file.`; then an unknown local path answers 404 `Not found.`, another method 405 `This operation does not support that method.`, an invalid id or body 400 `Check the submitted fields.` and a body that is not JSON 415; failures map as in the browser API (`conflict`, `not-found`, `hub-unavailable` with `retryable: true`, else `request-failed`) with redacted messages. Each operation holds a lifecycle request activity, so maintenance answers 503. Only the attach answer is sent without the redactor; the detach answer goes through it. Tests boot these routes through the project fixture's `control` option, which starts the daemon on loopback with its control file.
539. (D300) The terminal takeover command. Its control-file client is extracted from doctor: it reads `doctor.json` for every request (the token rotates on every daemon start, and the file keeps the origin on loopback, so nothing else is ever contacted), posts JSON without following redirects, with a 45-second timeout and a 64 KiB answer cap, and takes its failure sentences as options, so doctor throws exactly what it threw before. A thread command prints the daemon's `error-v1` sentence verbatim (`This thread runs on {device}. Run the command there.`, `The thread is working. Wait for the turn to end or stop it.`, `This thread was not found.`), so the command owns no refusal text and never imports the Projects package. Its own sentences: `Jevellan could not be reached on this device. Check that it is running, then run the command again.` (no control file, nothing listening); `Jevellan did not answer this command.` (an answer that is not JSON); `Resuming the thread in Claude Code. Exit it to hand the thread back to Jevellan.` (or Codex) before the start; after detach `The thread is back in Jevellan.` or, when a session was adopted, `The thread is back in Jevellan and continues from the session in this terminal.`; and when the detach did not reach the daemon `The thread stays attached. Run jevellan thread detach {threadId} when Jevellan is running.` (after the daemon's sentence when it refused), with exit 1 even when the native CLI exited 0. The command checks the daemon's answers itself: an attach answer it cannot use (a wrong schema, a home variable that is not the account home, an authentication variable the runtime never takes) prints `The answer from Jevellan could not be used.` and hands the thread straight back. A missing executable (`Claude Code is not installed on this device: the claude command was not found on PATH.`, and the same for Codex), a missing thread folder or a start failure also hand the thread back and exit 1; the coordinator still hears the owner-worked line, because detach cannot know that nothing ran. Arguments are checked before any request: a wrong shape prints `Usage: jevellan thread attach threadId, or jevellan thread detach threadId` and an invalid id `That is not a thread id. Copy the command from the thread page.`, both with exit 1, and nothing in the thread commands throws, because the installed entry point would print a stack trace. Progress goes to standard output and failures to standard error; `jevellan thread detach` sends no exit code.
540. (D301) Starting the native CLI and its signals. The executable is the first `claude` or `codex` on the absolute entries of the user's PATH (the owner's own install, as typed in a terminal), started with inherited stdio, no shell, in the thread's directory and in the command's own process group, so it is the terminal's foreground job. Its environment is the minimal one headless turns get: the user's PATH, USER, LANG, TERM, SHELL and TMPDIR, the account home as HOME and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `CODEX_HOME`, and the account's authentication variables; nothing else passes (COLORTERM, LC_*, SSH_AUTH_SOCK and editor variables are dropped). Claude's effort flag is probed at every attach: `claude --help` runs for at most 15 seconds with the account home and no authentication variable, and `--effort {effort}` is added only when the help lists `--effort`; a failed or slow probe means no flag. Codex always gets `-c model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"`. SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP are held from before the attach request until the detach answers, so the command always detaches. SIGTERM and SIGHUP are forwarded to the native CLI. SIGINT is forwarded only when standard input is not a terminal: a terminal's Ctrl-C already reaches the whole foreground group, and sending it again would read as a double Ctrl-C, so the native CLI receives each SIGINT once either way, which is how the brief's forwarding is read. A signal before the native CLI starts hands the thread back without starting it. Detach is sent the native exit code, or none when a signal ended it. Effort flag record (2026-10-05, with scratch home and configuration folders, never the owner's homes; only the help was printed and no session was started or resumed): `claude` 2.1.289 at `~/.local/bin/claude`, first on this machine's PATH, lists `--effort <level>` (low, medium, high, xhigh, max), `-r, --resume [value]` and `--model <model>`, so attach passes `--effort`; an older `claude` 2.1.19 at `/opt/homebrew/bin/claude` lists no effort flag, so a PATH that finds it first gets none; `codex-cli` 0.154.0 has `codex resume [OPTIONS] [SESSION_ID] [PROMPT]` with `-m, --model <MODEL>` and `-c, --config <key=value>`. Live resumes were not run; the tests use simulated CLIs. Since Projects phase 8 the Claude command also carries `--settings '{"cleanupPeriodDays":36500}'`, a deliberate deviation (decision 564).
541. (D302) Command output and its tests. The installed entry point drops only Node 22's `SQLite is an experimental feature` warning, which printed above the output of every command, the attach command included; every other warning still prints. The command tests run the real `bin/jevellan.mjs` as a process against a project fixture with its control file, with `JEVELLAN_HOME`, the fixture user's HOME and a PATH of only the fixture folder and `/usr/bin:/bin`, so the owner's CLIs are never found. The simulated `claude` and `codex` are executables with an absolute node shebang (a shell wrapper would add PWD and SHLVL to the recorded variable names, and test settings cannot travel through the minimal environment); they record their arguments, variable names (never values; a sha256 of the authentication variable), working directory, whether standard input is a terminal, signals and exits, write a new native session in the account home, echo standard input lines and exit on `exit {code}`. The terminal case runs the command under `script` in a pseudo-terminal (macOS only: BSD `script` syntax, and no Linux test runner exists), fed through a real pipe because macOS `script` refuses a socket as input. The simulated CLI installs its signal handlers before it writes its run record, so a test that signals on seeing the record cannot race it. macOS adds `__CF_USER_TEXT_ENCODING` to every process, so the tests drop that one name.
542. (D303) PJ7 harness and assertions (no production change). `tests/projects-attach.test.ts` shares the command tests' harness (`tests/helpers/attach-cli.ts`): the project fixture with its control file, the real installed command as a process and node-shebang simulated CLIs that end on the standard input line `exit 0`, where the build design planned a hand-built daemon, an in-process command, shell wrappers and a release file (test settings cannot cross the minimal environment, decision 541). The coordinator stays unavailable (the fake runtime does not enforce read-only turns, decision 300), so the owner-worked line is read from the coordinator queue, and the coordinator's message goes through the thread service exactly as `jevellan_thread_message` calls it; the bridge refusal itself is covered by `tests/project-coordinator-tools.test.ts`. The attached state is checked on the browser API (the thread view's state, `canMessage`, `attach.startedAt`, `attachCommand` and `deviceName`; the work view and the hub index) and through the page's own composer rule. Beyond the build plan, PJ7 asserts that a pulse while attached starts no turn, the receipt is not a repeat, the queue holds only the owner's message, the transcript after detach reads the adopted session (the terminal's rows before the turn that continued it), the second turn's report, the ledger tail `attached, idle, running, idle` and the leak check. Codex arguments, a thread on another device, detach with the daemon down and the Tailscale and `Origin` refusals are not repeated in PJ7: the command and route tests cover them.
543. (D304) Take over in a terminal on the thread page. The line sits under the input row, inside the composer form but outside its disabled fieldset, so Copy still works while the thread is attached, and shows for every thread that has not concluded (running and attached included: the line says how, the daemon says when; attach refuses concluded threads, so they get none). It reads the brief's `Take over in a terminal on {device}: jevellan thread attach {threadId}` with the command as code, and an icon button labelled `Copy command` copies only the command: the Clipboard API in a secure context; otherwise, or when it refuses, a hidden read-only textarea (16 px, so iOS does not zoom) focused, selected and copied with `execCommand('copy')`, which covers the plain-HTTP Tailnet URL; success says `Command copied.` and focus returns to the button (decision 544 covers a copy refused both ways). A command wider than a whole line (phones) ends in an ellipsis, its title holding the whole command and the button copying all of it, so a phone gives the line two rows, not three; the paragraph never widens the composer grid. The browser fixture's control routes `POST /projects/attach` and `/projects/detach` take `fixture-attach-v1 { threadId }`, call the daemon's own attach and detach, answer only the state (never the working directory, native session or environment) and pass a refusal's status and sentence through. The PJ7 browser journey creates its own thread fixed to the hub device (a thread on the member refuses with its device sentence), attaches it through the control route, checks the disabled composer, the notice and the line, captures `PJ7-attached`, copies through all three paths, then detaches and sees the composer take messages again.
544. (D305) Takeover line polish after the builder's fifth visual review, standing in for the vision checks that did not run (refines decisions 543 and 430; no server change). (1) A copy the browser refuses both ways no longer reports through a toast, which is success-toned and on phones sits over the composer and the very command it says is selected: the command shows whole (it wraps instead of ending in an ellipsis), stays selected, and `The browser blocked copying, so the command is selected for you to copy.` shows under it as a status line; both fold back when the selection leaves the command or a later copy works. The command is `user-select: all`, so a long press on a phone selects all of it. (2) The line uses the composer note's 12.5 px (the command stays 11.5 px mono), so the composer's two muted lines share one size. (3) The copy button still draws 28 px, but a transparent extension widens its target to about 38 by 34 px (measured by 1 px sampling: 5 px above, 1 px below, 3 px left and 7 px right of the drawn box); it reaches only 1 px under the button, inside the composer's padding, because a box that reaches below the composer adds scroll height (a 6 px extension made every short thread page scroll by 1 px). The PJ7 journey asserts the inline reason, no toast, the whole command, a `PJ7-copy-blocked` capture, the fold-back and that the attached page does not scroll vertically. (4) An attached thread's row shows `Attached` (muted, like a concluded row's outcome, and hidden from screen readers because the row's state already says it) before its elapsed time: decision 430 gives attached idle's outline dot, and attached is the one idle-looking state that holds messages until the terminal exits. Logged, not changed: the sticky composer on phones is 44 px taller with the line's two rows, and `Command copied.` covers the composer for its 8 seconds on phones (the app-wide phone toast position).
545. (D306) Phase 7 copies the decisions it implements as 534 to 545 in the format of decision 392: the attach decisions deferred until now (534 and 535), the phase 7 decisions (536 to 544) and this item. No phase 7 decision is a new deviation from the brief, so the list of deliberate deviations is unchanged: SIGINT reaches the native CLI once either way (decision 540); detach never adopting a session Jevellan itself runs narrows a case the brief does not consider (decision 537); the device name in the attach answer and the refusals beyond running and publishing fill silences. Decisions 266, 288, 299, 305, 392 and 430 now point to their phase 7 successors. The roadmap entry `projects-attach` is `complete-with-blockers`: PJ7 passes with simulated provider CLIs on PATH, as brief section 13 asks, and no real session was resumed, but the vision checks that brief section 13 requires were not run (ENV-CLAUDE), and neither four-worker browser matrix run of the phase was fully green (skeleton conversation journeys under machine load that pass alone). `Last updated` already read 2026-10-05, the day phase 7 ran. The texts that still described the takeover line as later work, or the Projects screenshots as written by the phase 6 run (PJ3.md, PJ4.md and PJ5.md), get dated, minimal updates.
546. (D307) AGENTS.md's "Architecture in ten lines" becomes "Architecture in eleven lines": `packages/projects` is inserted as item 5 after `packages/conversations`, its closest neighbor, instead of merging two existing lines, because every existing line names one boundary and a merge would blur two. Items 2, 6, 10 and 11 gain Projects, thread placement, turns and thread attach; the vocabulary line adds the Projects terms and the rule that the Projects area never shows stretch, handoff or conversation; the verification line adds project limits in packages/projects.
547. (D308) Roadmap entries for the specification's non-goals (BRIEF-projects.md section 2). Three non-goals are covered by two existing planned entries, whose one-sentence `why` is extended instead of adding near-duplicates: Cursor threads and Gemini/OpenCode by `more-runtimes` (adapters with turns, so conversations and project threads can run on them), and the native iOS app by `native-companion` (a native iPhone app, project threads and questions included). The other seven get new `planned` entries after `projects-attach`: `projects-claude-cloud`, `projects-browser-terminal`, `projects-scheduled-coordinator`, `projects-coordinator-merge`, `projects-placement-learning` (not covered by `improver-autonomy`, which applies existing proposals), `projects-push-notifications` and `projects-remote-branch-cleanup`, titled `Projects: ...` like the phase entries and written so that no existing line changes except the two extended `why` values.
548. (D309) The Projects documentation describes the system as built, not the specification. Where the build deliberately differs, the documents state the built rule and cite this report's decision number (the deviations 238, 244, 250 with 346, 255, 299, 448, 488 and 564; 245 is cited as built behavior, not as a deviation), never a build design id. [docs/projects.md](../projects.md) owns the Projects reference (concepts, the coordinator, threads, a placement summary, isolation, mail and reservations, publication and the GitHub token, attach, limits, restart, the relay, state, the interface, deviations and evidence); [docs/decisions.md](../decisions.md) owns question set p-v1, whose sections say "placement Call A/B" so they are not confused with q-v2's Call A/B; [docs/runtimes.md](../runtimes.md) owns turns and safety profiles; [docs/mesh.md](../mesh.md) owns the hub namespaces, envelopes and the absent peer routes; and [docs/architecture.md](../architecture.md) the package boundaries. Prompts are summarized with a pointer to `packages/projects/src/copy.ts`, their one verbatim source, rather than copied. Stale statements were fixed: architecture.md (the improver is built and its live run pending), decisions.md (the evaluator and the improver exist; live evaluation needs the dedicated key), this report's Next work, and mesh.md's "Automatic boundary resumption remains unfinished.", which the same document's introduction and the [hub waits evidence](phase4-hub-waits.md) contradict: publication and the other conversation boundaries wait for the hub and continue after its next heartbeat.
549. (D310) The PJ-live runner and its receipt. `scripts/spikes/live-journeys.mjs` becomes a dispatcher: the conversation journeys' body moves byte for byte to `live-conversation-journeys.mjs` (an ES module cannot return early from its top level, and wrapping 370 lines would re-indent them), and PJ-live lives in `live-projects-journey.mjs`. PJ-live reads every `JEVELLAN_TEST_*` value it needs, deletes them all from its environment, then checks presence before any option. A missing or malformed required credential (ENV-JEV, ENV-CODEX, ENV-GITHUB; the repository must read `owner/repository`) writes a `live-journey-v2` receipt with `label: 'blocked'`, `blockedBy` (blocker id, variables and reason), `optionalMissing` (ENV-CLAUDE), presence-only `variables`, `daemonStarted: false` and `passed: false` to [PJ-live.json](PJ-live.json) (or `{output}/evidence.json`), and exits 0 without creating a home, daemon, browser or clone. `live-journey-v2` is `live-journey-v1` plus the `blocked` label and those fields; the conversation journeys keep v1. A live run that fails a check or stops on an error exits 1. The secret scanner's allowlist comment names the moved file; the allowlisted expression is unchanged.
550. (D311) The live Codex credential is `JEVELLAN_TEST_CODEX_KEY`, for the specification's "a real Codex account": an OpenAI API key of a dedicated test project, added through `POST /hub/accounts` as a Codex API-key account with paid use `always`, so it lives only in the vault and the account's authentication file. A subscription cannot be passed as a value (adding an account refuses a Codex subscription secret, because the sign-in belongs to a device), and the earlier prepared Codex test home was moved, never copied, so it cannot be reproduced from a variable. Its absence is the blocker ENV-CODEX.
551. (D312) PJ-live isolation and journey choices (AGENTS.md: never borrow the user's credentials). The runner's Git uses only the isolated user home, whose `.gitconfig` resets the credential helper list with an empty `credential.helper` (Homebrew Git's system configuration names `osxkeychain`, the owner's keychain; checked with a traced `credential fill` against a fake host) and then uses `credential-cache` on a private socket that holds the token in memory (`git credential approve` over standard input, `credential-cache exit` at the end). Before Jevellan starts, `HOME` becomes that home, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` are dropped, and the repository visibility comes from the test token's repository read instead of `gh`. The repository must default to main and carry the `jevellan-acceptance-sandbox` marker; the runner clones it fresh into a new `--sandbox` with a test commit identity and requires a new `--home`. The repository name is a `JEVELLAN_TEST_*` value the scanner blocks, so evidence writes it as `[repository]` and asserts its absence; it is not added to the daemon's redactor, which would rewrite stored pull request addresses. The project uses the external branch policy (worktree threads and a pull request, never a publication to main) and device memory; the coordinator is pinned to the first enabled Codex model; one thread runs at a time; the global timers are off and the Projects timers periodic (15-second pull request polls), with a heartbeat every minute. The owner message goes through the page composer and the merge through the page's Merge and its confirmation (the page's own merge answer is recorded). Checks that read `none` count only after holding for a minute; failing checks stop the journey. The test token asks for Contents write and Commit statuses read beyond the Settings help line of the specification, because GitHub lists the merge under Contents write and the combined status read under Commit statuses read; the product copy is unchanged and the question is open (see Next work).
552. (D313) An attached thread refuses Stop, Restart and Discard (corrects decision 536's "nothing else refuses an attached thread"). While a thread is `attached`, the owner's Stop, Restart (Override with a restart) and Discard, the coordinator's `jevellan_thread_stop` and a turn-limit `Stop the thread` answer are refused with 409 and one sentence: `This thread is attached in a terminal: exit that terminal session first, or run jevellan thread detach {threadId}.` The runner's stop checks before it begins to stop (a refused stop leaves the runner usable after detach, and attach refuses a stopping runner, so the check cannot race); discard checks before its own refusal; restart checks first, before placement, receipts or the override record, and the restart refusal adds the attached case last (after an open pull request, a published main and an earlier restart, which outlast the terminal), so the Override dialog disables Restart with the sentence. A coordinator stop of a thread on another device checks its hub index first (an attach after that read makes the owner refuse the relayed command, which the inbox drops as a permanent refusal). The turn-limit answer is checked before the hub records it, so the question stays open (unknown during a hub outage, where the relayed stop is dropped the same way). The thread view gains `stopRefusal` (an optional phase 8 field); the page shows Stop disabled, described by the sentence on a visible line under the reason. Next-turn overrides, Allow 10 more turns and owner messages are unchanged (messages still wait).
553. (D314) Unsettled main checkout claims are settled again at every sweep (corrects decision 528's "nothing retries"). A durable marker `unsettledCheckout { settle: published | unchanged | stop, message? }` in `thread-local-v1` (an optional phase 8 field) is written before every settlement and cleared on success, so a failure or a crash in between leaves the retry to the sweeps. It is also set by a main thread whose process cleanup was not confirmed, by a failed preparation whose release failed too, and by recovery for a main thread whose process cleanup failed, which now keeps that process record (other threads drop it as before). The thread sweep (every 5 seconds and on each pulse) ends with the settlements, so a slow one never delays dispatch. For every ended thread with a marker, on its runner: a recorded process must be confirmed gone by recovery's rule, else the claim stays with `Jevellan could not confirm this thread's process stopped.`; nothing happens when the claim is no longer the thread's (released, or by hand); a release takes the claim for this process first (so the claim is not rewritten each time and a restart's old process id is reclaimed); and a stop settlement runs only while the checkout is on main and no other agent is active in it, so nothing is committed or reset under an owner repairing the checkout (decisions 561 and 576 narrow this further). A stop whose head is already at the base still returns the ref an earlier attempt saved, so the reason names it. A failure writes the notice `The project checkout stays held by this thread: {message}` only when its message changes; success clears the marker, writes `The project checkout was given back.` and appends the saved-commits sentence to the reason when a ref was saved. The thread view gains `checkoutHeld` (the kept sentence plus ` Jevellan tries again until it is released.`), shown on an ended thread's page as a warning line. Not retried: a restart's deferred new main thread still prepares right after the old one's stop, so it fails with the in-use sentence when the old claim was kept; the owner can restart that failed thread once the claim is released.
554. (D315) An attached thread holds its account for turns on its device (refines decision 536, under which attach held nothing). Held accounts are read at every launch from the threads themselves (the placement account of local threads in `attached`), so a restart keeps the hold and detach releases it, also `jevellan thread detach` after a terminal that ended without detaching. They apply whatever the runtime's one-run-at-a-time guard says (Claude and Codex both declare per-launch configuration, so that guard alone would change nothing). The launcher passes held accounts over like ineligible ones (the pinned account while free, else the next in ranking order, which is decision 252's account move with its fresh session and notice) and answers `Account {label} is in use in a terminal.` when only held accounts are eligible (the rare attach between a turn's check and its launch: a thread then gets a synthesized blocked report, a coordinator a failed turn). Threads check first, before the turn takes its messages: with no free eligible account the thread rests (a live step ends `idle`) with `Waiting for account {label}, which is in use in a terminal. This thread continues when the terminal session ends.`; a message turn keeps its messages queued, and a task, verification or conflict turn is kept as `pendingTurn { reason, body }` in `thread-local-v1` (an optional phase 8 field, body up to 40,000 characters), which runs first at the next turn from rest, before messages that arrived meanwhile. The waiting sweep retries these threads like slot and hub waits, and detach schedules the sweeps and the coordinator's re-check at once. The coordinator's turn plan (not its Move readiness) keeps its model and takes a free eligible account, else the coordinator is `Unavailable` with `Account {label} is in use in a terminal. The coordinator continues when the terminal session ends.`: no failed turn is counted, the sweep re-checks it, and the owner-question fallback of an unavailable coordinator applies meanwhile. Known limits: conversations (stretches, the improver's drafts) do not take part in the hold, so conversation behavior is unchanged, and a turn already running on the account when the owner attaches is not stopped, nor does attach wait for it.
555. (D316) The owner's own stop reads in the owner's voice in the project chat. The `thread-interrupted` event's message stays `The owner stopped this thread.` (the coordinator's event block is unchanged); the constant moves to core `project-rules.ts` beside the other texts the owner device writes and the browser reads back (decision 439), and the Projects copy module re-exports it. The chat card of a `stopped` event with that message reads `You stopped "{title}"` (like `You started` and `You worked on`) with no detail; a `stopped` event with any other message keeps the neutral `"{title}" was stopped` card with the message as its detail. Its only producer is the runner's notifying stop (the owner's Stop and the turn-limit `Stop the thread` answer); the coordinator's own stop does not notify. Old ledgers render the new card too, because the card's text is derived when it is drawn.
556. (D317) An attached thread's disabled composer reads disabled. The rules apply to the disabled field set only, so an empty but enabled composer, whose send icon is also disabled, keeps its look: the row keeps its background and loses its shadow, as before, and takes a dashed border and the not-allowed cursor; the text field takes the not-allowed cursor, the placeholder the muted ink at 60 % and the send icon 45 % opacity. Dark themes needed the border and the faded placeholder because the row and card colours are almost the same there. PJ7 compares the computed border style, cursor, placeholder colour and send opacity of the enabled, attached and detached composer.
557. (D318) The conversation pages' live bar sits on the page colour (the phase 3 visual review had fixed it only for Projects pages). One rule, `.reading-conversation .composer { background: linear-gradient(to top, var(--bg) calc(100% - 8px), transparent); }`, replaces the Projects-only rule (Projects routes are reading pages too, so their composer is unchanged); the fade keeps to the composer's 8 px top padding, so no transcript text runs through Jump to latest or the working line. Ordinary and Cursor conversation pages change; the new-conversation page keeps the shared 82 % fade. The phase 2 manual journey scrolls a paragraph under the top of the bar and compares a fixed, transparent, whole-pixel probe over the bar beside its button with and without the page behind it; it failed in all four layouts before the rule and passes after.
558. (D319) The device switcher pill fits the 236 px sidebar. The device name is its own span that ends in an ellipsis, the summary cannot grow past its container while the dot and chevron keep their size, the sidebar's switcher may shrink, and the summary's title names the device in full. The device journey sets a 900 px viewport, checks the title, the full text and that the pill ends inside the sidebar, then restores the layout's viewport.
559. (D320) The browser fixture server runs in a process group of its own (the remaining cause of the close failures in decision 510). Playwright's shutdown sends SIGTERM to the web server's whole process group, so it also reached whatever `ps`, Git or provider process the fixture server was running at that moment: the memory transport lost its `/bin/ps` to the group SIGTERM, `Application cleanup did not complete` came from the signal handler's unawaited close, and the temporary root stayed. The listener-before-application order of decision 510 was already in place. Now `scripts/test-server.mjs`, started without an IPC channel, only launches itself again, detached, with one, passes SIGINT and SIGTERM to that process alone and exits with its code. The server closes on its signals and when the channel disconnects (its launcher died, also by SIGKILL after Playwright's 15 seconds); it closes the fixture control listener first and waits for control requests in flight, then the member listener, the member, the hub listener and the hub as before, always removes its root, prints a failed close and exits 1 (else 0). The signal and disconnect handlers exist from the moment the root does; a close requested during startup waits until the server is ready, and any requested close ends the process within 30 seconds (root removed, exit 1), because no group SIGKILL reaches the server any more. `tests/test-server-close.test.ts` starts it in a group of its own as Playwright does, on free ports away from the matrix and with a private `TMPDIR`: a group SIGTERM gives exit 0, no failed close, no root and free ports; a group SIGKILL, when ready and while starting, still removes the root and frees the ports. The ready cases failed before the change.
560. (D321) The heaviest skeleton journeys wait on measured budgets (the owner's standard on flaky tests). Blocked files, context merge and J11 failed only under the four-worker matrix, on fixed 30 to 45 second waits for server work, while passing alone. Each such wait now runs in a named `test.step('server work: {title}')`, so Playwright's reports record its duration, and takes `budget(aloneSeconds, floor)`: the worst duration of that wait with its journey alone (one worker, four layouts, 2026-10-05) times `LOAD_MARGIN = 6`, rounded up to 5 seconds and never below the earlier fixed wait. The three tests' own timeouts take the same rule over the journey's alone duration (blocked files 26.2 s to 160 s, context merge 33.7 s to 205 s, J11 73.8 s to 445 s, which was 240 s). Waits that grew: blocked changes 25 s (was 15), accepted changes 60 s, context apply 75 s, context created 70 s, J11's reply with memory 80 s (was 45), review 60 s, reply on the member 55 s and the member's context apply 70 s; the others keep 30 or 45 seconds. The margin: the four layouts running these journeys at once inflated single waits only 1.1 to 1.6 times, but in the full matrix whole journeys took up to 2.6 times their alone duration and single waits failed past 3.4 times, so 6 leaves about twice the worst seen. No assertion changed, only timeouts. A dedicated serial project was not used: it would change when these journeys run against the shared per-layout server state, and Playwright skips dependent projects after a failure.
561. (D322) A main thread never writes to a claimed checkout that is off main (crucial-issues review finding TH-1; refines decisions 517 and 553). The claimed checkout stays the owner's. Stopping a main thread, and so the sweeps' stop settlement, which is now the same stop, requires before any rebase is aborted or anything committed or reset that the checkout works on main (HEAD's branch, or while a rebase is unfinished the branch it rewrites, so the agent's own rebase onto main counts as main) and that no other agent is active in it; after an abort it checks main again. A failure keeps the claim with decision 528's notice and retries. Main publication refuses with `This project must be on main before Jevellan can change git.` before its leftover commit (after the unfinished-operation check), as an error outcome (the thread rests idle with the reason, and the coordinator gets a blocked report). Before every turn, after the claim is taken again, a separate on-main check decides: off main the turn rests like an account wait (decision 554) with `This project must be on main before Jevellan can change git. This thread continues once the checkout is back on main.`, its messages queued and a task, verification or conflict turn kept as the pending turn, and the waiting sweep retries it. Departure from the finding's suggested fix, not from the specification: the check is a separate step after the claim rather than part of it, because attach also takes the claim, and an owner taking the thread over in a terminal decides the branch themselves. Known gap: an agent that leaves the checkout off main in its own turn parks the thread on that reason with no coordinator event, so the coordinator's later messages queue until main is back (the thread page and list show the reason). A second gap, that a retried stop committed an owner's uncommitted files on main into the discard ref, is closed by decision 576.
562. (D323) Stop ends a main publication before the push (review finding TH-2). Main publication checks the runner's abort signal before the leftover commit, before verification, at every push attempt before the fetch, after a rebase that brought upstream commits, before the push and in the lease assertion the push makes. An abort ends the publication, and the stop step settles the checkout (commits saved, claim released). A `git push` already running finishes, because it cannot be aborted; verification already stopped on the signal.
563. (D324) Redaction never lengthens a checked text (review finding S-1). Core adds `fitRedacted` (the longest prefix, or suffix, within a maximum that redaction leaves unchanged; the cut moves back past a partly cut `Bearer [reda`), `redactWithin`, `clipRedacted` (patterns only, for text already redacted) and `boundedRedaction` (redaction of a whole document that keeps every string at most its old length, cut back at its end to a fixed point, so every result string is a fixed point). Writers: the project ledger redacts boundedly, then checks, then stores (the conversations' shared ledger base redacts again, a no-op); the coordinator queue and the relay outbox redact boundedly before their schema checks; the hub's `/hub/mesh/projects/*` replies are bounded, and so are the Projects API replies (browser and peer proxies; defense in depth without a test of its own). Producers: a synthesized report is redacted with the device redactor before it is cut (the last 1,200 characters kept), which also keeps a token in a final message out of thread.json and the hub index; thread reasons, the index summary, step-failure texts and `thread-interrupted` messages are cut at fixed points. Readers: a ledger record whose only schema problems are over-long texts (written by earlier versions) reads with those texts cut to their maximum, so one such record no longer fails the thread page, the coordinator's history or a thread read. Unchanged: the conversations' ledger, `SecretRedactor` and the conversation API replies. The report tool still redacts, then checks, so an agent report that redaction lengthens past its maximum is refused to the agent with the field sentence, which the agent can act on (a known limit at that input boundary).
564. (D325) Deliberate deviation from brief phase 7, whose attach command is `claude --resume <sessionId> --model <model>`: Claude transcripts must outlive the CLI's retention on Jevellan's account homes (review finding R-T1; brief section 4 decision 4 makes the native session the only thread transcript). Core `CLAUDE_FLAG_SETTINGS = { cleanupPeriodDays: 36500 }` goes as flag settings (the SDK's `settings` option, the CLI's `--settings`) on every Claude process Jevellan starts on an account home: thread and coordinator turns, conversation stretches (deliberately: they share the homes, and the headless CLI schedules its retention sweep in its idle startup work, so a stretch could delete a thread's journal; nothing else in a stretch changes), model listing, and the attach command, which becomes `claude --resume {id} --model {model} [--effort {effort}] --settings '{"cleanupPeriodDays":36500}'`. No file in the home changes: its settings.json belongs to Rigging, whose delivery and page would treat a new key as a loose setting. Not covered: `claude setup-token` (a login command, not a session); Codex rollout retention was not checked. Evidence: the strings of the Claude CLI 2.1.281 bundled with the Agent SDK 0.3.281 (the sweep's cutoff reads the merged settings, flag settings included), and the help of that CLI and of the installed `claude` 2.1.289, both of which take a JSON string for `--settings`. No live run (no Claude test credential, ENV-CLAUDE).
565. (D326) A resume of a session the runtime no longer has starts a new session (review finding R-T1). The runtime contract's `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` is `The session to resume no longer exists.`; the Claude worker maps the CLI's `No conversation found with session ID: {id}` (its stream-json error result, checked against the bundled CLI 2.1.281) to it, so no session id leaves the worker. A thread turn that failed so on a resume records its turn end, drops its native session (also the id the CLI's error result carried), writes the notice `The thread's earlier session no longer exists, so it continues in a new session.` and runs the same turn again at once without resuming, so it starts with the task block; the turn number is reused (the model never ran), and this happens at most once per turn. A coordinator turn that failed so drops its session (when it is still the turn's own) and counts as a failed turn, so the normal retry 30 seconds later starts fresh with the fresh context. Codex's missing-session error was not checked or mapped. Known gap (second review finding N-3, minor, not fixed): the rerun of a messages turn carries no messages, so if that rerun then rests (an account wait, or the checkout off main), the messages it had already taken from the queue are kept nowhere.
566. (D327) The owner's retry survives a restart (review finding C-1; brief 8.6). After two failed coordinator turns, the coordinator rebuilds its in-memory owner retry from its ledger when it starts: walking back from the newest record, a still-queued owner message seen before any turn record allows the next turn (the owner wrote after the last turn launched), a newest turn end `dropped` allows it (recovery dropped a turn that was allowed to run), and any other turn record does not. Nothing new is stored.
567. (D328) A coordinator status the hub missed is written again (review finding C-2; decisions 393 and 501). A status write that fails with anything but a 4xx refusal (the device no longer holds the assignment) marks the status dirty; the coordinator sweep writes the current state again while it is dirty and no turn runs; a success clears it.
568. (D329) Handover envelopes name the assignment they follow (review finding RL-1; decisions 488, 490 and 503). Coordinator events a device forwards because the coordinator moved away (its queue at the assignment check, at the startup or stalled check, a relayed event that arrives after the move, or a relayed event it holds no state for) get an envelope id derived from the project, source, kind, event id, `handover` and the assignment revision: the same handover repeated keeps its id, an event that comes back after a later move gets a new one, and thread relays keep their ids. The receiving coordinator still deduplicates by event id and honors the forwarded event ids.
569. (D330) A relayed envelope a member cannot read is dropped and named (review finding S-2; decision 494). A member reads the hub's pending envelopes record by record (the page itself is still checked strictly) and returns unreadable records with the id, project, source and kind they still name. The inbox acknowledges each one that has a readable id, remembers it and reports it once, written as an error notice in that project's chat: `{An event for the coordinator | A thread start | A thread command | A relayed message} from device {id} could not be read here and was dropped ({envelope id}). Every device should run the same Jevellan version.` One without a readable id stays on the hub. Errors a handler throws are not reclassified as permanent: a corrupt local file also throws a schema error, and a valid event must not be dropped for it. The inbox's report is tested; its wiring to the chat notice is defense in depth without a test of its own.
570. (D331) Only a thread's own worktree registration is ever forgotten (review finding TH-3; brief 8.3). A missing or prunable thread worktree is removed with its folder (inside Jevellan's home) and `git worktree remove --force {path}` (Git 2.55 removes that one entry for a missing folder), both when a worktree is created and when it is removed; no repository-wide `git worktree prune` remains, so the owner's own worktrees whose folders are away keep their registrations.
571. (D332) Restart cleans up a thread stopped during its setup command (review finding TH-4; decision 480). A restarted thread's worktree and local branch are removed whenever the worktree exists, also when its fields were never stored (a Stop during the setup command); a thread without one (stopped while queued) writes nothing.
572. (D333) Messages left waiting when a publication concludes the thread are named (review finding TH-5). When publication ends a thread `done` (no changes, or published to main) while messages wait in its queue, the thread ledger gets the error notice `The thread concluded before these messages reached it: "{text}"; ...` (each message on one line, the whole within 400 characters) and the queue is cleared. Not changed: the coordinator, whose message was answered `queued`, learns only that the thread concluded (`thread-published`); telling it more would change the relayed event schema.
573. (D334) Coordinator tool ids name the coordinator device, and a question is asked once per call (review findings C-3 and C-4; decisions 255, 415 and 524). The ids the coordinator's tools derive from the turn and the input (a thread start request, a thread message, a thread stop command, an owner question and coordinator mail) include the coordinator's device id, because the turn number is the device's own and starts again on a device the coordinator moves to. `jevellan_ask_user` creates its question under such an id: a stored question of the project under it is returned as it is, and a create that loses a race reads it back.
574. (D335) Recovery's interrupted event is the same at every start (review finding RL-2; decision 365). Its time is the thread ledger's last event time (its id already came from that event), so recovery that runs again after a crash before the thread rested sends a byte-identical event, which an outbox entry still waiting for it takes instead of refusing it with 409, which had stopped Projects on that device for good.
575. (D336) Review findings not fixed (minor; each needs a new durable record). TH-6: a crash between a main rebase conflict and its abort leaves the checkout mid-rebase; the next done report rests the thread with `The checkout is in the middle of a rebase or merge. Finish or abort it, then report done again.`, the coordinator gets it as a blocked report, and Stop aborts the rebase and settles the checkout (an automatic abort would need a marker that tells Jevellan's own rebase from the agent's, and aborting the agent's would discard its conflict resolution). C-5: an answer recorded on the hub can be lost to a crash before its delivery; a client retry with the same request id already delivers again. C-6: a thread report or publication event can be lost to a hard crash between the state write and the send; it needs a pending-event record and a resend at recovery, which decision 574's byte-stable events now allow. The second review's N-3 is recorded with decision 565.
576. (D337) A retried stop settlement changes Git only in the checkout its refusal saw (second review finding N-2; refines decisions 517, 553 and 561). When a stop refuses before its leftover commit because someone else has the checkout (off main at either check, or another agent active in it), it records what it saw: local main's tip (`refs/heads/main`, not HEAD, which off main is the owner's branch) and, only when the checkout was on main (another agent active), a digest of the working tree. Off main the tree is the owner's branch plus whatever they carry back, so a digest would match an owner's untracked draft carried to main at the same tip. The record is `thread-local-v1` `unsettledCheckout.seen` (an optional phase 8 field); the first record is never replaced, a later refusal adds no second notice, and recovery keeps it. The retried stop goes on only if local main is still at that tip and either HEAD is main with a clean tree (nothing to commit) or the working tree digest equals the on-main record (the thread's own leftovers once the other agent is idle). Otherwise the owner has worked there since: the claim is released `unchanged` with no Git change (no commit, no ref, no reset), the marker is cleared, the reason stays as it was, and the notice is `The checkout changed after the Stop, so Jevellan gave it back as it is: nothing was committed, saved or reset, and any of the thread's commits still on main stay there.` instead of `The project checkout was given back.`. Departures from the finding's suggested fix, not from the specification: (1) keeping the claim until a fresh owner Stop was rejected, because an ended thread takes no Stop and no hand release exists, so the claim would keep conversations and main threads off the checkout for good; giving it back as it is loses nothing, since preparation refuses a dirty tree or a local main that is not an ancestor of origin main, so no later thread or conversation publishes the stopped thread's commits silently. (2) The clean or dirty state the finding suggested recording became the clean-tree rule (or the on-main digest), because a dirty tree seen off main already holds the owner's files. Known gap: markers without a record (a release that failed after the reset, an unconfirmed process cleanup, a hub outage before Git was read) keep the first-attempt behavior, so owner edits made on main during such a wait would be committed into the discard ref (kept there, nothing pushed).
577. (D338) The thread index redacts the report summary before it fits it (second review finding N-1; refines decision 563). The index builds its summary with `redactWithin` over the redaction patterns only, a module constant, so the index stays a pure function of thread.json (decisions 327 and 341), instead of `clipRedacted`, which only cuts and turned an unredacted `Fixed the Bearer token parsing ...` into `Fixed the Bearer `. Evidence note: an agent's own report is already redacted with its tool arguments before it is checked (the bridge runs the device redactor over every tool call), and synthesized reports are redacted before they are cut, so a stored report holds no unredacted text in practice; the review's helper-level reproduction holds, but its integration effect did not reproduce (an integration guard is green before and after the change). No second redaction was added in the runner.
578. (D339) Phase 8 copies the decisions it implements as 546 to 578 in the format of decision 392: the documentation and roadmap decisions (546 to 548), PJ-live (549 to 551), the Projects and pre-existing defects fixed before the review (552 to 560), the fixes of the crucial-issues review and of the second review (561 to 577) and this item. Decision 564 is a new deliberate deviation from the brief (the attach command's `--settings`); decisions 561 and 576 depart from a review finding's suggested fix, not from the brief. Decisions 439, 510, 517, 528, 536 and 540 now point to their phase 8 successors. The seven Projects roadmap entries stay `complete-with-blockers`, each `why` gaining one closing phase 8 sentence: the final backend and browser runs are green, but PJ-live is blocked (ENV-JEV, ENV-CODEX, ENV-GITHUB) and the vision checks were not run on this machine (ENV-CLAUDE). The Projects definition of done audit sits beside the section 20 audit, with the dev-madrid vision run and the deployment on their own lines. The texts that still described the attached-thread gaps, the fixture close failures or the screenshots of earlier runs as current (the phase 7 Current state bullet, PJ3.md to PJ7.md) and the deviation list in docs/projects.md get dated, minimal updates. `Last updated` reads 2026-10-06.
579. Deliberate deviation from brief 12.4: the GitHub token help line names the permissions the build actually uses. Squash merge through the REST API needs Contents write, and the combined status Jevellan polls needs Commit statuses read, so a token made from the brief's line (Contents read and Checks read) would get 403 on Merge and leave checks pending. The card reads: "A fine-grained token with Pull requests read and write, Contents read and write, Checks read and Commit statuses read on your repositories is enough." Found while writing PJ-live; covered by the copy unit test and the PJ3 browser journey.
580. The phone-only PJ3 screenshot taken before the Waiting tab is tapped is named `chat-with-waiting-badge` (it was `waiting-tab`): the browser fixture builds the vision judge's expectation from the file name, so the name now says what the screen shows, the chat still selected with the badge on the Waiting tab. The old `waiting-tab` screenshots and vision records are removed.
581. The pull request card and the chat timeline can describe different moments, and that stays: the brief sends `pr-update` only on a transition to conflicts (there is no resolved change), so once conflicts clear the card shows the current `Checks passing` while the timeline keeps the earlier `has conflicts` event.
582. The thread page shows a turn's closing words once: when the last block of the agent turn right before a report card is text that equals the report's summary after trimming (recorded or synthesized), the text is left out and the card carries it; a turn left empty is dropped. Conversation pages are unchanged.
583. The thread page placement line and the Why panel's Jev call lines wrap between their ` · ` parts: each part keeps its separator in an inline block, so a part that fits a line (such as `Worktree on` and its branch) never breaks inside, and a part wider than the whole line still breaks. A part wider than the line now breaks between its words, and separators and the last two parts stay together (decision 586).
584. While the coordinator's Stop icon shows, the chat field keeps 14 px of end padding, and on phones Stop and Send narrow from 40 to 36 px, so on a 390 px phone the placeholder keeps one line with a 22 px gap before Stop instead of 14 px. The end padding is now 26 px, with an ellipsis where the placeholder does not fit (decision 587).
585. Projects text meets WCAG AA in both themes without changing shared tokens: measured from computed colors, light `--ink-3` read 4.12 to 4.44:1 (sidebar, page, cards) and light `--warn` 4.47:1 on the page, so Projects rules use `--pw-ink-3` and `--pw-warn`, a deeper mix toward `--ink` in light themes (5.4 to 5.8:1, at least 4.8:1 on `--bg-3`; amber 5.1:1) that equals the shared tokens in dark themes, which already passed (5.5:1 and up; amber 9.4:1). Disabled controls such as the Main option in project settings keep `--ink-3` (WCAG exempts them), amber chips already pass on their card (4.59:1), and the takeover command's selection takes the accent with its on-accent ink, because the app's selection tint was 1.2:1 against the code background (now 4.6:1 light, 6.1:1 dark). The shared light `--ink-3` has since taken the `--pw-ink-3` value, and `--pw-ink-3` is gone (decision 591).
586. A ` · ` line (the thread page placement line, a Jev call line in Why) never leaves a separator alone and never strands a short last part. On dev-madrid the phone placement line put a lone `·` on its own row after the branch, and the Why panel's Jev line put `6 ms` alone on its last row; both depend on the exact line width: with the same CSS and font, the lone `·` reproduces at a 340 px line and the lone `6 ms` at 336 and 340 px lines (phones of about 360 px), not at this machine's 366 px. Each separator now sits in a no-wrap span with the word before it; a part wider than the line breaks between its words, so `Worktree on` moves down before the branch name breaks, and only a word wider than the line still breaks; the last two parts form one block that moves down whole when it fits a line (`60 tokens · 6 ms`). That block also counts as one row of the placement line's three-line clamp, so the device stays in view on 360 px and narrower phones, where the clamp hid it behind a long branch before. The text reads exactly as the line. The PJ3, PJ4b (placement and Jev lines) and PJ5 journeys check, at the page's width and at every line width from 296 to 406 px, that no row starts with a separator, that the last two parts share a row whenever they fit one together, and that the clamp keeps the last part visible; a unit test covers the word split.
587. While the coordinator works, the chat placeholder keeps one line and ends at least 28 px before Stop (26 px end padding and the 2 px row gap, twice the text's 14 px start inset), in an ellipsis where it does not fit. The brief's placeholder and the 16 px phone text (needed so iOS does not zoom) fill a 390 px phone's field: measured, the full placeholder is 250 px wide and ended 24 px before Stop there, the most it could keep, which the vision review still read as running into Stop. Unfocused, it now reads `Tell the coordinator what you ne…` there and ends 30 px before Stop (39 px if a wider rendering drops one more letter). Chromium draws no ellipsis in a focused field, which the chat field is after sending (the PJ3 coordinator-working screenshots show this state), so there the last 24 px of a placeholder that does not fit fade out instead of stopping mid-letter; a placeholder that fits never reaches the fade. Before, it wrapped to two lines on 375 and 360 px phones and the field grew; now it stays one line. Without Stop, the field is unchanged and the placeholder shows in full. The PJ3 journey checks one line, the clear space and an ellipsis or fade for a placeholder that does not fit, at the layout's width and, on phones, at 320 px; the check fails on the previous rule at 390 px. The ellipsis (styled through `::placeholder`) and the fade are verified in Chromium only; a browser that ignores those `::placeholder` properties keeps the earlier wrapping.
588. Projects heading chips (the coordinator's `Working…`, the thread state, pull request and fallback chips) keep their 11 px text and 7 px side padding on phones, where conversation headings shrink chips to 10 px and 5 px. Measured, the chip was never clipped (its scroll width equals its width on every layout); it looked squeezed at the smaller size, and `Working…` ends in the brief's own ellipsis, which the copy keeps. On phones the chip is now 85 px wide (76 before). The PJ3 journey checks the chip is not clipped and keeps 11 px text and 7 px padding on every layout.
589. The settings tabs keep the page shown in view and show when more tabs are hidden. Below 1024 px they are one strip that scrolls sideways; on a 390 px phone the strip is 915 px wide, and on Git it stayed scrolled to the start, so the selected Git tab was off screen and the cut `Impro…` tab gave no hint. The strip now centers the selected tab whenever the page changes, and each end that hides tabs fades out over 40 px (an end that hides none does not); wide pages keep the vertical list. The fade masks an inner strip, so the bar's border and background stay solid. Wrapping into rows was not taken: nine tabs would take three rows on a phone. The Git settings journey checks that Git is in view and that both ends fade on phones and nothing fades on desktops.
590. On thread pages the prompts are cards in the transcript, not chat bubbles: their left and right edges line up with the tool rows and report cards, 4 px in on both sides (desktop 320 to 1412 px; phone 16 to 374 px), where they started 5 % in (3 % on phones) and ended 4 px further out. Queued messages, which used the same indent, follow them. Conversation pages keep their right-aligned message bubbles, where the indent is the chat layout, not a misalignment. The PJ3 and PJ5 thread journeys check the shared edges.
591. Muted text meets WCAG AA (4.5:1) app-wide in the light theme: the shared light `--ink-3` changes from `#647a90` to `#53677c`, the value Projects text already used (decision 585), a slightly deeper grey of the same hue. Measured from computed colors, before and after: sidebar `#f3f7fa` (shell session meta, filters, `v0.1.0`, `Some native sessions are unavailable`) 4.12 to 5.42:1; page `#fbfcfd` (transcript `completed`, breadcrumbs, intros) 4.32 to 5.68:1; cards `#ffffff` (placeholders, muted notes) 4.44 to 5.84:1; `--card-2` (tool rows) 4.20 to 5.52:1; `--bg-2` (the thread Prompt label) 4.00 to 5.26:1; `--bg-3` (a selected sidebar row's meta) 3.67 to 4.83:1. Dark themes keep `#8098ae`, which already passed (5.51 to 6.35:1). `--pw-ink-3` and the Projects-only overrides of shared headings are removed, since they now equal the shared token; `--pw-warn` stays, because light `--warn` is still 4.47:1 on the page. The Offline badge reads 5.68:1 light and 6.35:1 dark; the fallback chip 4.59:1 light and 9.41:1 dark, on its card. Disabled controls stay exempt and read darker too. The PJ3 journeys check every visible muted text and placeholder against the backgrounds under it on the project page and the thread page, in both themes. The website's own `--ink-3` (site/) is unchanged.

## Latest verification

- Projects after the vision review (2026-10-06, goncalos-macbook-pro, Node 22.22.0 and Homebrew Git 2.55.0): the final verification after the vision review polish (decisions 580 to 591). Build (only the Vite chunk-size warning that predates Projects phase 3), typecheck, lint, the secret scan (`node scripts/secret-scan.mjs --worktree`, which scans the whole Git history and the working tree) and `git diff --check` pass. No source, test or script file changed after the build that these runs tested. The complete backend run passed **1603 tests in 136 files, with 4 skips (the live Jev tests; `JEVELLAN_TEST_JEV_KEY` is not set) and 0 failures, in 1290.9 s**; it was green on its first run, so it was not rerun. Against phase 8 (1602 in 136 files) only `tests/project-work-ui.test.ts` changed, from 35 to 36 tests (the closing words rule of decision 582; decision 586 extends an existing case); no file lost a test or changed its skip count, `tests/conversation-service.test.ts` passed 94 of 94 and every Projects journey passed by name again. Browser: one complete matrix run with four workers, **208 of 208 in 31.7 minutes on the first run, with no retry, flake or skip**: desktop and phone, light and dark 37 of 37 each, each improver layout 3 of 3, each Projects layout 11 of 11 (PJ3's seven journeys, PJ4b, PJ5's two and PJ7) and the installation journeys 4 of 4, while the one-minute load ranged from 4.5 to 23.6 (median 10.1). It is the first complete matrix with the shared changes of decisions 589 (the settings tab strip) and 591 (the light `--ink-3`) in every layout: the Git settings journey passed in all four, with the selected tab in view, both fades on phones and none on desktops, and the skeleton, improver and installation journeys passed 164 of 164; the new checks of decisions 582 to 588, 590 and 591 (the closing words shown once, whole ` · ` parts, the placeholder clear of Stop, the heading chip, aligned prompt cards and readable muted text) passed in the Projects journeys in every Projects layout. PJ7 took 24.8, 24.8, 25.0 and 26.0 s (desktop-light, phone-light, desktop-dark, phone-dark). No browser temporary root was left, and no fixture server, test runner or browser process stayed running. Load noise: none; nothing failed, so nothing needed a rerun or a comparison with the previous commit. Vision labels: not run (`JEVELLAN_TEST_CLAUDE_TOKEN` is not set) in all 356 vision records, all written by this run; its 104 Projects records replace the committed dev-madrid judgments of the screens before decisions 586 to 591, so the Projects vision run is due again (see the vision line in the Projects definition of done audit). All 104 Projects screenshots, and 348 of the 469 screenshots in the repository, come from this run.
